## Addendum — Dispatch fees: RUGGS 10% per board load (edit, 2026-10-26)
- RULES AS LOCKED (owner, 2026-10-26): carriers who claim loads off the Fleet Load Board owe RUGGS LLC a dispatch fee of 10% PER LOAD, figured off the load's final freight amount. Settled chain: Peacock/customers pay carriers direct; the carrier pays RUGGS. Fee earned at sealed delivery; 7-day collection window with the carrier OFF the board until paid; day 7 unpaid → WARNING on the carrier's record; day 10 unpaid → board permission REVOKED until the debt is settled in full; payment restores standing, warning/late history remains visible. Rails: ACH, debit card, crypto. RECORDS + GATE ONLY — no live card capture, no money movement inside the app.
- MIGRATION 0052: `dispatch_fee_invoices` (one row per load — `load_id` UNIQUE; board_load_id, packet_id, company_id, carrier_username = the board row's claimedBy, freight_amount, fee_amount, paid_amount, delivered_at = the POD seal time, paid_at = the moment the invoice reached paid-in-full, probe flag for staging probe loads) and `dispatch_fee_payments` (invoice_id, load_id, rail ach|debit|crypto, reference = ACH trace / card receipt ref / crypto tx hash, amount, paid_at, recorded_by, verification, note). The full wipe clears both tables.
- PURE RULES MODULE `server/src/dispatchFee.ts` (+ unit tests `dispatchFee.test.ts`): `freightAmountFor` (rate + additional charges — the packet's invoice amount), `dispatchFeeFor` (10%, cents-rounded), `planFeeSync` (idempotency: no invoice → create; same amount → none; corrected amount → update_amount on the same invoice), `ladderFor` (status window|warning|revoked|paid + daysOutstanding + balanceDue + everWarned/everRevoked computed from delivered_at and paid_at, so late history survives settlement), `gateFor` (blocked while ANY balance is due — partial payments never clear it; revoked when any blocking invoice is at day 10), `feeEligibility` (the Granted-lane guard).
- GENERATION: `ensureDispatchFeeInvoice` runs inside `assemblePaymentPacket` (every assembly path — seal, re-seal refresh, on-demand first view), so the fee invoice spawns from the Payment Packet exactly once. A corrected packet amount updates freight/fee on the SAME invoice and seals `dispatch_fee_amount_corrected`. Chain events: `dispatch_fee_invoiced`, `dispatch_fee_amount_corrected`, `dispatch_fee_payment_recorded`, `dispatch_fee_paid`, `dispatch_fee_error` (a fee failure never fails the seal or the packet).
- GRANTED-LANE DOUBLE-CHARGE GUARD: eligibility is board-claim provenance ONLY — a load generates a fee iff a `board_loads` row exists for it AND that row is taken (claim or assign) by a carrier. Loads under the RUGGS→Granted agent agreement are not board loads (no taken board row) and can never generate an invoice; neither can manual loads or solo broker-approval loads. One load, one 10%, enforced by provenance + the UNIQUE constraint.
- GATE: `claimBoardLoad` checks the claimer's standing (per company) before the atomic first-claim-wins flip and refuses with the gate message naming the blocking load, amount, days, and rails; `assignBoardLoad` checks the target driver the same way. Day-10 revocation refuses at the permission level (distinct wording + revoked flag). `getLoadBoard` returns `feeStanding` (blocked/revoked/warned/blocking load/amount/message) and the board UI renders the block banner from it; claim buttons stay visible so the refusal reason is one tap away and always server-enforced.
- LADDER SEMANTICS: computed at read time from delivered_at + payments, never stored — days 0–7 window (gate closed until paid), day 7 (≥7×24h since seal) warning, day 10 revoked. everWarned/everRevoked derive from how long the fee sat unpaid (paid_at − delivered_at for settled invoices), so the warning/late mark persists after payment while standing is restored.
- RAILS / CONFIG: constants in `dispatchFee.ts`. `DISPATCH_FEE_CARD_LINK = null` (debit renders "being set up" until RUGGS connects its card processor — no fake checkout). `DISPATCH_FEE_CRYPTO_ADDRESS = 0x3a6fEBE311F98629C9A98452dB0968C315930447` — the STONEMILES house lane, owner decision 2026-10-26 — rendered on every invoice with the fee amount and Robinhood Chain (4663). Crypto payments are labelled in their ledger note as RUGGS dispatch fees to the house lane so they stay distinguishable from subscription team-share receipts in the shared wallet.
- PAYMENT RECORDING: `recordDispatchFeePayment {loadId, rail, reference, paidAt?, amount?, chain?}` — Owner/Dispatch seat of the invoice's company only (the RUGGS desk). Amount defaults to the full balance due; overpayment is refused with the exact balance; a partial records and does NOT clear the gate; paid-in-full stamps paid_at and seals `dispatch_fee_paid`. Crypto verification: when the reference is a tx hash, the server asks the chain RPC (Robinhood 4663 → rpc.mainnet.chain.robinhood.com, Base 8453 → mainnet.base.org) for the transaction; found + recipient = the house lane → `onchain_verified`; found but different recipient → `onchain_recipient_mismatch`; not found → `onchain_not_found`; RPC unreachable / not a hash / no address configured → `admin_confirmed` with the reason written into the payment's note and the sealed event. Verification NEVER blocks recording — the desk's record is the book; the verification outcome rides on it.
- READS: `getMyDispatchFees` (the carrier's own invoices + standing — board panel "Dispatch fees" section with rails and, for desk seats, the record-payment form), `getDispatchFeeForLoad` (carrier or desk seats of that company only), `getDispatchFeeLedger` (Owner Console → RUGGS Ledger section, Owner/Dispatch only: every invoice — load ref, carrier, lane, freight, fee, paid, balance, ladder status, days outstanding, warning/revocation history, payments with rail + reference + verification — plus invoiced/collected/outstanding totals and ladder counts; the same record-payment form rides each unpaid invoice).
- LIMITS, PLAINLY: no live card capture until the owner's Stripe is connected (by design — debit shows "being set up"); ACH is pay-off-app + desk-recorded (no bank integration); crypto verification is best-effort against public RPCs and degrades to admin-confirmed, never silently "verified". In this single-device staging build the carrier and the desk are company members on the same data model; per-device carrier accounts arrive with the accounts release.

## Addendum — Fiat conversion 75/25 on the card path (edit, 2026-10-26, x's amendment)
- FIAT (card) subscription payments no longer convert 100% to $STONER: 75% converts and the converted amount splits 50/25/10/15 as before; the remaining 25% never converts — it stays USD cash to the business debit/bank account as company operating cash.
- $STONER-native payments unchanged (no conversion, no cash leg — split 50/25/10/15 in full). ALT-TOKEN path unchanged (converts to $STONER; its 25% rewards slice goes to the Reward Wallet).
- Recorded as first-class params in the canonical SUBSCRIPTION_SPLIT config (server/src/actions.ts): fiatConversion { convertPct: 75, cashLegPct: 25, cashLeg: "USD operating cash — business debit/bank account (never converted)" }; the coin-flow ledger-rules copy updated to match. RECORD ONLY — billing and all split execution stay gated OFF; no money movement is activated by this recording.

## Addendum — Verify & Audit layer (edit, 2026-10-26, x's direction: "focus on verification and auditing")

- ONE HUB, EXISTING SPINE: the Verify & Audit hub consolidates the proof spine that already existed — sealed hash-chained records, POD seals with grades, the payment-packet + invoice/receivable registries (0049/0051), ELD connections/snapshots + IFTA reconciliation, the Settlement Checker, the DOT inspection packet, and the record access log — into explicit verification and audit surfaces. Nothing was rebuilt and no schema migration was needed: the layer is new read/verify actions over the same tables and chain. Reachable from More → Verify & Audit (full-screen sheet) and from the Owner Console's new Verify & Audit section (owners/dispatch). The hub's first line states the scope plainly: "STONEMILES verifies and audits records; it is not an FMCSA ELD. Planning aid — your official record is your ELD."
- PURE LOGIC MODULE `server/src/verifyAudit.ts` (+ unit tests `verifyAudit.test.ts`, 11 tests): `verifyChainEntries` (re-walks entries in chain order recomputing sha256(prevHash|event|payload) from GENESIS exactly as appendChain writes; first failure wins — hash_mismatch or prev_mismatch — and every later entry is attributed failed as after_break, so per-record pass/fail never overclaims), `categoryForChainEntry` (pod_seal* → POD seals, payment_packet*/invoice + packet duplicate + dispatch_fee* → payment packets, then refKind trip/load/other), `classifyProofReference` (PKT-<id>-<fp8> → packet_ref, 64-hex → fingerprint, anything else unknown — an unknown format can never become a match), `summarizeSettlementChecks` (totals over SAVED checker flags only), `clockDifferences` (app vs ELD clocks, difference = app − ELD, never resolved).
- CHAIN VERIFICATION (`runChainVerification`): full-chain walk via `computeChainVerification` (shared with the packet export) returning a dated result — entries checked, records checked, intact or first broken link (id + reason), chain head, per-category totals (trips / loads / POD seals / payment packets / other), and per-record detail (entries, failed entries, pass/fail, first/last sealed, record head hash; capped at 500 records, newest first). The run itself seals a `chain_verification` chain event AFTER the walk, so the report covers the chain as it stood and the audit is itself auditable. An empty chain returns intact with 0 checked and the hub shows the honest empty state ("No sealed records yet — nothing to verify"). Ungated, exactly like the existing `verifyChain` it extends (the Security Center re-check stays as-is).
- PROOF LOOKUP (`lookupProof`): one reference in — a packet ref (PKT-…) or a 64-hex fingerprint (chain entry hash, packet fingerprint, invoice fingerprint, or receivable key) — and exactly three outcomes out. VERIFIED requires the record's anchor to check out: for a packet, its latest `payment_packet_created/refreshed` chain entry must carry the same fingerprint the packet row holds AND the chain must verify up to that entry (`verifyChainPrefix`); evidence lines report the anchor entry, the invoice-registry state (including a ⚠ when the invoice fingerprint was FIRST registered by a different load — the double-financing flag), and the receivable-key registry state. MISMATCH covers: packet with no anchoring chain entry, anchor fingerprint ≠ stored fingerprint, chain broken at/before the anchor, and registry rows whose packet is missing. NOT FOUND covers unknown formats (with the accepted formats spelled out) and well-formed references with no matching record. A match is never invented.
- ELD RECONCILIATION AUDIT: two halves, both presented as one audit view. HOS half = new `getEldHosAudit`: the app's planning clocks (effective duty timeline + `hosClocksFor`, corrections applied) beside `mirroredEldHos` (the connected ELD's clocks snapshot), per-clock differences with evidence lines; premium-gated via the existing `eldManageGate` (Diamond/Owner), same as ELD connections. Miles/IFTA half = the EXISTING `getEldIftaReconciliation` called unchanged for the chosen quarter and rendered as the audit's state-by-state table (app vs ELD vs difference + totals). Differences are shown, never overwritten or auto-resolved. Honest states verbatim: no connection → "No ELD connected — audit runs on app-sealed records only."; connected but no snapshot yet → the audit says so and runs on app-sealed records only.
- SETTLEMENT AUDIT SUMMARY (`getSettlementAuditSummary`): totals over the SAVED Settlement Checker reports (`settlements.checkJson` — the checker's own stored flags): imported, checked, discrepancies (total flags), and total shortfall. Shortfall counts only the flag kinds that mean money the sealed records say is missing (underpaid / missing_load / missing_detention / detention_short) at positive amounts; an overpaid line and a net_mismatch (the document disagreeing with itself) count as discrepancies, NOT shortfall — the definition lives in `verifyAudit.ts` and in the hub copy's provenance note. The checker's logic is never re-run or duplicated — this is its scoreboard. Diamond-gated exactly like the checker (`hasDiamondAccess`); off-plan returns the flag, zeros, and the checker's own Diamond message.
- AUDIT TRAIL (`getAuditTrail`): `record_access_log` rows merged with the chain's `portal_access` events as one who/what/when trail, deduplicated on viewer|subject|what|minute (company-doc views are written to both stores and must not double-list). Scoping matches the existing readers: a driver sees views of their own records and their own views; an Owner/Dispatch seat sees the fleet trail for their company members (member usernames + "company" subject). The scope line states the staging truth: "as recorded on this device."
- AUDIT PACKET EXPORT (`exportAuditPacket {loadId?}`): one text packet — header + honest scope lines, the chain verification report (same `computeChainVerification`), for a load: its lane/status/rate, rate-con + BOL fingerprints, POD seal + grade (`podStatusByLoad`), the packet row with invoice + registry states (⚠ first-registered-by-a-different-load lines included), and the load's sealed chain entries; then the correction chain (latest 20 `duty_correction` events rendered original → corrected with editor, role, and reason — originals stay sealed, stated on the line), the settlement audit summary (or its Diamond-gate line), and the latest 50 access-log entries. The client appends `getInspectionPacket`'s `packetText` under the packet's DOT heading, so the DOT packet content ships inside the same export through the standard ExportBar (download/copy/print/send, step-up PIN as always). Export is read-only: it never assembles a packet and seals nothing.
- HARD CONSTRAINTS HELD: no money movement of any kind; no changes to subscription split, dispatch-fee, Stripe, or wallet code (the only dispatch-fee touchpoint is read-only: its chain events classify into the payment-packets audit category); pricing constants unchanged; no migration (0052 remains latest); no fake/demo data — production starts honest-empty and every surface says so when it is (verified live at build time: production chain intact with 268 sealed entries, settlements list empty).
- VERIFICATION: full suite 84/84 (73 pre-existing + 11 new), client + server typecheck clean. New actions could not be live-probed in this pass: the published artifact bundle still serves the previous build (its action list predates these actions) until the normal rebuild/redeploy flow runs — the underlying spine was live-probed instead (verifyChain: intact, 268 entries). ELD HOS audit on a connected ELD remains unexercised for the same staged reason as the rest of the ELD layer: production eld_snapshots are empty until the go-live sync worker exists, and the audit says exactly that rather than showing numbers.

## Addendum — Rate-con / BOL cross-verification (edit, 2026-10-26, x's direction: top item on the locked product priorities)

- WHAT IT IS: each load's rate confirmation and its BOL are compared field by field, and the disagreements become record — open exceptions on the load — instead of staying invisible until a claim or a short-pay. Pure engine `server/src/docVerify.ts` (+ `docVerify.test.ts`, 18 tests); schema migration 0053 (three tables: `load_doc_extractions` — per-side extraction snapshots; `load_doc_verifications` — append-only verification runs; `load_doc_exceptions` — the exception ledger). Client: a "Document verification" card on the load detail (both the Loads tab and the tracker load-details views) and a roll-up section in the Verify & Audit hub.
- FIELDS COMPARED (18): broker/shipper identity · pickup address · delivery address · pickup window · delivery window · commodity/description · weight (lb) · pieces/pallet count · seal number · rate · fuel surcharge · detention · lumper · other charges · total charges · load # · PO # · PRO #. Both documents' values are always carried on the report for display.
- FIELD OUTCOMES: `match` / `mismatch` (both sides have the field and it differs) / `missing_ratecon` / `missing_bol` (one side never printed or never yielded the field) / `not_readable` (a side's document was attached but its extraction read failed or the document was never read at all — this also covers a document attached as a PDF or on a plan without the document read: present, never read). "Verified" is a statement about the fields the pair actually carries: a BOL that prints no money lines can never produce a rates verdict, and the run reports those fields missing_bol instead of implying they were checked and found fine.
- PAIR VERDICTS: `verified` = both sides present AND read AND every compared field matches. `differences_found` = at least one mismatch (takes precedence even when other fields are missing). `incomplete` = no mismatch, but something is missing/not-readable, or only one document is present.
- NORMALIZATION (exact, documented — no fuzzy hiding): money is parsed to integer cents with an exact decimal-string parser (BigInt, half-up away from zero beyond 2 decimals) and compared cent-exact — a 1-cent difference is a mismatch. Weight and pieces compare as exact numbers (weight rounded to whole lb). Addresses normalize: uppercase, ZIP pulled out as its own component (different ZIP = mismatch even when street text matches), punctuation stripped, common street suffixes (ST/STREET…) and directionals (N/NORTH…) expanded, state abbreviations mapped to full names; city/state lines from the load use the same normalization. Windows parse to date + minutes-since-midnight when a date/time is recognizable (12h/24h, "Nov 12, 2026 9:00 AM" and ISO both read); unparseable windows fall back to normalized-text equality. Identity drops trailing legal suffixes (INC/LLC/LTD…) and SUNDRY trailing punctuation before comparing.
- WHERE THE TWO SIDES COME FROM (honest sourcing, never guessed): extractions are snapshotted when the document's bytes are in hand, because server-side blob re-reads are unreliable in this runtime. Rate-con side: at load save, when a new document is attached — Gold+ image documents get the extended server-side vision read (the full 18-field set, `source: document`); otherwise the fields the driver confirmed at save (`source: confirmed`). BOL side: at pickup-photo seal, the extended read runs ungated on the just-received bytes (`source: document`); plus the pickup flow's driver-confirmed BOL read is recorded by `recordBolExtraction` (`source: document`, tied to the sealed photo's fingerprint). A run uses the latest snapshot matching the attached document's fingerprint. Pre-feature loads fall back to load-record fields (`source: load_record`, labelled as such in the report notes) — with the stated caveat that the pickup flow's BOL-confirm merges BOL values into the load record, so a load-record-vs-load-record comparison can agree with itself; the report says when this is the case. PDFs are fingerprinted at attach but never machine-read: their side is not_readable unless confirmed fields exist.
- ACTIONS: `runLoadDocVerification` (Gold+ gate — the same document-reading tier as loads by photo; refuses when neither document is attached), `getLoadDocVerification` (latest report + run history + exception ledger), `acknowledgeDocException` (reason required), `getDocVerificationSummary` (hub roll-up: checked / verified / differences open / incomplete / never checked + per-load rows), `recordBolExtraction` (pickup flow). Auth reuses existing scoping: board loads — the claiming carrier or an Owner/Dispatch seat of the board's company; non-board loads — the local driver of record. Every run seals `doc_verification_run` (verdict + outcomes hash over the canonical field-outcome JSON); exception opens/closes seal `doc_exception_opened` / `doc_exception_closed` onto the load's chain.
- EXCEPTIONS + SEAL GRADE (extended, not paralleled): every mismatch opens (or refreshes) an exception row on the load. `sealGrade.ts`'s `noOpenExceptions` criterion — the one the release-grade POD seal reads — now fails on an open seal-number mismatch OR any open cross-verification exception, and the grade detail names the open fields. Both grade paths (seal time and save-trip re-evaluation) feed it the open-exception list, and a verification run or an acknowledgment re-evaluates the grade of an already-sealed load on the record. An exception closes ONLY by (a) a re-run whose field now matches (`closeKind: rerun_match`) — a re-run where the field merely went missing does NOT close it — or (b) an explicit acknowledgment with who + role + reason sealed (`closeKind: acknowledged`). Acknowledgment never edits a report: the mismatch stays in the run history and the closed-exception ledger keeps both documents' values.
- HONEST LIMITS: extraction quality is the ceiling. Before this build, extraction never produced reference numbers, pieces, street addresses, or itemized charges at all — only the new extended read captures them, and only for documents read from now on; older snapshots and load-record fallbacks simply don't carry those fields, so those comparisons report missing rather than pretending coverage. BOLs in the real world rarely print money lines, so rate/charges pairs commonly land at `incomplete` rather than `verified` — by design, not a defect to paper over. The extended read is a vision model: a misread digit on either document shows up as a mismatch the driver can see and acknowledge; nothing is auto-corrected. Production has exactly one load with no documents, so production starts honest-empty.
- HARD CONSTRAINTS HELD: no money movement; no changes to subscription split, dispatch-fee, Stripe, wallet, or pricing code or constants (the Gold+ gate reuses the existing document-tier rule — no new pricing invented); no fake/demo data; honest labels untouched (the hub keeps its "not an FMCSA ELD" scope line).
- VERIFICATION: full suite 102/102 (84 pre-existing + 18 new docVerify tests covering all-match→verified, rate mismatch→differences_found + exception opens, seal mismatch→exception→release-grade blocked through the real `evaluateSealGrade`, missing BOL→incomplete, unreadable BOL→not_readable, corrected re-run closes / missing-field re-run does not, acknowledgment closes while the stored report still shows the mismatch, address normalization incl. ZIP mismatch, cent-exact money, window parsing). Client + server typecheck clean. Not live-probed: the published artifact bundle serves the previous build until the normal rebuild/redeploy flow runs — same staged state as the Verify & Audit layer.

## Addendum — Earning Pool governors: conversions first + thinner welcome vesting (edit, 2026-10-26, x's order: "make sure we always stay in profit — driver actually get paid and the whole mechanic works")

- WHY: a full audit + 12-month Earning Pool simulation (2026-10-26) found the mechanic solvent by construction — payouts can never exceed the pool ledger — but at mid-range $STONER prices, earned points conversions starved behind the front-loaded welcome-credit queue and paid 0%: the pool's early balance went to bonus credits first, so drivers who had actually ground out points queued indefinitely. Simulation of the base case at a $0.005 token price: conversions paid 0% under the old order; with conversions-first + thinner vesting they pay 99.6% with ~0.2-month average wait in the same base case.
- CHANGE 1 — QUEUE PRIORITY (server/src/actions.ts): new helper `processEarningPoolQueues(ctx)` drains `processPointsConversionQueue(ctx)` FIRST, then `processWelcomeQueue(ctx)`. It replaces the standalone `processWelcomeQueue(ctx)` call at `fundEarningPool`, at the yearly-credit grant path (`maybeAwardYearlyCredit`), and at the subscription welcome-grant path (`maybeAwardWelcomeCredit`, reached from `recordSubscriptionEvent`'s reward flow) — so whenever the pool is funded or a credit is granted, queued points conversions drain before welcome/yearly credits. Stated plainly in the code comments: earned points conversions are paid before bonus credits when the pool can't cover both. Conversion-queue-only call sites and the Reward Wallet's `processReferralQueue` are untouched.
- CHANGE 2 — VESTING SCHEDULE (`WELCOME_VEST_MONTHLY_TRANCHES`): diamond 3 → 6, owner 6 → 12. The 25% liquid head at first subscription stays (`splitVestedCredit` already implements head + equal monthly tranches generically): Diamond = 2,500 head + 1,250/month × 6; Fleet/Owner = 25,000 head + 6,250/month × 12. Gold's 1,000 stays fully liquid (below the unchanged 10,000 vest threshold). (The tranches counts ×6/×12 stand under Option C; the token amounts + threshold were re-denominated in USD by the Option-C addendum below.) The 2026-10-22 vesting addendum above, the client plan/FAQ copy, and the schema comment were updated to the new schedule.
- NOT CHANGED BY THE GOVERNOR EDIT (locked at that point; the conversion rate, welcome credit amounts, and WELCOME_VEST_THRESHOLD named here were SUPERSEDED later the same day by the Option-C addendum below — subscription prices, the 50/25/10/15 SUBSCRIPTION_SPLIT, CONV_MIN_BALANCE, CONV_MONTHLY_CAP_POINTS, the daily points table, DAILY_POINTS_CAP, giveaway vesting (25% at payout + 25%/month × 3, time-only), and every wallet address remain unchanged): subscription prices, the 50/25/10/15 SUBSCRIPTION_SPLIT, the conversion rate (CONV_STONER_PER_BLOCK = 100 per 1,000 pts), CONV_MIN_BALANCE, CONV_MONTHLY_CAP_POINTS, the daily points table, DAILY_POINTS_CAP, welcome credit amounts (1,000 / 10,000 / 100,000), WELCOME_VEST_THRESHOLD (10,000), giveaway vesting, and every wallet address.

## Addendum — OPTION C: dollar-anchored rewards (edit, 2026-10-26, x approved: "Sounds great")

- DECISION: points conversion and the welcome/yearly credits are no longer promised as fixed token counts. Every anchored reward carries a fixed USD value and pays in $STONER at the live market price when the payout actually runs, from the Earning Pool only. This supersedes the token-fixed terms in every earlier entry above (conversion rate, welcome credit token amounts, yearly credit token amount, the 10,000-token vesting threshold) wherever they conflict; the earlier entries are kept as dated history.
- ANCHOR: **$0.20 USD per 1,000 SM Points** (`ANCHOR_USD_PER_BLOCK` in `server/src/rewardsAnchor.ts`) — **SUPERSEDED by the REWARDS REWORK addendum (2026-10-26) at the end of this file: the anchor is now $0.25 per 1,000 SM Points; every other term in this addendum stands.** Conversion rules unchanged otherwise: 1,000-point blocks, 10,000-point minimum, 100,000-point monthly cap, queue-if-dry, earned conversions drain before bonus credits (`processEarningPoolQueues` order preserved). The conversion gate (`points_conversion_live`) stays OFF until Nov 20 + x's go-live word — this edit builds the mechanic, it does not activate it.
- DERIVATION: blended sub = 0.6×$9.99 + 0.3×$19.99 + 0.1×$49.99 = $16.994/mo. Rewards slice = 25% of the 75% converted = $3.186375/sub-month = $38.2365/sub-year. Expected credit liability per new sub = 0.6×$1 + 0.3×$5 + 0.1×$25 + 0.3×$1 yearly = $4.90; over the launch year with 5%/mo churn-replacement (1.55 cohort-equivalents) = $7.595 per initial sub. Earned conversion demand at the anchor, 30% breakage: earner ratio 1.0 → $15.475/yr; ratio 1.5 → $22.317/yr. TOTALS vs the $38.2365 slice: ratio 1.0 = $23.07 (60.3% of slice, 39.7% headroom); ratio 1.5 = $29.91 (78.2%, 21.8% headroom). Zero-breakage stress: conversions alone stay ≤ the slice at ratios 1.0 ($22.11) and 1.5 ($31.96); ratio 2.0 zero-breakage is the only corner where conversions queue (90.0% paid in year one in the coordinator's sim). Sim verdicts (coordinator-verified): base case (30% breakage) — conversions paid 100% at every price path ($0.001/$0.002/$0.005/$0.01/$0.05/rising) at ratios 0.5/1/1.5 with 0.00 mo average wait; ratio 2.0 also 100% (0.09 mo wait); scales 100 and 5,000 identical at ratio 1. Stress (0% breakage) — 100% at ratios 1 and 1.5 at every price; ratio 2 pays 90.0%, remainder queued, pool never negative. Year-one credit claims paid 100% in the base case at all ratios/prices.
- CREDITS (USD, paid in $STONER at the payout price): Gold welcome **$1.00** (liquid) · Diamond welcome **$5.00** (25% head + remainder in 6 monthly tranches) · Fleet/Owner welcome **$25.00** (25% head + remainder in 12 monthly tranches) · Yearly subscriber credit **$1.00** (annual subs only, still locked until first sealed trip). Vesting is by plan shape only — the old 10,000-token threshold no longer applies to anchored credits. Split math is exact in integer cents (`splitVestedCreditUsd`): 25% head rounded, last tranche absorbs the remainder, tranches always sum exactly to the credit.
- PRICE + FAIL-CLOSED RULE: each payout run (`processPointsConversionQueue`, `processWelcomeQueue`, `processWelcomeVesting`) reads the live $STONER price ONCE from a single server-side provider (`liveStonerPriceUsd` in `server/src/actions.ts`; returns null pre-launch — the CHIP pool read wires in at launch). Null price → the run pays NOTHING and every row stays queued/pending; no stored, request-time, or guessed price is ever used. With a price: tokens = USD ÷ price, rounded DOWN to 6 decimals (a payout never exceeds its USD anchor); the pool must cover the token amount or the row stays queued. Points leave the driver's ledger only at actual payment. Security step-up in `requestPointsConversion` is gated on a live price existing (velocity rules still apply without one; no payout can occur without a price).
- SCHEMA (migration `0054_option_c_dollar_anchored_rewards.sql`): `points_conversions` gains `anchor_usd` (total USD value of the request, stored at request time) + `price_usd` (nullable, the price actually used at payout); `amount_stoner` becomes the FINAL token amount actually paid (0 while queued). `welcome_credits` + `welcome_vesting` gain `amount_usd` (the USD entitlement; per-tranche for vesting rows); their `amount_stoner` likewise becomes the final paid token amount. `admin_settings` gains `welcome_credit_gold_usd` (default 1), `welcome_credit_diamond_usd` (5), `welcome_credit_owner_usd` (25), `yearly_credit_usd` (1); the old `*_stoner` credit columns are RETIRED for new grants (kept for legacy rows). LEGACY ROWS: any row with `amount_usd` NULL predates Option C and pays its stored `amount_stoner` under the old terms — unchanged behavior. Reversals/clawbacks reverse paid rows at their FINAL `amount_stoner`; unpaid anchored rows simply cancel their USD entitlement.
- PUBLIC COPY (client): "each 1,000 SM Points carries $0.20 of value, paid in $STONER at the market price when the conversion pays"; credits shown as dollar values ("$5.00 of value, paid in $STONER at the market price when each part pays out"). No income promises beyond the anchor value; no price predictions. Referral $STONER amounts (Reward Wallet leg) and the subscriber giveaway stay token-denominated and are UNCHANGED, as are subscription prices, the 50/25/10/15 `SUBSCRIPTION_SPLIT`, the fiat 75/25 legs, `DAILY_TASKS` + the 500 daily cap, and swap fees.
- VERIFICATION: `server/src/rewardsAnchor.test.ts` adds 17 tests (block math, anchor math across prices, price-missing/zero/negative/non-finite fails closed, split sums exactly incl. Diamond $5 → $1.25 head and Fleet $25 → $6.25 head, queue-order constant). Full suite 119/119 (102 prior + 17). Client + server typecheck clean. Grep confirms no current-terms "1,000 points = 100 $STONER" / `CONV_STONER_PER_BLOCK` / `stonerPerBlock` remain in `server/src`, `client/src`, or this file outside clearly-marked superseded history.

## Addendum — USDG on-chain backing for the rewards slice (2026-10-26, x approved the concept: "Yes. Spec it." — **DECIDED 2026-10-26: "Add d1-d4 and Usdg plan" — D1–D4 ALL APPROVED/LOCKED; implementation phased per spec §8, nothing moves before live billing, fallback posture retained**)

- FULL SPEC: `~/workspace/goals/trucker-gps-app/files/usdg-pool-backing-spec-2026-10-26.md`. This addendum is the summary; the spec governs.
- CHANGE PROPOSED (one asset's parking spot, nothing else): the rewards slice (25% of the 75% fiat-converted amount = 18.75% of gross) currently buys $STONER immediately and waits in the Earning Pool (`0x4550E380182c8CdE5Fbc01004eb7DA42CF102213`) as tokens. Under the spec it instead parks as **USDG** (Paxos Global Dollar, native to Robinhood Chain, `0x5fc5360D0400a0Fd4f2af552ADD042D716F1d168`) in the SAME Earning Pool wallet, and the $STONER buy happens at the payout moment. WHY: Option C made every reward a fixed USD promise ($0.20/1,000 pts; credits $1/$5/$25/$1); holding the slice in USDG makes the backing for every point in circulation a public on-chain dollar balance (Charter Stone #5). No seed funding — the pool self-funds from sub revenue flow.
- FLOW: fiat sub → 25% cash leg stays in the RUGGS LLC bank account (unchanged) → 75% converts on-chain → 50/25/10/15 split (unchanged) → team/burn/locker proceed as today → rewards slice converts to USDG → USDG to the Earning Pool. PAYOUT: the run reads the live price ONCE via the same single server-side provider as Option C (`liveStonerPriceUsd`; null → pays nothing, fail closed); token amount = USD ÷ price, rounded down to 6 dp; the pool spends the matching USDG to buy exactly that $STONER amount at market and pays the driver. Slippage: the driver always receives the full anchor amount or the payout re-queues — the pool absorbs small fill variance (buffer, D3) and never haircuts a payout. Queue order unchanged: earned conversions first, then welcome/yearly credits. Credits draw on the same USDG backing.
- UNAFFECTED: $STONER-native subscriptions (split in full in token; their rewards slice arrives as $STONER, never touches USDG) and the alt-token path / Reward Wallet (referrals, giveaway).
- REJECTED, on the record: issuing our own stablecoin (SMUSD/SVUSD) — issuer reserve/redemption/attestation/licensing burden on Stonervision forever, when USDG is already native and Paxos-regulated. SVUSD shelved by x 2026-10-25; do not re-litigate unless x reopens it.
- COVERAGE: liabilities (USD) = (convertible points outstanding ÷ 1,000 × $0.20) + unpaid credit dollars; backing = pool USDG balance; coverage ratio = backing ÷ liabilities. Sim (Option C derivation, `rewards-profit-sim-2026-10-26.md`): anchored demand = 60.3% of the slice at earner ratio 1.0, 78.2% at 1.5 → steady-state coverage above 1.00 by construction; payouts still cannot exceed the ledger. Internal admin display of balance + liabilities + ratio from day one.
- RISKS + RULES: (1) USDG/Paxos issuer risk (freeze/redemption) — backing is never lent/staked; exposure capped at the rewards slice. (2) DEPEG RULE (LOCKED with the plan, x 2026-10-26): USDG is valued at its real market price, never assumed $1; payouts continue only while true coverage ≥ 1.00, otherwise queue; severe depeg (>5%) pauses payouts pending x's word. (3) Chain/venue failure → payouts queue, nothing forced. (4) Price-read discipline unchanged from Option C.
- FALLBACK IS THE DEFAULT: until the on-chain leg is verified live (spec §8 checklist: canonical Paxos USDG address confirmed, on-ramp test lands in the pool wallet, a USDG→$STONER test buy fills within tolerance, coverage math hand-checked over one cycle, per-payout ledger records USD entitlement / price used / tokens paid / USDG spent / backing path, depeg rule implemented), the system runs exactly as today's design on bank dollars and NO conversion depends on USDG. If the on-ramp is unavailable at billing start, launch is unaffected.
- DECISIONS — ALL APPROVED/LOCKED BY X 2026-10-26 ("Add d1-d4 and Usdg plan"; spec §7 governs): D1 public backing display — YES, balance + coverage ratio on a delayed refresh (the wallet is public on-chain anyway; hiding wastes the proof, a live tick invites FUD-chasing). D2 payout-buy execution at launch — MANUAL BATCH v1 (human eyes on each buy while volume is small; automate after fills prove boring); payout runs execute only as an explicit admin-triggered batch at launch configuration. D3 buffer — hold next 30 days of expected payouts (trailing payout run-rate) as the minimum USDG buffer/floor; surplus above the floor is sweepable only on x's action (no auto-sweep); the floor blocks sweeps/non-payout outflows, never driver payouts. D4 bank/chain split — 100% on-chain USDG once verified (one wallet, one balance, one checkable ratio; bank path stays as fallback, not a standing split).
- PHASING: nothing moves before live billing exists. Sequence: billing live (fallback posture) → on-ramp test → internal coverage display → first manual-batch payout under USDG → x reviews evidence → x's go-live word makes USDG backing standing → public display (D1) only after that. Conversion gate stays OFF until Nov 20 + x's go-live word regardless of backing readiness.
- DOES NOT CHANGE: prices, the 50/25/10/15 split, the fiat 75/25 legs, the $0.20 anchor (SUPERSEDED later the same day by the REWARDS REWORK addendum at the end of this file — the anchor is now $0.25), credit amounts/vesting, the points table + 500/day cap (the table itself was later re-weighted by that same rework addendum — see it for v2), conversion rules (1,000-pt blocks / 10,000 min / 100,000-mo cap), queue discipline, referral/giveaway terms, team/burn/locker destinations, swap fees, any gate.

## Addendum — REWARDS REWORK: $0.25 anchor, daily table v2, free-tier half-earn on sealed work (edit, 2026-10-26, x approved with amendments A1/A2; spec: rewards-rework-proposal-2026-10-26.md; profit sim Part 5 PASSED — conversions pay 100% at earner ratios 1.0 and 1.5 in base and zero-breakage stress, pool never negative)

- ANCHOR $0.20 → **$0.25 USD per 1,000 SM Points** (`ANCHOR_USD_PER_BLOCK` in `server/src/rewardsAnchor.ts`). This SUPERSEDES the $0.20 anchor statement in the Option C addendum above (marked there; history kept). Everything else about conversion is UNCHANGED: 1,000-point blocks, 10,000-point minimum balance, 100,000-point paid monthly cap, Earning-Pool-only, queue-if-dry, earned-first queue priority, price provider returns null pre-launch (fail closed), and the conversion gate (`points_conversion_live`) stays OFF until Nov 20 + x's go-live word. **NO free-tier conversion cap was added** (proposed in the rework memo, dropped by x — the free-tier governor is the half-earn rule below, not a conversion cap).
- DAILY POINTS TABLE v2 (new pure module `server/src/dailyPoints.ts`; `DAILY_POINTS_CAP` stays 500 and a perfect paid day still pays exactly 500): briefing check-in **25** (UNCHANGED — owner amendment A1) · pre-trip DVIR **50** (unchanged) · post-trip DVIR **25** (unchanged, still absorbed by the cap) · trip_sealed 100 → **120** · pod_seal (on-time POD) 150 → **165** · clean_day **75** (unchanged) · intel 50 → **35** · fuel_stop 25 → **10** · debrief 25 → **20**. Lines still add to 525; the cap trims a perfect day to 500. Clean-week bonus stays **+200**, outside the cap.
- FREE-TIER EARNING RULES (owner amendment A2): a user on the free/Basic tier (no active paid plan — the app's existing plan resolution via `myPlan` + `isGoldPlan`; company/fleet-covered drivers on an Owner install count as paid tier) earns HALF on sealed-work points only: trip_sealed pays **60** instead of 120, pod_seal pays **82** instead of 165 (floor of half). All other daily tasks pay full table value on the free tier (check-in 25, pre-trip DVIR 50, post-trip DVIR 25, clean day 75, intel 35, debrief 20). The **fuel_stop task pays NOTHING on the free tier** (fuel route/planner features are paid-tier only) — `awardDailyTask` grants no fuel_stop points and writes no ledger row for free-tier users. Implementation: every daily award routes through `dailyTaskPoints(task, paidTier)` in `dailyPoints.ts`; the tier is read at award time only, so points already earned by anyone are never retroactively changed. `dailyTaskState` shows each caller their own tier's values, matching what an award would pay. Clean-week +200, signup 100 pts, and the 1,000 subscription/renewal points (paid tiers) are unchanged.
- CLIENT COPY: every "$0.20 per 1,000" / "carries $0.20 of value" is now **$0.25** (conversion panel, SM Points wallet card + Coin Flow/Earning Pool copy, FAQ, glossary, scam-warning copy — the dynamic conversion readouts already render `ANCHOR_USD_PER_BLOCK` from the server); the FAQ daily-table listing shows the v2 values; the Basic plan card states that Basic earns half points on sealed trips and deliveries (60 / 82 vs the full 120 / 165) and that fuel route & planner tools are paid-plan features (fuel-stop points line is paid plans only).
- NOT CHANGED: welcome/yearly credit amounts ($1 Gold / $5 Diamond / $25 Fleet / $1 yearly) and their vesting shapes, referral and giveaway terms, subscription prices, the 50/25/10/15 split, the fiat 75/25 legs, any gate or billing state. NO sealed-miles pay was added (discussed only, not approved). — **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-26 by the Sealed Miles addendum at the end of this file: sealed miles were approved by x later that day, sim-gated (Part 6 PASSED), and built. History kept; this line records the state at the rework's build time.**
- VERIFICATION: `server/src/rewardsAnchor.test.ts` updated for the $0.25 anchor (block math, payout-price grid, fail-closed); `server/src/poolBacking.test.ts` liability expectations re-derived at $0.25; new `server/src/dailyPoints.test.ts` covers the v2 table, the 500 cap, (a) free trip_sealed 60 / pod_seal 82, (b) free fuel_stop 0, (c) paid 120 / 165 / 10, (d) free check-in/DVIR/post-trip DVIR/clean-day/intel/debrief at full value. Full suite + client/server typecheck results recorded in the build report. ## Addendum — Clauses A2b–A2f: free-tier balance retention, conversion/cash-out standing gate, 14-day retainer, post-reactivation hold (edit, 2026-10-26, x's clauses on top of the REWARDS REWORK above)

- A2b — FREE-TIER BALANCE RETENTION (the rule everything below builds on): a free/Basic driver keeps his full SM Points balance forever. Every free-available reward (signup 100, check-in 25, pre-trip DVIR 50, post-trip DVIR 25, half sealed work 60/82, clean day 75, clean-week +200, intel 35, debrief 20) stacks into the single points balance. The balance NEVER expires, is NEVER forfeited for staying free, and is NEVER wiped by a season reset or any other rollover; points already earned by anyone are never retroactively reduced. AUDIT RESULT (points/season/lapse paths, read in full): the codebase has NO rollover path that touches balances — the balance was and is the sum of every `points_log` row (`pointsBalanceFromLedger` in the new pure module `server/src/pointsRetention.ts`, which also now holds the season clock moved verbatim out of actions.ts); seasons only READ season-windowed records for scores/ranks and AWARD finished-season milestones; the only monthly construct is the conversion-cap counter; the welcome-vesting lapse operated on `welcome_vesting` USD-credit rows, never on points (its timing is retimed by A2e below). The only negative points rows that exist are pre-existing and untouched: the driver's own conversion payout, an owner's in-company gift, the House Rule 1 refund clawback (reverses only points a refunded PAID subscription itself granted — a free signup has no subscription events, so it can never touch free-earned points), and the POD-seal void correction. Season ranks/titles still reset; the points balance does not participate.
- A2c — CONVERSION/CASH-OUT LOCKED WHILE FREE (SUPERSEDES the conversion-parity draft first written under A2b — parity wording was removed from code, copy, and tests in this same build): while free, a driver CANNOT convert points and CANNOT cash out at all. Both money-exit actions refuse up front with the honest explanation (points keep stacking, never expire; converting unlocks on upgrade) — before any step-up, Review Hold, or queue row; the balance is untouched. On upgrade to any paying tier (the app's existing plan resolution — fleet/company-covered drivers count as paid), the retained balance unlocks and converts under the normal rules ($0.25 anchor, 10,000-pt minimum, 1,000-pt blocks, 100,000-pt monthly cap, Earning Pool queue, `points_conversion_live` gate still OFF).
- A2d — UNIVERSAL ACTIVE-ACCOUNT GATE: NO user converts or cashes out without an ACTIVE paying account in good standing. The gate (`moneyExitRefusal` / `planConversionRequest` in the new pure module `server/src/accountStanding.ts`) applies to points conversion AND to cash-out — inside `requestCashout` BEFORE the lane handling, so it covers BOTH lanes (instant + standard) identically, superseding any older "standard ACH for everyone" design for free/inactive accounts. Queued conversions also pay out only while the account is paid_active (`processPointsConversionQueue` standing check). States: free = never convertible (A2c) · paid + active = normal rules · paid but lapsed/cancelled = locked until reactivation, earned balance retained (never forfeited), unvested welcome credits per A2e timing.
- A2e — 2-WEEK RETAINER ON NON-RENEWAL (state + timers; migration 0056 `account_retainers`): when a paying account does not renew, the earned balance goes ON RETAINER for 14 days from the non-renewal date — conversion/cash-out locked (A2d), balance retained in full and displayed as "on retainer" with the retainer end date (`accountState`/`lockEndsAt` on `getPoints` + `getPointsConversion`; state-branched locked copy in the conversion panel, cash-out section, and Today panel). Renew within the 14 days → the retainer lifts in full and VESTING CLOCKS RESUME UNSPENT: every pending tranche's due date shifts by exactly the paused span (`vestingShiftOnLift`), so no tranche time is consumed; while the retainer runs, `processWelcomeVesting` settles expiry if due and otherwise PAUSES (nothing pays, nothing returns). Retainer expires un-renewed → the settle (`settleRetainerIfExpired`, reusing the shared `returnVestingTranche` claim + memorial + seal) returns unvested welcome credits to the Earning Pool AT THAT MOMENT — the existing lapse rule retimed from lapse date to retainer end — while the EARNED points balance stays retained and locked indefinitely, reactivatable on any later return, no further decay ever. The retainer is a grace window, never a cash-out release: nothing becomes payable during or because of it. The queued welcome-credit HEAD is not a vesting tranche and is untouched (its payout queue is pool-funding, unchanged). STAGING TRUTH: billing is not live, so the recorded-plan transition in `setPlan` (paid → Basic) is the non-renewal event, exactly as the plan-choice flow already stands in for settled payments; at go-live the billing path posts the same transition into `notePlanTransition` and every rule runs unchanged. Lifecycle is sealed: `points_retainer_started` / `points_retainer_lifted` / `points_retainer_expired` (+ the tranche `welcome_vesting_returned` events with reason `retainer_expired`). A later downgrade to a lower PAID tier is not a non-renewal — the pre-existing vesting tier rule (plan ≥ credit tier) still governs it.
- A2f — 14-DAY POST-REACTIVATION HOLD (migration 0057 `account_holds`): ANY reactivation after a non-renewal starts a 14-day hold from the reactivation date; conversion and BOTH cash-out lanes stay locked until the hold ends, in BOTH branches — (a) renewed inside the retainer (nothing forfeited; vesting clocks resume per A2e and tranches pay during the hold into the wallet balance, unlocking nothing) and (b) returned after retainer expiry (earned points balance only — credits returned at expiry do NOT come back). The account must stay ACTIVE through the whole hold: cancelling during it flips the hold to "cancelled" and opens a FRESH 2-week retainer cycle from that cancellation (A2e rules apply to the new cycle, including its expiry treatment). A first-ever subscription (no prior non-renewal) starts NO hold. UI shows the unlock date plainly in the reactivated state ("Reactivated — your balance unlocks on <date> while your account stays active") in the conversion panel, cash-out section, and Today panel; sealed as `points_reactivation_hold_started` / `points_reactivation_hold_completed` / `points_reactivation_hold_cancelled`. The global `points_conversion_live` gate is separate and unchanged (OFF until Nov 20 + x's go-live word) — the hold is an account-state lock layered under it.
- COPY: Basic plan card, the points FAQ, the free-plan FAQ, the glossary, the points explainer, the wallet SM Points card, and new House Rule 10 state plainly that points never expire and are never lost for staying free; House Rule 11 states the retainer/hold lifecycle. NO "use it or lose it" language aimed at the free tier existed or remains (the only forfeiture copy in the app is House Rule 3's anti-fraud multi-account rule and House Rule 1's refund clawback — neither is aimed at staying free; both untouched). Every older "if the plan ends, the unvested remainder returns" copy (plan-card welcome line, Coin Flow note, wallet vesting note, referral explainer, vesting FAQ) was retimed to the A2e retainer rule.
- NOT CHANGED: the $0.25 anchor, the v2 daily table, free half-earn values (60/82), prices, the 50/25/10/15 split, welcome/yearly credit amounts and vesting shapes (25% head + 6/12 tranches), referral/giveaway terms, any launch gate. NO sealed-miles pay was added. — **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-26 by the Sealed Miles addendum at the end of this file: sealed miles were approved by x later that day, sim-gated (Part 6 PASSED), and built. History kept; this line records the state at the A2b–A2f build time.**
- VERIFICATION: new `server/src/pointsRetention.test.ts` (6 tests: free ledger composition from the real tier function; balance identical across the real Season 1→2 boundary at 2026-11-12 UTC and across the Oct→Nov month boundary; a finished-season milestone only ever ADDS) and new `server/src/accountStanding.test.ts` (12 tests: A2c (a) free conversion refused — no queue row, balance unchanged, (b) free cash-out refused the same way, (c) post-upgrade conversion under the normal rules; A2d/A2e (a) in-retainer conversion + both cash-out lanes refused with balance unchanged, (b) renewal inside the retainer resumes vesting unspent — a tranche due 20 days out pays on its shifted schedule, (c) retainer expiry returns unvested credits with the earned balance unchanged and still locked a year later, (d) reactivation after expiry unlocks the retained balance after the hold; A2f (a) renew-in-retainer hold refusals + vesting running during the hold + unlock at hold end, (b) return-after-expiry keeps returned credits returned, (c) cancel-during-hold opens a fresh retainer with a new end date). Full suite 166/166 (148 pre-existing + 18 new) across 13 files; client + server typecheck clean. Full data wipe now also clears `account_retainers` + `account_holds`.

## Addendum — $STONER subscription payments: DECISION RECORDED, Option B (2026-10-26, x approved — "Approve all")

- DECISION: **fiat-only at launch.** When subscription billing opens (app-store release) and at the Nov 20 $STONER genesis, subscriptions are payable in fiat only (card / app-store billing, the 75/25 fiat path in §7 of the white paper). No $STONER subscription checkout at launch.
- FIRST POST-LAUNCH UPGRADE: **$STONER payment for subscriptions on WEB checkout only** — never in-app (Apple/Google store rules bar crypto payment for in-app subscriptions, so the token path can only ever live on the web checkout). Terms when it ships: the $STONER amount is **quote-locked at the payment moment** (the driver pays the token amount quoted at checkout, priced server-side at that moment — the same fail-closed price discipline as conversion payouts: no live price, no checkout); a $STONER-paid subscription **splits in full** under the existing $STONER-native path (50/25/10/15, no cash leg — unchanged); the operating-cash needs the fiat path's 25% leg would have covered are met by converting the relevant share to operating cash **on a fixed monthly schedule** (batched, scheduled, never dumped on arrival).
- STATUS: **decision recorded only — NO build, NO checkout change.** Nothing about current billing state changes: billing is still staged OFF until app-store release, the in-app Stripe checkout still waits on the platform server-secret slot, and the connector bridge remains the interim path. The web-checkout token upgrade is a post-launch item behind its own build pass and x's go-live word, like every other money gate.

## Addendum — Face ID / biometric unlock: SPEC (2026-10-26, x approved — spec only, NO build)

- SCOPE: **native wrap only.** Face ID (iOS) / device biometrics (Android) ship with the Capacitor native wrap at app-store release. The web/PWA app keeps the existing **PIN lock** until then — browsers cannot call real Face ID, and the app already says so honestly on the lock screen ("Device biometric unlock (Face ID / fingerprint) arrives with the native app wrapper"). The wrap itself comes after developer-account activation; this addendum specs the unlock design so the wrap build has it from day one. There is no separate app security spec document — this section is the spec of record (the Security Pack lives in-app, `client/src/SecurityPack.tsx`).
- DEFAULT UNLOCK: in the native wrap, **Face ID / device biometrics are the DEFAULT unlock** for the app lock. **PIN stays as the fallback** — always available, never removed; the existing PIN mechanics (PBKDF2-SHA256, salted, session unlock, auto-lock timer) carry over unchanged underneath.
- STEP-UP ON MONEY MOVES: a **fresh biometric check is required at the moment of any money move**, regardless of how recently the app was unlocked: **points conversion, cash-out (both lanes), and any send/pay action** (in-app $STONER sends, dispatch-fee payments, subscription payment changes when they exist). This layers on top of the existing step-up PIN (`verifyStepUpPin`) — in the wrap, biometrics become the step-up factor for these actions, with step-up PIN as the fallback factor. A money move that cannot complete a biometric (or fallback PIN) step-up does not proceed — fail closed, exactly like the payout price rule.
- CREDENTIAL HANDLING: the unlock credential stays **in the device keychain (iOS) / Keystore-backed secure storage (Android), gated by the platform biometric prompt** (secure-enclave-backed keys where the hardware provides them). **Biometric data never leaves the device and is never stored server-side** — the server receives only the same session/step-up proofs it receives today; no fingerprint or face template is ever transmitted, logged, or sealed. The app never asks for or stores a seed phrase or private key (unchanged standing rule).
- LOCKOUT DISCIPLINE: **failed biometrics fall back to PIN, never to no-lock.** Repeated biometric failure → the platform prompt withdraws (OS behaviour) → PIN entry is the path; repeated PIN failure keeps the existing lockout behaviour. There is no state in which the app opens without either a successful biometric or the correct PIN while a lock is set. If a user removes their PIN inside the wrap, biometric-only unlock must still be backed by a device-level credential — the app lock is never silently disabled by the wrap migration.
- NOT CHANGED: the web app's PIN lock, the auto-lock timer, the security-event sealing, House Rules, any money gate. Nothing is built under this addendum; the native wrap is gated on developer-account activation (x's accounts) and its own build pass after app-store release prep.

## Addendum — SEALED MILES built (edit, 2026-10-26, x approved: "Approve all"; spec: rewards-rework-proposal-2026-10-26.md § Sealed Miles; profit-sim Part 6 in rewards-profit-sim-2026-10-26.md PASSED the gate — task conversions pay 100% at earner ratios 1.0 and 1.5, base and zero-breakage stress, pool never negative). This addendum SUPERSEDES the two earlier "NO sealed-miles pay was added" lines (REWARDS REWORK and A2b–A2f addenda above, both now marked superseded with history kept).

- EARNING (new pure module `server/src/sealedMiles.ts`): a sealed trip earns **1 point per 2.5 sealed miles (0.4 pts/mile)** on the paid tier and **half rate (0.2 pts/mile)** on the free/Basic tier — the same tier resolution the daily award path uses (`dailyPointsPaidTier`; company/fleet-covered drivers count as paid tier). Anti-fraud monthly ceilings on miles awards: **8,000 pts/month paid · 4,000 pts/month free**. Per-trip awards are floored to whole points; fractional miles value is not carried. The pure functions never return negative and never past the ceiling given the month's already-awarded miles total (net of clawbacks).
- ACCRUAL AT TRIP SEAL: in `saveTrip` — the same server path that inserts the sealed trip record and awards the `trip_sealed` daily task — `awardSealedMiles` awards from the trip record's SEALED `totalMiles` (the trip-sheet/IFTA figure just written; never a live GPS estimate) as a `points_log` row with source **`sealed_miles`**, OUTSIDE the 500/day daily cap (like the clean-week bonus). One award per sealed trip (refKey `sealed_miles:trip:<id>`; `awardPoints` idempotency; a trip record is inserted once). VOID REVERSAL: `voidPodSeal` now also reverses the miles award of the trip that hauled the voided load (trip-queue link: queue row for the load → its completed trip sheet), the same way it claws back the seal's daily points — negative ledger row stamped with the award's instant so the month nets back, refKey `clawback:sealed_miles:<award row id>`, idempotent, and the clawed amount is included in the void's `pointsClawedBack`.
- CONVERSION ATTRIBUTION + QUEUE-LAST (migration **0058**): `points_conversions` gains `miles_points` (whole 1,000-pt blocks only), `task_paid_at`, and `miles_paid_at`. At request time the miles portion = min(requested points, floor(unconverted miles balance / 1000) × 1000), where unconverted miles balance = net miles awards − miles portions of conversions already paid or queued (`milesPortionForRequest` / `unconvertedMilesBalance` / `netSealedMilesPoints` in sealedMiles.ts). Earning Pool processing order is now (1) **task portions** of queued points conversions (FIFO), (2) **welcome/yearly credits**, (3) **miles portions** of queued conversions (FIFO) — `EARNING_POOL_QUEUE_ORDER` in rewardsAnchor.ts records the three phases and `processEarningPoolQueues` runs them in that order; conversion-only call sites drain task portions then miles portions. A conversion row completes (status "paid", `paid_at` set) only when BOTH portions have paid; points leave the driver's ledger only as each portion pays (`conversion-<id>-task` / `conversion-<id>-miles` ledger rows); each portion's anchor USD is its own blocks × $0.25 (`anchorUsdForPoints` per portion) and pays in $STONER at the run's single live price, fail-closed exactly as before. Legacy rows (anchor NULL) have no miles portion — their stored token amount is the task portion, unchanged.
- UNCHANGED GATES AND TERMS: the 100,000-pt monthly conversion cap counts the FULL request (paid + queued, both portions) exactly as today (`convertedPointsInMonth` untouched); the account-standing gate (A2c/A2d/A2e/A2f) and the step-up USD evaluation apply to the conversion as a whole, unchanged; the `points_conversion_live` gate stays **OFF** and `liveStonerPriceUsd` still returns null pre-launch — this build is earning-side only and nothing can pay out under it. Prices, the $0.25 anchor, the v2 daily table, credit terms, and every other gate are untouched.
- CLIENT COPY (honest, Numbers Rule — no split percentages): the points explainer, the "What are Points?" FAQ, the wallet SM Points card, and the conversion panel now carry: "Sealed miles — 1 point per 2.5 sealed miles (about $1.00 per 10,000 sealed miles); free tier earns half. Miles points land outside the daily 500 cap and convert after your other earned points." The conversion panel also states the pay order plainly (task-earned points first, credits next, miles points last; points leave the balance only as each part pays), and each conversion row with a miles portion shows its task/miles part payment state.
- VERIFICATION: new `server/src/sealedMiles.test.ts` (18 tests: paid rate, free half rate, floor, ceiling clamp, already-awarded accounting; award path — miles land outside a maxed daily cap, a second trip in the month respects the ceiling, last month doesn't consume this month, free tier half under its own ceiling, void reversal frees the ceiling and is idempotent; attribution — whole blocks, unconverted-balance accounting incl. committed portions, attribution never exceeds the unconverted balance; queue order — task before miles on the same request, pool covering task only leaves the miles portion queued with its points in the ledger, credits pay before miles portions, miles portions FIFO after all task portions, per-portion anchor math). `rewardsAnchor.test.ts` updated for the three-phase queue order. Full suite **184/184** (166 pre-existing + 18 new) across 14 files; client + server typecheck clean.

## Addendum — Receipts wired into fuel prices + the +15 receipt bonus (edit, 2026-10-26, x approved: "Build it — wire receipts into fuel prices + receipt bonus points"; economics gate: rewards profit sim Part 7, script `hidden_files/rewards-profit-sim-receipt-bonus-2026-10-26.mjs` in the goal workspace, PASSED before this build)

- AUTO PRICE FEED + GRADE FIELD (migration `0059_fuel_price_report_grade_and_nullable_coords.sql`): every logged fuel purchase (`logFuelPurchase`) now ALSO files its price into the shared `fuel_price_reports` layer, sealed in the hash chain exactly like a manual report (`fuel_price_reported`, refKind `fuel_price_report`). New `grade` column (text, NOT NULL, default `"reported"`; pre-existing rows backfill to `"reported"`): **`receipt_verified`** = filed from a purchase whose receipt photo was fingerprinted (SHA-256) · **`purchase`** = filed from a purchase with no receipt · **`reported`** = a manual driver sighting (`reportFuelPrice`, which now sets the grade explicitly on insert and in its chain payload). The price layer stays ANONYMOUS: no username on the purchase-derived row, in its chain payload (station, state, price, at, grade, source `"fuel_purchase"`, fuelId only), or in any response.
- NULLABLE-COORDINATES RATIONALE: the purchase form collects NO coordinates, so purchase-derived reports carry NULL `lat`/`lon` (the same migration makes both columns nullable via table rebuild). Coordinates are NEVER fabricated for a purchase. In the client matcher (`matchStopPrice` in `client/src/App.tsx`), null-coordinate reports are skipped by the 1.2-mile coordinate match and instead match a stop by station name + state — they stand as state-level price evidence, not pin-level. Among coordinate matches, freshest wins, except reports in the same hour count as equal freshness and the tie breaks by grade (receipt_verified > purchase > reported). Source labels state the grade honestly and never say "live": "receipt-verified purchase · <date>", "logged purchase · <date>", and the existing "reported <age> by a driver" for manual reports. `getFuelPriceData` reports now carry `grade` per row with nullable lat/lon; every existing field and the newest-first ordering are unchanged.
- BONUS MECHANICS (new pure module `server/src/fuelReceiptBonus.ts`; `RECEIPT_BONUS_POINTS = 15`): the bonus is evaluated ONLY on the `logFuelPurchase` call that actually WINS the day's `fuel_stop` award (`awardDailyTask` returns > 0 on that call) AND carried a fingerprinted receipt. If the day's fuel_stop was already won by an earlier purchase — or that earlier purchase had no receipt — no bonus fires that day. PAID TIER VIA THE AWARD: fuel_stop pays 0 on the free tier, so the award returns 0 and no bonus can fire; there is deliberately no second tier check. ONCE PER DAY per driver (driver-local America/Chicago day, the award path's `localDayKey`). GLOBAL RECEIPT DEDUPE: if ANY `points_log` row on ANY account has source `daily_fuel_receipt_bonus` and a refKey ending in `:` + the receipt's SHA-256, the bonus pays 0 (`awardPoints`' own refKey check is per-user, so the global scan is explicit in the handler) — a duplicate receipt still logs its purchase and files its price report; it just pays no bonus. INSIDE THE CAP: the bonus counts within the 500/day cap under the same daily-row accounting as `awardDailyTask` (refKey `daily:` rows for the day plus same-day daily clawbacks); if day total + 15 > 500 the bonus pays 0 with no partial award and the base fuel_stop award is unaffected. Awarded via `awardPoints` with source `daily_fuel_receipt_bonus`, refKey `daily:fuel_receipt_bonus:<dayKey>:<receiptSha256>`, reason "Fuel receipt bonus — receipt fingerprinted & sealed". The bonus is NOT a `DAILY_TASKS` entry and `dailyTaskPoints` is unchanged. `logFuelPurchase`'s response gains `receiptBonusAwarded` (0 or 15); `id`/`total` unchanged. Client copy (NextLevel fuel log) states the deal plainly by the receipt control — +15 on paid plans, once a day, each receipt earns it only once anywhere, your price (never your name) joins the prices other drivers see marked receipt-verified, the receipt stays sealed in your records for IFTA and taxes — and the success banner notes the bonus when it lands. Numbers Rule: no internal percentages or split figures in any copy.
- PART 7 SIM RESULT (the gate this build shipped behind): at the expected frequency of **11 receipt-bearing fuel purchases per paid earner per month**, task conversions pay **100% at earner ratios 1.0 and 1.5 in base AND zero-breakage stress**, and the pool never goes negative. CEILING SENSITIVITY, DISCLOSED NOT HIDDEN: at **22/mo** (double the expected frequency), the ratio-1.5 stress case pays **98.7%** — the only sub-100% cell in the Part 7 grid; ratios 1.0 stays at 100% there. The bonus size (15), the $0.25 anchor, the daily table, prices, and the split were NOT changed by this build.
- IFTA / TAX NOTE: the same purchase record already feeds IFTA (state + gallons against the sealed per-state miles) and the tax-category expense file, with the fingerprinted receipt sealed behind the numbers — this build reads nothing new for those paths and changes nothing there; one receipt upload now serves points, prices, and taxes from the one record the driver already keeps.
- NOT CHANGED: `points_conversion_live` stays OFF; `liveStonerPriceUsd` still returns null pre-launch; the $0.25 anchor, daily table v2 values, subscription prices, the 50/25/10/15 split, welcome/yearly credits, and every other gate are untouched.
- VERIFICATION: new `server/src/fuelReceiptBonus.test.ts` (15 tests: grade helper + rank + constants; bonus pays 15 on a receipt-carrying winning fuel_stop call; second receipt same day pays 0; a winning no-receipt purchase blocks a later receipt's bonus; the same receipt hash on a DIFFERENT account pays 0 while its purchase + price report still land; free tier pays 0; cap behavior — day at 480, base 10 lands at 490, bonus 0, base intact; once-per-day guard; eligibility gates; price rows graded receipt_verified / purchase with always-null coordinates; manual rows grade "reported" with real coordinates; no-receipt purchase files the price but pays no bonus; getFuelPriceData-shaped output carries a grade per report and contains no username anywhere). Full suite **199/199** (184 pre-existing + 15 new) across 15 files; client + server typecheck clean.

## Addendum — map customization: Layers & display + phone rotation (edit, 2026-10-26)
- **Owner ask**: "map should be able to have user customization, some people don't like things on their maps" — plus the follow-up the same night: "Also should be rotational with the phone."
- **Toggle inventory** (enumerated from the marker/route layer effect in App.tsx — one switch per category the map actually draws; pure logic in `client/src/mapPrefs.ts`, server mirror in `server/src/mapPrefs.ts`): **Fuel prices** (fuel-stop markers + diesel prices in tooltips) · **Parking** (parking intel markers, kinds `parking_open`/`parking_full`) · **Hazards & incidents** (Overpass restriction/weigh hazards: low bridge, weight limit, no-truck, weigh station) · **Road intel** (all other driver intel reports) · **Route & stops** (route polylines + destination pin) · **Speed readout** (speed/posted-limit overlay) · **Place labels** (the Esri reference overlays — separable ONLY on Hybrid; street tiles bake labels into the tile, stated in the panel). The base style picker (**Streets / Satellite / Hybrid**) moved into the same panel. **Traffic is NOT a toggle**: live traffic (TomTom) is never drawn as a map layer — it annotates route cards and the 30-minute scan — so no switch was invented for it. **Never toggleable**: the driver's own truck marker and active-navigation guidance. Panel copy states plainly that **spoken/audio hazard alerts are a safety behavior and stay on regardless of display toggles** — hazard/intel DATA keeps loading; only drawing is gated (the fetch structure is shared with the safety alerts and other tabs, so no per-category fetch is cut; nothing about points, prices, or gates changed, and no new map data source was added).
- **Persistence design**: the prefs ride the app's existing app-settings pattern — new columns on the single-row `app_settings` table (migration **0060**: `map_base_style` + seven `map_show_*` booleans), the `appSettingsShape` / `getAppSettings` / `saveAppSettings` actions extended (row mapping via `server/src/mapPrefs.ts` `mapPrefsFromRow`/`mapPrefsToRow`), applied at login on any device because they are account state. The client keeps the same local cache as all app settings (`tgps-app-settings`) so the map opens in the driver's chosen setup before the server answers; changes apply instantly and save in the background via `updateAppSettings`. There is no shared/global prefs store: prefs live on the account's own settings row in the account's own database, so one driver's setup can never surface on another's.
- **Defaults = today's exact behavior**: base `streets`, every category ON. A driver who never opens the panel sees a byte-identical map to the pre-customization build; an account with no settings row gets the defaults from both the server and the client.
- **Phone rotation (portrait ⇄ landscape)**: both orientations fully usable on the map screen. The heading-up plane side is now `mapPlaneSideFor(w, h)` in `routeGeometry.ts` — the frame diagonal + margin, symmetric in w/h, so rotating the phone yields the SAME plane side and the rotated plane covers the frame identically in both orientations (no clipped plane). Re-measure runs through the existing invalidateSize machinery on three paths: the wrap ResizeObserver (wrapSize change), the mapSize change, and new explicit `resize` + `orientationchange` window listeners — each scheduling the same staggered 60/220/500 ms `invalidateSize()` passes so Leaflet never keeps a stale size after rotation settles. Portrait remains the default driving view; the rotation/heading-up machinery itself is untouched (display + sizing only).
- **Compass heading-up**: bearing source selection is the pure `selectBearingSource` (`client/src/mapPrefs.ts`): GPS course while moving (≥3 mph), phone compass while stationary, hold otherwise (a parked truck without compass never spins its map). Compass listening starts ONLY from the driver's explicit tap on "Enable compass" in the Layers panel — on iOS that tap calls `DeviceOrientationEvent.requestPermission()` (never requested on load); elsewhere listening simply starts. `webkitCompassHeading` is preferred, absolute `alpha` is the fallback. The truck marker arrow uses the same chosen heading.
- **Tests**: `client/src/mapPrefs.test.ts` (defaults = today's map; normalize round-trip set → reload → same setup incl. stale-cache merge and invalid-value fallback; turning any one category off removes exactly that drawn layer; parking intel obeys the parking switch not the intel switch; bearing source selection — GPS moving / compass stationary / hold / fallback), `server/src/mapPrefs.test.ts` (defaults; prefs → row → prefs identity; per-account isolation — one driver's row never leaks into another's read), `routeGeometry.test.ts` additions (plane side identical across rotation, covers frame corners in both orientations, unmeasured fallback). Full suite **219/219** (199 pre-existing + 20 new) across 17 files; client + server typecheck clean.

## Addendum — profile display: tier chip + featured badge (edit, 2026-10-26)
- **Tier chip**: `getUserProfile` now carries `tier` — derived SERVER-SIDE from the account's recorded plan (the `myPlan` source of truth; never client-supplied, never stored on the social profile) via `tierForPlan` in `server/src/profileBadges.ts`: basic → **Basic**, gold → **Gold**, diamond → **Diamond**, owner → **Fleet** (the Owner plan is the fleet plan); legacy plan values normalize as everywhere else in the app. Rendered as a chip beside the display name in the profile header, styled per tier (Diamond distinct cyan, Gold gold, Fleet violet, Basic plain — `tierChipFor` in `client/src/profileBadges.ts`). Display-only: no price, no internal numbers, no gate reads it. In this staging build the tier is real for the local user (their plan lives on this device); for other drivers it is null and no chip renders — another driver's plan is never invented, the same honesty rule the rank fields already follow.
- **Featured badge**: the driver picks ONE earned badge to feature. Earned set = what exists today: **referral** (referral badge, level ≥ Dispatcher — Spotter has no badge), **all_time_best** (an all-time-best season rank on record), **season_rank** (a current-season rank). Stored per account on `social_users.featured_badge` (migration **0061**; null = no choice → today's default display order: season rank, all-time best, referral). The payload carries `earnedBadges` + the validated `featuredBadge`; the profile-edit form's "Feature a badge" picker lists ONLY earned badges. Validation is server-side on both write (`updateMyProfile` recomputes the earned set from referral + rank records and stores null for an unearned choice) and read (`getUserProfile` re-validates the stored choice via `resolveFeaturedBadge`) — a driver cannot feature an unearned badge, and an invalid/stale choice falls back to the default order silently. The featured badge renders first in the header badge row with an accent ring + ⭐; the rest render as before. Existing privacy holds: reputation and miles panels stay driver-controlled, no location is ever on a profile.
- **LOCKED DECISION (x, 2026-10-26) — profile miles panel stays VISIBLE BY DEFAULT**: `showMilesPanel` defaults to true (current behavior, unchanged by this build); the driver can hide it in profile settings, and a hidden panel is never overridden — for another driver the numbers are not even computed into the profile response. Record-only: no default or behavior changed.
- **Tests**: `server/src/profileBadges.test.ts` (tier derivation per plan incl. legacy normalization and the no-row default; earned-set composition — Spotter earns no referral badge; unearned featured choices rejected; stale/invalid choices fall back silently), `client/src/profileBadges.test.ts` (distinct chip styling per tier; featured-first ordering with the rest in default order; out-of-set featured choice changes nothing). Full suite **232/232** (219 + 13 new) across 19 files; client + server typecheck clean.

## Addendum — Traffic notes display toggle (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Owner ask**: "Add it — traffic notes on route cards get a display toggle too" (x, 2026-10-27). This resolves the traffic-toggle audit flag left by the map-customization addendum above: its line "**Traffic is NOT a toggle**" is SUPERSEDED by this addendum (history left as written, per this file's dated-addenda convention). Live traffic is still never drawn on the map, so the toggle is honestly scoped to what traffic actually is in this app — DISPLAY-ONLY annotations — and lives in the same Layers & display panel under its own "Route cards" heading. It is deliberately NOT in `MAP_LAYER_CATEGORIES` / the "On the map" list, because it draws nothing.
- **Scope — exactly two surfaces gated**: (a) **Route cards** — the whole TomTom traffic note block on each route option card (live delay + traffic-aware ETA vs free-flow, the "flowing — no live delay" state, "Traffic: unavailable right now", "Traffic: checking live traffic (TomTom)…", and the Basic-tier "live traffic is a Gold feature" line). With the toggle OFF none of that block renders; everything else on the card is untouched, including the NWS severe-weather paragraph and the safety label/score. (b) **Scan-ahead status pill** — (i) the "clear" headline's traffic suffix ("· live traffic adds ~+N min through this stretch") renders only when ON; (ii) the secondary source line's TomTom traffic-state notes ("traffic data as of …", "TomTom traffic didn't answer — this scan is running on driver reports only…", "checking traffic…") are replaced when OFF (Gold tier) with wording that carries no traffic-state annotation but stays honest that the scan still uses live traffic and still warns ("Driver reports + live traffic watching ahead. Traffic notes are off in Layers & display — the scan still uses live traffic and still warns."). The threats count line ("N report(s) ahead…") is alert-grade and NEVER gated; the Basic-tier source line is unchanged.
- **Safety carve-out — never gated**: traffic/hazard data fetching (`getRouteTraffic` / `getScanTraffic` calls happen exactly as before), the 30-minute scan-ahead protective function itself (threat detection, escape-route pre-build, alert banner, voice/banner alerts), spoken/audio hazard alerts, the separate "Hazards & incidents" map toggle, and map hazard/intel drawing. Ask Meca's traffic answers are driver-initiated explicit queries, not display annotations — unchanged. Panel copy by the switch states this plainly: turning traffic notes off hides the notes only — safety alerts never hide.
- **Storage — per account, default ON**: migration **0062** adds `map_show_traffic_notes` (integer, DEFAULT 1 NOT NULL) to the account's own single `app_settings` row (id = 1, own database) — the same 0060 mechanism end to end: `server/src/schema.ts`, `server/src/mapPrefs.ts` (`MapPrefs` / `MapPrefsRow` / `MAP_PREFS_DEFAULTS` / `mapPrefsFromRow` / `mapPrefsToRow`), `appSettingsShape` + `APP_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS` in `server/src/actions.ts`, and the client (`MapPrefs.showTrafficNotes` in `client/src/mapPrefs.ts`, App.tsx settings defaults + normalize mapping + the local-cache path). DEFAULT ON (true) = today's exact behavior; nobody's display changes until they change it. The gating seam is the pure helper `trafficNotesVisible(prefs)` in `client/src/mapPrefs.ts`, consulted by both the route-card render and the scan-ahead pill.
- **Tests**: `server/src/mapPrefs.test.ts` (traffic notes default ON when no row; round-trip preserves explicit false incl. the new field; isolation coverage unchanged), `client/src/mapPrefs.test.ts` (normalize defaults `showTrafficNotes` true, preserves explicit false, round-trips; `trafficNotesVisible` OFF suppresses the notes while `visibleMapLayers` hazards category / `isIntelVisible` are unaffected, and no layer category exists for traffic notes; ON/default shows them). Full suite **236/236** (232 pre-existing + 4 new) across 19 files; client + server typecheck clean.
