Nothing about points, prices, or gates changed: the $0.25 anchor / `live: false` conversion answer and the seal chain are untouched.

## Addendum — miles-only 2x for the 2x cohort built (edit, 2026-10-27, Part B of the narrower-2x order; sim gate: rewards-profit-sim-2026-10-26.md § Part 9, script rewards-profit-sim-narrow2x-2026-10-27.mjs, PASSED for the miles-only variant)

- **GATE VERDICT RECORDED**: Part 9 PASSED for the miles-only variant, and that variant ONLY is what shipped here. The FULL 2x (doubled daily table + 1,000/day cap) FAILED its earlier gate and stays UNSHIPPED — it was not built. The fuel receipt bonus doubling was HELD at 15 by the sim: `server/src/fuelReceiptBonus.ts` amounts and behavior are unchanged for everyone, and the bonus can only be revisited if the rewards slice grows; any future task-point addition re-runs the gate first.
- **COHORT RULE (new pure module `server/src/earningClass.ts`)**: an "earning class" is resolved SERVER-SIDE ONLY (never client-supplied) from existing facts — company membership (`myMembership`: none = owner-operator; role owner/driver/dispatch), the recorded plan (`myPlan`/`normalizePlan`: basic|gold|diamond|owner), and the resolved account standing (`myStanding`/`resolveStanding` in accountStanding.ts). IN the cohort (miles multiplier 2) ONLY when standing state is `paid_active` AND either (a) no company membership (owner-operator) AND plan is gold or diamond, or (b) company role `owner` AND plan is `owner` (Fleet). Company drivers (role driver) are NEVER in the cohort at any tier; dispatch never; free/Basic never; any non-paid_active standing (free, in_retainer, lapsed_locked, reactivated_hold) never — a lapsed owner-operator loses the 2x until paid_active again.
- **SEALED MILES WIRING (`server/src/sealedMiles.ts` extended; existing exported signatures/tests intact)**: cohort rate **0.8 pts/mile (1 point per 1.25 miles)** (`SEALED_MILES_RATE_COHORT`) and cohort monthly ceiling **16,000 pts** (`SEALED_MILES_MONTHLY_CEILING_COHORT`), added alongside `SEALED_MILES_RATE_PAID` etc. via `sealedMilesRatePerMileWithCohort` / `sealedMilesMonthlyCeilingWithCohort` / `sealedMilesPointsForTripWithCohort` (a non-cohort call is byte-identical to `sealedMilesPointsForTrip`). Everyone else is exactly as before: paid 0.4 pts/mile / 8,000 ceiling; free 0.2 / 4,000. `awardSealedMiles` in actions.ts routes through the cohort math with the class resolved by the new `myEarningClass` helper. The award ledger reason records the class honestly — cohort: "…at 1 point per 1.25 miles (2x miles — Owner-Operator Diamond)" / "(2x miles — Owner-Operator Gold)" / "(2x miles — Fleet Owner)"; non-cohort reason strings are unchanged. Idempotency is unchanged (same refKey `sealed_miles:trip:<id>` per trip — already-awarded trips are NOT re-rated; points already earned are never re-rated, same rule as the daily table). Conversion attribution and miles-queue-last ordering are unchanged.
- **UI — TRUTHFUL EARNING CLASS**: `getPoints` now returns an `earningClass` block — `{ milesMultiplier (1|2), kind ("oo" | "fleet_owner" | null), label, milesRatePerMile, milesMonthlyCeiling }` — with labels "2x miles earning — Owner-Operator Gold", "2x miles earning — Owner-Operator Diamond", "2x miles earning — Fleet Owner", and "Standard miles earning". The client points/wallet panels (Driver-tab SM Points panel, the Money-tab points card, and the wallet SM Points card) display the label plus the driver's actual miles rate (1 point per 1.25 / 2.5 / 5 sealed miles, derived from `milesRatePerMile`) and monthly ceiling; the sealed-miles explainer copy and the "What are Points?" FAQ now state the cohort terms. No internal numbers (no split percentages, no pool figures) appear anywhere. Daily table values, the 500 cap, welcome/yearly credits, and referral points are NOT multiplied — miles only.
- **NO MIGRATION**: the cohort derives entirely from existing rows (company_members, profile.plan, account retainers/holds) — no schema change was needed.
- **VERIFICATION**: new `server/src/earningClass.test.ts` (14 tests: the pure cohort matrix — OO Gold paid_active IN; OO Diamond paid_active IN; Fleet owner (role owner + plan owner) paid_active IN; company driver on Diamond OUT; company driver on Owner-plan install OUT (role driver); dispatch OUT at every plan; OO Basic OUT; OO Gold in_retainer/lapsed_locked/reactivated_hold OUT with the 2x regained at paid_active; near-misses OUT (OO on Owner plan alone, fleet role without the Owner plan); cohort miles math — 0.8 rate, floor, 16,000 ceiling clamp, month netting with clawbacks freeing ceiling room; non-cohort math byte-identical to `sealedMilesPointsForTrip` across a rate/ceiling grid). Full suite **250/250** (236 pre-existing + 14 new) across 20 files; client + server typecheck clean. `fuelReceiptBonus.ts` untouched; the $0.25 anchor and every other gate untouched.

## Addendum — owner-operator fleet-creation right (edit, 2026-10-27, Part C of the narrower-2x order; locked by x 2026-10-27)

- **THE RIGHT**: creating a fleet (a company) is an owner-operator Gold/Diamond right. Company drivers and dispatch can NEVER create a fleet — they hold a company membership — and a company owner cannot create a second company.
- **SERVER GATE (`createCompany` in actions.ts, pure decision in new `server/src/fleetCreation.ts`)**: creation requires BOTH (a) no existing company membership (unchanged refusal: "You already belong to a company — leave it first."), AND (b) an eligible class in ACTIVE standing (`paid_active`, resolved by the existing `myStanding`/`resolveStanding` machinery): the owner-operator branch — no membership + recorded plan gold or diamond + `paid_active` (the Part B cohort's OO branch) — OR the fleet-owner class — no membership + plan `owner` + `paid_active`. Refusals are plain driver language with the path forward: Basic/free solo → "Creating a fleet is an Owner-Operator Gold/Diamond right — upgrade to Gold or Diamond first…"; eligible plan but not `paid_active` (retainer / lapsed / reactivation hold) → "Creating a fleet unlocks when your account is back in active standing — renew or finish your reactivation hold…". The gate facts are resolved server-side only (`myMembership` / `myPlan` / `myStanding`); nothing is client-supplied.
- **FLEET PRICING FROM CREATION**: when a Gold/Diamond owner-operator creates a fleet, the fleet runs on Fleet (Owner) pricing from creation. The creator's recorded plan transitions to `owner` through the same recorded-plan path a normal plan change uses: `notePlanTransition` runs the lifecycle effects (gold/diamond → owner is paid → paid, so no retainer/hold effects fire), the profile plan is rewritten to `owner`, a `subscription_events` row is recorded (plan `owner`, `paymentRef` `fleet-creation:<username>:<companyId>`, `pointsAwarded` 0, not counted for giveaway — it is a plan-basis record, NOT a payment), and a sealed chain event `fleet_creation_plan_transition` records the from/to plans with fleet creation as the cause. NO charge is taken and NO checkout is faked — billing begins at app-store release, like all billing in this app, and the copy says exactly that where the plan is shown. A creator already recorded on the Owner plan transitions nothing (Fleet pricing already applies). Downstream, the creator is company role `owner` + plan `owner`, so the Part B cohort resolves them via the fleet-owner branch (`earningClass` kind `fleet_owner`) from creation.
- **CLIENT**: `getCompany` now returns a `fleetCreation` block for SOLO accounts only (`{ canCreate, plan, standingState, note }`; null for company members — their view is unchanged). The create-company surface shows the form enabled for qualifying OO Gold/Diamond accounts with the copy "Owner-Operator Gold/Diamond accounts can create a fleet — Fleet pricing applies from creation; billing begins at app-store release"; non-qualifying solo accounts see why and what unlocks it (the gate's own refusal text) instead of a dead button. A successful creation also invalidates the recorded-plan query so every plan display reflects Fleet pricing immediately.
- **NO MIGRATION**: the gate and the transition run entirely on existing rows (company_members, profile.plan, account retainers/holds, subscription_events, chain_log) — no schema change was needed.
- **VERIFICATION**: new `server/src/fleetCreation.test.ts` (12 tests: OO Gold paid_active allowed; OO Diamond paid_active allowed; solo Owner-plan paid_active allowed via the fleet-owner class; creator ends as company role `owner` on the owner/Fleet plan basis; cohort continuity — post-creation facts resolve to `fleet_owner` in the Part B cohort; refused for solo Basic (free standing, upgrade path), solo Gold in_retainer, solo Gold lapsed_locked, solo Gold reactivated_hold (active-standing path); refused for company driver and dispatch (membership held); refused for an existing company owner (second company)). Full suite **262/262** (250 pre-existing + 12 new) across 21 files; client + server typecheck clean.

## Addendum — Owner Giveaway Portal (edit, 2026-10-27, Part D of the narrower-2x order; locked by x 2026-10-27)

- **OWNER-MONEY-ONLY RULE**: the portal is one company owner's own funds given to that owner's own drivers. It NEVER reads or writes the Earning Pool, the Reward Wallet, or any platform giveaway table (the subscriber giveaway above is a separate system and is untouched), and the app never holds owner funds: it records payouts the owner made from their OWN profile wallet, verifies them on chain, and publishes the record. WHO: company owners only (role "owner" via `myMembership`) — owner-operators WITH a fleet (Part C) qualify the same way. A solo account with no company gets the explanation (create a fleet first — the Part C right — and add drivers), never a dead end; dispatch and drivers never run giveaways.
- **ROSTER SCOPING**: recipients/entrants are ONLY the owner's own company members with role "driver", enforced server-side on EVERY action through the pure `validateRosterSelection` in `server/src/ownerGiveaway.ts`: a driver from another company is not on the roster and is refused; the owner and dispatch are never entrants (refused by name); duplicates refused. Reads are scoped the same way — an owner loads giveaways only for their own company id, and a driver sees only giveaways of their own company where they hold a recipient/entrant row.
- **DATA (migration 0063)**: `owner_giveaways` (company, owner, currency USDG|USD|STONER|ETH, pot in major units, mode selected|random, winnerCount, status draft|funded|distributed|cancelled, commitmentHash, seedMethod + drawSeed + drawHeadHash set at draw, distributionNote, created/funded/distributed stamps) and `owner_giveaway_recipients` (giveaway, username, payoutAddress — the driver's Robinhood Chain address supplied/confirmed by the owner at creation and VISIBLE to the driver in their own view so a wrong address can be flagged before funding — amount/share, isWinner, latest txHash + verification + verifiedAmount + recordedAt). In selected mode every recipient row is a payee; in random mode every entrant gets a row at creation (amount 0) and the draw flips winners to their equal share. Every lifecycle step appends a seal-chain event (`owner_giveaway_created` — with commitment + entrant list in random mode — `owner_giveaway_drawn`, `owner_giveaway_payout_recorded`, `owner_giveaway_funded`, `owner_giveaway_distributed`, `owner_giveaway_cancelled`): the chain is the published record.
- **PURE MATH (`server/src/ownerGiveaway.ts`, unit-tested)**: splits run in integer minor units (USD 2dp; USDG/$STONER/ETH 6dp) with largest-remainder distribution so recipient amounts sum EXACTLY to the pot; per-driver amounts validate sum == pot exactly or refuse with the signed difference named. Commitment = SHA-256 over the canonical string of {companyId, sorted entrant usernames, pot at currency precision, currency, winnerCount, createdAt ISO} (`commitmentCanonicalString`; the hash itself is computed by the caller, the receivableKey/verifyAudit injected-hash pattern) — recomputable by anyone from the published record. Draw: seed = SHA-256(commitmentHash || seal-chain head hash immediately before the draw entry is appended); winners = deterministic sampling without replacement from the sorted entrant list via mulberry32 seeded from the seed's first 32 bits (partial Fisher–Yates); same seed + entrants always yields the same winners; winners share the pot equally via the split function. State machine: funded ⟺ every payee's amount is covered by a VERIFIED recorded payout (verifiedAmount ≥ amount per payee; partial verification stays draft); distributed only from funded, by owner confirmation; cancelled only from draft.
- **FUNDING / VERIFICATION MODEL (honest rails only)**: crypto (USDG/$STONER/ETH, all Robinhood Chain 4663 assets) — the owner sends each payout from their own profile wallet, then records the tx hash against that recipient. The portal verifies on chain 4663 (`verifyGiveawayTx`, following the `recordFeePaymentCore`/`verifyCryptoTx` pattern): the tx exists AND value actually reaches the recipient row's payoutAddress — native ETH via tx.to/value; ERC-20 via the receipt's Transfer logs to that address from the currency's token contract (USDG uses the app's known canonical Paxos USDG address from `poolBacking.ts`; $STONER has NO verified contract address in the app pre-launch, so a $STONER transfer cannot be confirmed from the tx and the outcome is recorded honestly as "unavailable"/not verified — never faked). Outcomes map exactly like dispatch fees — verified / not_found / recipient_mismatch / unavailable — and only "verified" counts toward funded, at the amount that truly arrived (verifiedAmount), so a short payment never covers more than it paid. A failed verification never blocks recording a corrected tx hash for the same recipient: the latest record stands, with its outcome on record.
- **THE DRAW'S HONEST LIMIT**: the published record makes post-commitment tampering DETECTABLE — the commitment fixes entrants + pot before the draw exists, the seed binds that commitment to the chain head at draw time, and every participant can recompute both. It is a verifiable record, NOT a claim of owner-proof randomness: the owner runs the draw, and what the record guarantees is that the inputs were fixed in public beforehand and the outcome follows from them.
- **USD GATE**: USD campaigns can be created, rosters set, and random draws run, but `recordOwnerGiveawayPayout` refuses with the honest gate — USD distribution "opens with Stripe rails" (the app has no server-secret slot for a payout processor). NO fake money movement, NO fake balances: a USD giveaway stays in draft with that note shown to owner and drivers, and can never reach funded/distributed.
- **ACTIONS + CLIENT**: `createOwnerGiveaway` (roster scope, amounts, winnerCount ≤ entrants; seals the commitment for random mode at creation), `runOwnerGiveawayDraw` (random mode, draft only, undrawn only; publishes entrants, seed method, head hash, seed, winners — visible to owner AND every entrant), `recordOwnerGiveawayPayout` (owner only, crypto only, per recipient), `confirmOwnerGiveawayDistribution` (funded → distributed), `cancelOwnerGiveaway` (draft only), `getOwnerGiveaways` (owner console: roster + full detail incl. per-recipient verification states), `getMyOwnerGiveaways` (driver view: giveaways where I am a recipient or an entrant — currency, my amount, my payout address, status, and the FULL draw record for random ones). Client: a "Giveaways" section in the Owner Console (create form, list, draw/payout/distribute/cancel controls, draw record) and a driver-facing "Giveaways from your owner" card in the wallet area. Copy is plain and honest about what is verified vs merely recorded; no internal numbers.
- **VERIFICATION**: new `server/src/ownerGiveaway.test.ts` (15 tests: roster scoping — owner A cannot select owner B's driver, owner/dispatch never entrants, own drivers validate, duplicates refused; split math exactness — equal split incl. remainder cases at 2dp and 6dp, per-driver sum mismatch refused with the difference named; draw determinism — same inputs → same winners, winners ⊆ entrants, exact count, no duplicates, commitment recomputation matches and is fact-sensitive; state machine — no funded until ALL payees verified-covered, partial verification stays draft, only "verified" counts, USD payout recording refused and USD can never reach funded/distributed, cancel only from draft, distribute only from funded). Full suite and typecheck results recorded in the build report for this edit.

## Addendum — OO → Fleet Owner switch billing basis: plan difference + per-seat (edit, 2026-10-27; locked by x 2026-10-27)

- **THE RULE**: "OO → Fleet Owner switch = plan difference + per-seat beyond included allowances (locked by x 2026-10-27)." This amends the Part C fleet-creation addendum above: the gate (who may create/switch) is UNCHANGED — company drivers and dispatch still can never create; only the billing basis the switch records is new. No downgrade flow exists or was invented.
- **DIFFERENCE**: Fleet base price minus the switching account's current plan price at the LOCKED public prices, computed on the same billing period the account is on (the period on the account's most recent subscription event; monthly when none exists). Monthly: Gold $9.99 / Diamond $19.99 / Fleet $49.99 → differences $40.00 / $30.00 / $0.00. Annual: $99 / $199 / $499 → $400.00 / $300.00 / $0.00. An OO already on the Fleet/Owner plan pays $0 difference.
- **PER-SEAT**: $9.99/mo ($99/yr) per driver OR dispatcher seat beyond the Fleet plan's included allowances — up to 3 trucks/drivers (the owner occupies one driver seat, the same counting as the Owner plan's "up to 3 trucks") plus the first dispatch seat included. All math is integer cents in pure functions in `server/src/fleetCreation.ts` (`PLAN_PRICE_CENTS`, `FLEET_EXTRA_SEAT_CENTS`, `fleetSwitchBillingBasis`, `countFleetRosterSeats`, `fleetSwitchBillingLine`, `fleetBillingBasisChanged`), unit-tested.
- **ITEMIZED RECORD**: `createCompany` now writes the transition record in ALL switch cases (including already-on-Owner, $0 difference — the record is the anchor the living basis recomputes from): a `subscription_events` row (`paymentRef` `fleet-creation:<username>:<companyId>`, plan `owner`, the account's ACTUAL billing period — Part C hardcoded monthly; pointsAwarded 0, not a payment) AND the sealed chain event `fleet_creation_plan_transition`, whose payload carries the full itemization: previous plan, fleet plan, period, current-plan price, Fleet base price, difference, included seats (3 drivers / 1 dispatch), roster seats at transition, extra seats × rate, seat charges, total first-cycle fleet billing basis, and the plain switch line.
- **LIVING FIGURE**: the basis is recomputed on roster/plan changes — every roster mutation (`joinCompany`, `leaveCompany`, `setCompanyRole`, `removeCompanyMember`, `addCompanyDriver`) calls `refreshFleetBillingBasis`, which reads the anchor (fromPlan + period + last basis) back from the sealed chain, recounts seats from the CURRENT roster, and — only when the basis moved — records a `subscription_events` row (kind `fleet_basis`, `paymentRef` `fleet-billing:<companyId>:<n>`, pointsAwarded 0) plus a sealed chain entry `fleet_billing_basis_update` with the new full itemization. Companies created before this rule have no anchor and are untouched.
- **UI — BEFORE CONFIRM**: `getCompany`'s `fleetCreation` block (solo accounts only, as before) now also returns `billingBasis` + `billingLine`, computed by the SAME pure function `createCompany` records, projected on the roster the company starts with (the creator alone: 1 driver seat, 0 dispatch — inside the allowances, so the seat charge at creation is $0 and the first-cycle basis is the difference alone). The create-fleet flow renders the line verbatim — Diamond monthly: "Switching to Fleet Owner: $30.00 difference from your Diamond plan + $9.99 per extra driver/dispatcher seat — charged when billing opens at app-store release; nothing is charged today." — plus the itemized table (current plan + price, Fleet plan + price, difference, seats included, extra seats × rate, first-cycle total) ABOVE the company-name field and Create button.
- **HONESTY**: billing itself still opens at app-store release — no checkout, no charge today; the amounts are computed, recorded, and shown as what WILL be charged when billing opens. Nothing about points, the $0.25 anchor / `live: false` conversion, or the seal mechanics changed.
- **NO MIGRATION**: the basis rides on existing rows (subscription_events, chain_log payloads, company_members, profile).
- **VERIFICATION**: 13 new tests in `server/src/fleetCreation.test.ts` (difference math per starting plan — Gold $40.00 / Diamond $30.00 / Fleet $0.00 monthly, annual variants $400/$300/$0, legacy "full" normalization; seat counting — roster within allowances = $0 seat charges, extras exact at both periods incl. the change detector the living recompute uses; itemization fields present in the basis object; switch copy verbatim for the Diamond monthly case and following the account's own figures otherwise). Full suite **290/290** (277 pre-existing + 13 new) across 22 files; client + server typecheck clean.

## Addendum — Pay Book: W-2 + 1099, combined tracking, separate filing (edit, 2026-10-27)

- **X'S RULE, in his words**: a driver can "keep up with w2 n 1099 pay into a total" — "You can mix the two" for tracking — but "When you do taxes you have to file them separately." Locked product rule: **combined tracking, separate filing.** The combined total (W-2 gross + 1099 gross) is a keep-up/dashboard number only, labelled exactly that in the UI ("for keeping up, not a filing number"); it NEVER appears as a merged filing figure in either tax packet.
- **DATA (migration 0064)**: new `pay_entries` table, starting EMPTY. Each entry: kind (`w2` | `1099`), `isForm` flag, payer/employer name, pay date, tax year (form entries), gross, federal withheld, state withheld, other deductions (W-2 pile fields), notes, and an optional pay-stub/form photo stored as a blob with a SHA-256 fingerprint — the expenses pattern verbatim. Logged and deleted entries seal `pay_entry_logged` / `pay_entry_deleted` chain events (append-only; deletes never rewrite history). The full data wipe now also clears `pay_entries`.
- **ACTIONS**: `logPayEntry`, `listPayEntries`, `deletePayEntry` (same discipline as `logExpense`/`listExpenses`/`deleteExpense`), and `getPayBook({period})` with week/month/quarter/year/all windows (week/month/quarter mirror getMoneyTruth's rolling windows; year = Jan 1 local). Summary math lives in pure functions in `server/src/payBook.ts` (`summarizePayBook`, `payBookPeriodStart`) — integer-cents internally: W-2 subtotal (gross, federal/state/total withheld, other deductions, net paid, per-employer breakdown), 1099 subtotal (gross, per-payer breakdown), combined tracking total, plus the period's expense categories + fuel total carried on the 1099 side and the 1099 net line (1099 gross − expenses − fuel).
- **YEAR-END FORMS**: an entry can be the form itself (`isForm`) — payer + tax year + gross as printed — tying the January W-2/1099 to its pile. Form entries are listed separately ("Year-end forms on file") and are NEVER counted in the pay totals (the paychecks already counted that money — no double-count). The form's scan rides the same stub photo slot (JPEG/PNG); a PDF form file still lives in the existing personal document vault, with its numbers logged here — no second vault was built.
- **UI**: `PayBookPanel` (client/src/NextLevel.tsx) sits directly under Money Truth on both the Money tab and the Driver tab: period tabs, W-2 total card (with withholding), 1099 total card, combined tracking card with the not-a-filing-number label, per-payer lines, forms-on-file list, the log form (kind toggle W-2/1099, year-end-form checkbox, withheld fields shown for W-2 only, stub photo), and the recent-entries list with delete. Honest empty state when nothing is logged.
- **TAX EXPORTS — SEPARATE, the heart of the rule**: two XBar exports beside the Pay Book summary. (a) **W-2 packet**: per-employer breakdown, every logged W-2 paycheck, W-2 totals (gross, federal/state/total withheld, other deductions, net paid), year-end W-2 forms on file. (b) **1099 packet**: per-payer breakdown, every logged 1099 payment, 1099 gross total, the categorized expense totals + fuel purchases from the driver's own logged records, the 1099 net line, year-end 1099 forms on file. Each packet states plainly: "STONEMILES organizes paperwork — it does not file taxes," names itself as ONE pile only, and notes the other pile is a separate packet. The combined total appears in NEITHER packet as a figure. The pre-existing Money Truth tax-time category export is unchanged.
- **UNCHANGED**: Money Truth cost-per-mile/break-even math, IFTA, rewards/points, pricing/tiers, seal-chain mechanics. No internal percentages or split figures in any Pay Book copy.
- **HONEST LIMITS**: entries are driver-entered (no payroll import, no employer feed); withholding fields are what the driver reads off the stub; the 1099 net line is a prep figure from logged records only, not tax advice; stub photos are JPEG/PNG (PDF forms go to the document vault as above).
- **VERIFICATION**: new `server/src/payBook.test.ts` (10 tests: W-2-only gross/withholding/net, 1099-only with expenses on the 1099 side, mixed piles summed apart under one combined total with per-payer breakdowns, cents-exact fractional sums, form entries listed but never double-counted, honest empty state, period windows). Full suite 300/300 across 23 files; client + server typecheck clean. Live action + seal-chain verification recorded in the build report for this edit.

## Addendum — Pay-stub delivery: owners send stubs in-app; email is driver-optional (edit, 2026-10-27)

- **X'S ORDER, in his words**: "add where employers / owners can send their drivers their w2 paystub or 1099 stub prior to payday instead of email" — with his refinement governing the email part: "put it optional for driver to email himself or others his paystub." Locked posture: **sending is in-app only; the app NEVER auto-emails anyone.** The sealed record lives in STONEMILES. Email exists only as the DRIVER's own choice, from the driver's own mail app / share sheet (see below).
- **DATA (migration 0065)**: new `pay_stub_deliveries` table, starting EMPTY — sender username, recipient username, company id, kind (`w2` | `1099`), delivery type (`stub` = a paycheck stub | `form` = the year-end W-2/1099 form itself), pay date, tax year (forms), gross, federal/state withheld, other deductions, notes, optional stub file as a blob + SHA-256 fingerprint (the pay-entries pattern verbatim), status (`sent` → `accepted` | `flagged` | `withdrawn`), flag reason, resulting pay entry id on acceptance, sent/responded timestamps. `pay_entries` gains `source` (null = driver logged it by hand; `company` = company-sent and accepted) + `delivery_id` columns — additive only; no existing row or figure changes. The full data wipe clears deliveries and their blobs.
- **ROSTER SCOPING (server-side, the Owner Giveaway discipline)**: `sendPayStub` requires an Owner or Dispatch seat; the recipient must be a role-"driver" member of the SENDER's own company, validated against the company roster in pure functions in `server/src/payStub.ts` (`senderGate`, `eligibleRecipients`, `validateStubRecipient`). A driver from another company is refused BY NAME ("@x is not a driver in your company"); the owner/dispatch named as recipient is refused by name; a driver seat can never send; a solo account gets the create-a-fleet path, never a dead end. `listSentStubs` is the sender company's view (statuses + roster); `listReceivedStubs` is the driver's own inbox (withdrawn deliveries leave the inbox; sent/accepted/flagged stay as the driver's record). `getPayStubFile` serves the stub file only to the recipient or the sender company's owner/dispatch — anyone else reads it as not existing.
- **ACCEPT-ONCE RULE**: accept runs through the EXISTING pay-entry shape via the pure mapping `deliveryToPayEntry` (kind + `isForm` from the delivery type; payer = the company name; the accepted entry points at the delivery's own sealed stub blob — one blob, fingerprinted once at send time; `deletePayEntry` no longer deletes a blob a delivery still references). The accept first CLAIMS the delivery with one conditional update (`sent` → `accepted`), so a double-tap/retry/second accept updates nothing and is refused with "already in your Pay Book — it filed once and won't be counted twice"; totals then count the entry by the stub's PAY DATE under the unchanged `getPayBook` logic. A **form** delivery accepts into a form entry — excluded from the pay totals exactly like a hand-logged January form. **Flag** (recipient only, only while `sent`, short reason required) creates NO entry — it is the mismatch path; the sender's sent list shows the flag + reason. **Withdraw** is sender-only and only while still `sent` — never after accept/flag. Seal chain events: `pay_stub_sent` / `pay_stub_accepted` (plus the accepted entry's own `pay_entry_logged`, payload carrying `source: "company"` + delivery id) / `pay_stub_flagged` / `pay_stub_withdrawn`.
- **UI**: Owner Console gains a **Pay Stubs** section (`PayStubSendPanel`): pick a driver (own roster only), W-2/1099 toggle, stub-or-form toggle, pay date, gross + withholding + other deductions (W-2 only), tax year (forms), note, stub photo attach, Send — plus the sent list with status chips (Sent / Accepted / Flagged + the driver's reason / Withdrawn) and a Withdraw control while a stub is still `sent`. Driver side: the Pay Book gains a **"From your company"** inbox — each delivery shows sender company + sender, kind, stub-or-form, pay date, gross, the stub file (view/download), and Accept / Flag controls; accepted stubs appear in the pay-entries list tagged **Company-sent**. Copy rule held everywhere: "Sent in STONEMILES, not by email — the sealed record lives here," and a stub may be sent before payday with totals following the stub's pay date.
- **DRIVER-OPTIONAL EMAIL/SHARE (the honest build)**: the app has NO server email rails and does not pretend otherwise. On each received stub AND each Pay Book entry, "Email / share this stub" (`StubShareButton`) builds the SAME plain-text single-stub summary the exports use — one stub only, one pile only (W-2 and 1099 paperwork stays separate per x's filing rule) — then, behind the existing Security Pack 2 export step-up: (a) the device share sheet where available, with the stub FILE attached where the platform supports file sharing; (b) fallback: download the stub copy + summary, then open a `mailto:` draft with the summary as the body and the recipient LEFT BLANK (never pre-filled from company data). Copy states it plainly wherever the button sits: "Emailing is your choice — it opens your own mail app. STONEMILES does not send the email and does not store who you send it to." Nothing is ever sent automatically.
- **UNCHANGED**: Pay Book totals/exports math, the manual log-pay flow, Money Truth, IFTA, rewards, pricing. No internal percentages/splits in any copy. Honest empty states on both sides ("Nothing from your company yet", "No stubs sent yet").
- **HONEST LIMITS**: stub files attach as JPEG/PNG photos (the pay-entry pattern); a PDF stub's numbers can be sent while the PDF itself stays in the paperwork vault — stated in the send form. Deliveries are records between company seats on the sealed chain; no payroll system import or employer feed exists.
- **VERIFICATION**: new `server/src/payStub.test.ts` (role gates — driver cannot send, dispatch can, solo gets the fleet path; roster scoping — cross-company send refused by name, owner/dispatch as recipient refused by name, eligible list is drivers only; accept-once — second accept refused with the no-double-count line; flag path allowed only from `sent`; withdraw rules — sender-only, only while `sent`; mapping — stub files as a paycheck entry, form files as a form entry with tax year defaulted, and an accepted form delivery excluded from totals via `summarizePayBook` while an accepted stub counts in its pile). Full suite + typecheck + live action verification (send → accept → totals, seal chain intact) recorded in the build report for this edit.
- **OWNERSHIP FIX (same edit, audit repair)**: `pay_entries` gains an owner `username` (migration 0066; stamped by `logPayEntry` and by accept with the recipient; pre-existing rows backfilled from the device profile). `getPayEntryStubFile` — the one action that hands out a stub file URL for a Pay Book entry — now issues the URL only when the entry belongs to the caller (stamped owner matches, or the linked delivery's recipient matches), failing closed otherwise — the same gate `getPayStubFile` applies to deliveries. Another driver's stub photo is never served.

## Addendum — Pay Book per diem: non-taxable, paid separately (edit, 2026-10-27)

- **BACKGROUND (x-approved, from real-world stub-format testing)**: on per-mile W-2 pay stubs the printed "Gross Earnings" is commonly the TAXABLE gross AFTER a fixed per diem has been taken out, and that per diem is paid to the driver on a separate check — non-taxable, never taxed, never deducted again. A driver who logged only the printed gross understated real earnings and the Pay Book stopped reconciling against miles × rate. This field fixes that. (Testing used the owner's own stubs privately; NO figures from them appear anywhere in the app, tests, or this plan — the test fixture is synthetic.)
- **SEMANTICS (locked)**: taxable gross EXCLUDES per diem — every filing figure (pile gross subtotals, combined taxable tracking gross, both packets' filing totals) is computed exactly as before, with per diem never added in. Per diem is non-taxable and paid separately: it is summed PER PILE (W-2 and 1099 separately, never merged into a filing figure) as `perDiemTotal`, and feeds only "total received" per pile = (taxable gross − federal − state − other deductions) + per diem. The combined TRACKING view of real earnings (`combinedPerDiem`, `combinedTotalReceived`, labelled "for keeping up, not a filing number") includes per diem; every filing figure stays taxable-only.
- **DATA (migration 0067)**: `pay_entries.per_diem` and `pay_stub_deliveries.per_diem`, REAL NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 — existing rows read as 0 and no existing figure changes. Applies to pay entries of kind `w2` and `1099`. Year-end FORM entries are excluded: `logPayEntry` and `sendPayStub` store 0 for forms, the delivery→entry mapping zeroes a form's per diem, and `summarizePayBook` sums per diem from non-form checks only — a form's per diem is ignored and never counted anywhere.
- **ACTIONS**: `logPayEntry` accepts optional `perDiem` (non-negative, same money-field bounds; cents-exactness comes from the integer-cents summary math). `getPayBook` returns `perDiemTotal` + `totalReceived` per pile (the 1099 pile also returns its withheld/other sums so its total-received formula is the same shape), per-payer `perDiem`, and the combined per-diem/total-received tracking fields; its note now states the per-diem rule. `listPayEntries` returns `perDiem` per entry. Seal payloads for `pay_entry_logged` / `pay_entry_deleted` carry `perDiem`. Deleting an entry recomputes per-diem totals automatically (the summary re-reads the table).
- **TAX PACKETS**: filing gross in BOTH packets stays taxable gross ONLY. Each packet appends, when the pile has per diem in the period, the labelled line "Per diem (non-taxable, paid separately) — not included in filing gross: $X" (constant `PER_DIEM_PACKET_LABEL` in `server/src/payBook.ts`, mirrored in the client) plus a total-received line; per-check and per-payer lines show per diem where present. Per diem is never added into any filing total in either packet.
- **STUB DELIVERY**: `sendPayStub` gains the same optional `perDiem` (form deliveries store 0); on Accept, `deliveryToPayEntry` carries it intact onto the created Pay Book entry under the existing accept-once claim — a second accept is refused, so the per diem can never be counted twice. Flag and withdraw behavior unchanged; seal payloads for send/accept carry `perDiem`.
- **UI**: the Log pay form gains "Per diem (non-taxable — paid separately)" (hidden for year-end forms, with the phone-width `min-w-0` grid discipline kept); entry rows show a per-diem + total-received line only when per diem > 0; the summary cards show per-diem total and total-received lines per pile when present, and the combined tracking card shows the combined per-diem/total-received line; the single-stub share text gains the per-diem and total-received lines. The Owner Console Pay Stubs send form gains the same field with the same label (hidden for form deliveries); the driver inbox and the sent list show the per-diem amount when present.
- **UNCHANGED**: pricing, rewards/points, Money Truth math, IFTA, trip-sheet math, and every total outside the Pay Book scope above.
- **VERIFICATION**: new tests in `server/src/payBook.test.ts` (synthetic per-mile fixture — 2,000 mi × $0.55 + $50 extras − $280 per diem = $870 taxable gross: taxable subtotals unchanged by per diem; per-pile per-diem sums never mixed; total received exact to the cent incl. fractional cents; both packets exclude per diem from filing gross but list the labelled line; form entries never count per diem; delete recomputes per-diem totals) and `server/src/payStub.test.ts` (a delivery carrying per diem accepts into an entry with it intact, exactly once; a form delivery's per diem is zeroed). Full suite + typecheck + seal-chain verification recorded in the build report for this edit.

## Addendum — Pay Book findability (edit, 2026-10-27)

- **BACKGROUND (x's report: "I'm not seeing it. App kinda confusing to use. I gotta ask Meca to take me everywhere")**: the Pay Book existed and worked, but was unreachable in practice — a closed accordion card below the Wallet and Money panels on the Money tab; the Log pay form sat at the BOTTOM of the open panel with per diem as its last field; the per-diem field vanished with no explanation when the year-end-form box was ticked; Ask Meca had NO Pay Book destination (pay questions resolved to Wallet or Settings); the default period was "Year", hiding older paychecks from the totals; and Owner Console "Pay Stubs" (the send desk) vs "Pay Book" (the driver's own pay) read as the same thing.
- **FIXES (client-only; no schema, no action, no money-math change)**:
  - **Ask Meca destination `pay_book`** (App.tsx): added to `MecaDestId` + `MECA_DEST_INFO` (label "Money → Pay Book", blurb names the real path and disambiguates Send Pay Stubs); `resolveMecaDest` routes pay book / pay stub / paycheck / log pay / per diem / W-2 / 1099 / year-end form questions to it (placed before the wallet/settings rules so "my pay" can't steal it); `goMecaDest` jumps to the Money tab, opens the book, and scrolls to the Log pay form (`#pay-book-log`); the destination also rides the Meca context snapshot's `appDestinations` line so the model can suggest it, gated through the same `buildMecaNav` path as every other destination. No gate: every account has its own Pay Book.
  - **Money tab shortcut**: a neon "📒 Pay Book" button directly under the Settlement Checker button (same one-tap pattern), plus a Pay Book row in the More list — both perform the same open-and-scroll jump.
  - **Panel restructure** (`PayBookPanel`, NextLevel.tsx): the Log pay form now sits at the TOP of the open panel (anchor `pay-book-log`); "From your company" follows; totals/tax packets below; the logged-entries list stays last under a new "Logged pay — newest first" heading. The panel takes an `openSignal` prop (nonce bumped by every jump) and controls its `Section` open state — jumps open it instead of landing on a closed card. `Section` gained optional controlled `open`/`onOpenChange` props (all other panels unchanged, uncontrolled).
  - **Default period is now "All time"** so a driver's older paychecks show in the totals the moment the book opens; the period tabs still narrow on demand.
  - **Per diem no longer vanishes silently**: in year-end-form mode both the Log pay form and the owner send form now show a plain explanation where the field would be (forms never carry per diem; it is logged per paycheck / sent with each stub). Form entries still store per diem 0 — semantics unchanged.
  - **Naming**: Owner Console section renamed "Pay Stubs" → **"Send Pay Stubs"** (nav label, page heading, restricted-view heading), with a lead line stating this is the send desk and your own pay lives in the Pay Book; the Pay Book intro carries the reciprocal pointer.
- **UNCHANGED**: every Pay Book total, packet, accept/flag/withdraw rule, and the per-diem semantics from the 2026-10-27 per-diem addendum. No server code touched.

## Addendum — Pay Book 1099 model: taxes not applicable + itemized stub deductions (edit, 2026-10-27)

- **X'S MODEL, in his words**: "1099 don't have tax deductible and most are owner expenses and Owen responsibility. Broker just pays owner" — and the correction that completes it: "Owner or 1099 some times have deduction on thier sub. Like fuel truck payment etc." Locked rules: (1) NO tax is ever withheld from 1099 pay — the federal/state fields on the 1099 side are NOT APPLICABLE, not zero-by-omission; the owner settles their own taxes at filing time. (2) A 1099 settlement stub CAN carry named deductions that came off before the owner was paid — fuel, truck payment, insurance, escrow, quickpay fee, service fee — and those belong INSIDE the pay entry, line by line. (3) Expenses the owner pays out of pocket stay in the expense tracker; the packet nets stub deductions once and out-of-pocket expenses once — never the same dollar twice.
- **DATA (migration 0068)**: `pay_entries.deduction_lines` + `pay_stub_deliveries.deduction_lines`, TEXT nullable holding a JSON array of `{label, amount}` — additive only; existing rows (incl. the user's W-2 test entries) read as no lines and no figure changes. Pure helpers in `server/src/payBook.ts`: `normalizeDeductionLines` (trimmed labels, positive cents-rounded amounts, ≤30 lines), `sumDeductionLines` (cents-exact; the sum IS the entry's `otherDeductions` for 1099 — no second deductions number), `parseDeductionLines` (missing/unreadable → no lines), `aggregateDeductionLines` (per-label totals across a pile, case-insensitive, largest first); typical labels in `TYPICAL_DEDUCTION_LABELS`.
- **ACTIONS**: `logPayEntry` and `sendPayStub` accept `deductionLines`; a shared resolver (`resolveDeductionFigures`) enforces the model server-side — non-form 1099: lines normalized, `otherDeductions` = their sum, federal/state stored 0; W-2: figures pass through untouched, no lines; forms: printed figures kept, no lines (a form's gross is as printed). `deliveryToPayEntry` carries a 1099 stub's lines intact onto the filed entry under the existing accept-once claim, and zeroes a 1099 stub's withholding on filing. `listPayEntries`, `listSentStubs`/`listReceivedStubs` (via `stubDeliveryView`), and `getPayBook` (1099 pile gains `deductionLines` aggregate) all return the lines; seal payloads for `pay_entry_logged` / `pay_stub_sent` carry them.
- **SUMMARY MATH**: `summarizePayBook`'s 1099 pile gains the aggregated `deductionLines`; `net1099` is now gross − stub deductions (once) − logged expenses − logged fuel — for every pre-existing entry deductions were 0 on the 1099 side (the old UI forced it), so no existing figure moves. W-2 math, per-diem handling, combined tracking total, and form exclusion are unchanged.
- **UI**: Log pay on the 1099 side now shows the **"Taxes — not applicable on 1099 pay"** note in place of the tax fields (plain words: the broker just pays the owner; the owner settles taxes at filing) plus the **itemized deduction editor** (add/remove label + amount rows, pick-or-type labels with the six typical stub labels as suggestions, live deductions total, and the line separating stub deductions from out-of-pocket expenses). The Owner Console **Send Pay Stubs** form carries the identical note + editor for a 1099 stub; the driver's inbox, the sent list, and the logged-entries list each render the lines read-only (`DeductionLinesList`). The single-stub share text lists the lines + total + paid-to-owner for 1099. The **1099 packet** now prints each payment's stub deductions, an aggregated "DEDUCTIONS ON THE STUBS (itemized)" section with the total, relabels the expenses section as out-of-pocket, and its net line reads gross − stub deductions − expenses − fuel. The 1099 totals card shows the deductions total + lines + total received when deductions exist.
- **UNCHANGED**: W-2 side of both forms (withholding fields + lump other deductions + per diem exactly as before), per-diem semantics everywhere, accept/flag/withdraw, roster scoping, filing separation.
- **VERIFICATION**: new tests in `server/src/payBook.test.ts` (normalize/sum/parse round-trip; aggregation merges label case variants; net1099 nets stub deductions once + expenses once + fuel once; a lineless 1099 entry keeps the old net) and `server/src/payStub.test.ts` (a 1099 stub's lines file intact with sum as the entry total; a 1099 stub never files withholding even if the delivery row carries some; W-2 keeps its lump figure and no lines; a form ignores lines).

## Addendum — speed-limit fix + ETA in plain sight (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report**: screenshot on I-10 at 62 mph with the limit box showing "––" in both readouts (map overlay + Drive panel), plus the ask that ETA sit in plain sight while driving.
- **Diagnosis (root cause)**: `querySpeedLimit` asked Overpass for `way(around:160,…)[highway][maxspeed]; out tags 6;` and took the FIRST element whose tag parsed. Overpass returns elements in storage order, not nearest-first, and the 6-element cap could exclude the truck's own segment entirely — so the readout got a frontage-road/ramp limit or nothing for whole stretches. No geometry was fetched, so "nearest road" was never computed; a failed lookup also blanked the display instead of holding the last known limit.
- **Fix**: new pure module `client/src/speedLimit.ts` (unit-tested, `speedLimit.test.ts`, incl. the I-10 regression shape). The lookup now fetches nearby highway ways WITH geometry (`out tags geom 80`, radius 200 m, widening once to 500 m) and picks the posted limit of the way whose LINE is nearest the truck (`pickNearestLimit`, point-to-segment distance, ≤120 m claim range at the first radius). Tag parsing also covers `maxspeed:forward/backward/lanes`; `maxspeed:type` is never read as a limit. Fallback ladder: posted (nearest way) → last posted limit while fresh (<3 min, <½ mi, labelled "LAST KNOWN") → labelled interstate estimate (~70, motorway class only, marked EST / "~", display-only — it never feeds the overspeed alert, which trusts posted/last-posted only) → honest "no data". A sequence guard stops a slow earlier lookup overwriting a newer one. Both readouts (map overlay box, Drive panel speed row) show the source label.
- **ETA in plain sight**: the shared ETA computation is now traffic-aware (TomTom whole-route delay scaled to the remaining stretch, Gold+ when live) and feeds three surfaces: a glass ETA pill on the map itself while a route runs in Drive mode (arrival clock + time left + miles left), a large ETA row in the Drive-mode panel (arrival clock in 6xl condensed, time left, miles left, destination), and the enlarged ETA in the Routes guidance strip ("with traffic" noted when the delay is included). No route → no ETA is shown anywhere; nothing is estimated without a route.

## Addendum — Map engine swap (MapLibre) + Explore/Go (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Direction (x, 2026-10-27)**: "Swap the map engine in the Explore/Go pass", from the spec sheet `~/workspace/goals/trucker-gps-app/files/hammer-map-specs-2026-10-27.md` (Hammer *functional* structure only — no Hammer art, branding, icons, or code; STONEMILES design system throughout).
- **Engine**: Leaflet + raster OSM picture tiles retired. The map is now MapLibre GL JS 4.7.1 (bundled npm package, no CDN script, no API key) rendering vector tiles: streets = OpenFreeMap Liberty style at https://tiles.openfreemap.org/styles/liberty (verified live 2026-10-27; source = OpenStreetMap vector data via OpenMapTiles), restyled at runtime into the design system (deep green-black ground, muted green roads, 3D building extrusions from the style's `building-3d` layer where the data carries heights). Satellite/Hybrid keep the same Esri World Imagery + Esri reference overlays as before, as raster sources inside the vector engine. The adapter lives in `client/src/mapEngine.ts` behind the small window.L surface the app already spoke; route lines are GeoJSON layers re-added after style swaps, markers are DOM markers (screen-upright at any bearing/pitch), tooltips are popups. The rotating-wrapper heading-up hack is retired — bearing/pitch are native camera controls.
- **Explore/Go**: the Drive map has two modes persisted per device (`tgps-map-mode`), named "Explore" and "Go" identically in the mode switch, Ask Meca destinations (`explore`, `go` — Meca routes to the mode and flips the map full-screen), and help copy. EXPLORE: full-map-first, top search bar (geocodeSearch) with voice (Web Speech API where the browser offers it, honest banner where it doesn't), category chips Truck Stops · Weigh Stations · Fuel · Parking · Rest querying Overpass around the truck (~15 mi, nearest first, real pins only — an empty result says so), restriction diamonds for bridge/weight/no-truck hazards, right rail (Layers · Places · Compass · Center · Zoom), saved-places panel, and a selected-place card with the one big neon Route button (routes via routeToPoint, then flips to Go). GO: pitch 55 heading-up camera (drive mode auto-flips to Go), speed bubble bottom-left with the posted limit as a badge ON the bubble (overspeed states the bubble; LAST KNOWN / ~EST fallback labels ride under it), top turn+ETA strip (maneuver arrow, distance + time to next turn, arrival clock + time/miles left), End route one tap bottom-right.
- **Carried over unchanged**: geometry-based speed-limit lookup + labelled fallback ladder (speedLimit.ts, est never feeds overspeed), all three ETA surfaces (Go strip, non-Go map pill, Drive panel row + route strip), Layers panel choices + per-account map prefs (labels toggle now works on every style — vector labels are separate layers; mapPrefs.ts logic unchanged), scan-ahead, hazard/fuel/intel/parking layers, route lines, the fleet map in the Owner Console (same engine), all data/money/rewards/gating/seals, and the bottom nav (Today/Drive/Loads/Money/More).

## Addendum — GPS "not working" + half-screen map fix (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report**: screenshot from the cab (Drive tab, route active): the speed bubble and LIMIT badge both read "––", the map renders as a dark ~46dvh strip with no truck marker visible, the right rail overlaps the "Full screen" button, and the chip claims "GPS LIVE". Verbatim: "GPS not working and I hate how it's half a screen instead of full screen".
- **Diagnosis (from the screenshot + source)**: (1) `gpsOn` was set the moment `watchPosition` was *registered*, and the chip said LIVE from then on — even with zero fixes ever delivered. No code path restarted a watch that stalled while the app stayed open (recovery existed only on foreground return). (2) The fix filter only produced a speed sample from a platform-reported speed or credited movement; platforms that report no speed at a standstill (iOS null/−1) left `speedMph` null forever while parked — position live, speed "––", reading as dead GPS. (3) The map's normal size on Drive was `h-[46dvh]`/`h-[38dvh]` — literally half a screen — with the Drive panels stacked under it.
- **Fixes**: the chip now says **GPS SEEKING** (pulsing dot) until the first accepted fix, LIVE only with a position (WEAK unchanged); a "Waiting for a GPS fix" pill sits on the map itself while there is no position; a 20 s **watchdog** restarts a wanted watch that has delivered no fix callback for 45 s while the app is open (same fresh-start discipline as the visibility path: clearWatch + filter reset + re-anchor), and `startGps` stamps the stall baseline at watch start so a never-delivering watch is restartable; a permission denial is now explained even on automatic starts. In `gpsFilter.ts`, stationary evidence (a fix inside the parked deadband, or inside the noise floor while rolling) now yields a **0 mph** speed sample when the platform reports none — parked reads 0, never "––" (unit test added; distance integrity unchanged — standstill still credits exactly zero miles). **Drive map is full screen**: on the Drive tab the map fills all space between the top bar and the bottom dock (`flex-1`), and the Drive panels moved into a bottom sheet over the map opened from a labelled "Drive panel — route · hours · Fuel Coach · places" bar (routes/stops keep their strip; the existing fixed full-screen mode is unchanged). No data, money, or server changes.
- **Honest limit**: the sandbox has no GPS and no live tiles on a real device — filter logic is unit-proven and the layout is probe-verified, but the fix stream on the driver's phone (webview geolocation delivery) can only be confirmed on the next drive; the SEEKING/watchdog states are built to surface and self-heal exactly that failure if it recurs.

## Addendum — "GPS still blank": the map host was 0px tall (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report**: after the GPS/full-screen fix shipped, the Drive map STILL rendered as a pure black panel in both Explore and Go — no roads, no labels, no truck marker, no route line — while the GPS chip read LIVE and the parked speed read 0. Verbatim: "GPS still blank" (screenshot 4:52 PM, Go mode).
- **Diagnosis (proven in a real browser against the shipped bundle, not inferred)**: MapLibre's bundled stylesheet (`maplibre-gl.css`, imported by `mapEngine.ts`) is UNLAYERED CSS; Tailwind v4 emits utilities into cascade layers, and unlayered rules beat layered ones — so `.maplibregl-map { position: relative }` silently overrode the map host's Tailwind `absolute` class. The host computed to `position: relative; height: 0px` (measured live: host 390×0 inside a 390×629 wrapper, canvas stuck at MapLibre's 390×300 default, truck marker present in the DOM but clipped inside the 0px overflow-hidden box). Everything inside the map — tiles, labels, route line, markers — has been clipped away since the engine swap, on every browser; the app overlays are siblings of the host, which is why the rest of the screen looked normal. The tile source was never the problem: OpenFreeMap style/tilejson/tiles/glyphs all verified live (HTTP 200, CORS *) during diagnosis, as was WebGL2.
- **Fix**: the two map hosts (Drive `#trucker-map`, Owner Console Fleet Map) now carry inline `style={{ position: "absolute", inset: 0 }}` — inline styles outrank every stylesheet — plus a `#trucker-map` ID rule in theme.css as a second belt, and load-bearing comments at both sites so the sizing is never "cleaned up" back to class-only. No engine, source, data, or behavior change.
- **Verification**: headless Chromium + the real built bundle + real OpenFreeMap tiles (node-proxied) + a GPS fix in Theodore, AL: BEFORE the patch the host measured 390×0 / canvas 390×300; applying exactly the patch's inline style → host 390×629, canvas 390×629, and the screenshot shows I-10 with its shield, CR 28, local roads and landcover in the dark theme with the gold truck marker centered on the fix. Typecheck clean; 54/54 client tests pass.

## Addendum — true-color map, long-haul routing, route chooser prompt (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report** (verbatim + 3m17s screen recording): "Not routing and use map true color. That's too much green n neon green. The clip shows all defects. When you input you route. App should prompt 2-3 of best routes to choose from instead of haven't to stroll and look for the route." The recording shows: the dark green-black map with muted-green roads and piled labels; a search for Denver (1,137 mi) whose Route tap produced NO route, no progress, and no error on the map (Go mode sat on "pick a place in Explore"); and the Routes tab's routing header + multi-stop planner with the route options nowhere in sight without scrolling.
- **Diagnosis (all proven live, not inferred)**:
  1. **Long hauls could never route**: the public Valhalla server (valhalla1.openstreetmap.de) rejects any request whose path exceeds 1,500 km — `{"error_code":154,"error":"Path distance exceeds the max distance limit: 1500000 meters"}` — verified with curl for Theodore AL → Denver CO under both truck and auto costing, and for a via-waypoint variant (the cap is on total path). The driver's Denver run (1,137 mi straight-line) was exactly this failure. Short routes work (verified: Theodore → Mobile, 15.9 mi, HTTP 200).
  2. **Silent failure**: routing progress/error/options rendered ONLY on the Routes tab; the Drive/Explore map showed nothing when a plan was tapped from Explore.
  3. **Buried options**: on the Routes tab the option cards sat below the multi-stop planner section and the ChainLawPanel.
  4. **Green wash**: `applyStonemilesTheme` in mapEngine.ts repainted every Liberty layer (background #07090b, water dark teal, land dark green, roads muted green) and the selected route line was neon green #2ee6a8 with a teal glow.
- **Fixes**:
  - **True color**: the repaint is deleted (`applyTrueColorTheme` touches only the 3D-building tint, neutral gray). Liberty ships as-is — blue water, natural land, white/yellow roads — in both app themes. Map canvas backing is Liberty's land tone (#eceae4); the selected route is navigation blue #1a73e8 on a white casing, alternates slate #64748b; adapter default line/marker colors are the same blue.
  - **Sectioned long-haul routing**: when the direct Valhalla request fails on a >300 mi (straight-line) plan, `routeSectioned` rebuilds the route in hops of ≤900 km straight-line (each ≈1,100 km of road, under the cap — verified live: Theodore → Denver stitched = 1,463 mi / 24.2 h across 3 hops, matching the expected road distance), with recursive halving as a safety net; `buildRouteOption` now stitches multi-trip/multi-leg geometry + maneuvers (joint arrive/start pairs dropped) and sums miles/time/tolls. Two extra options backfill in the background (`backfillLongHaulVariants`): a toll-flip (or no-highways) sectioned run and an offset-corridor sectioned run, deduped at ≤1 mi. Sectioned failure falls through to the honest error, which now names the long-haul case.
  - **Route chooser prompt**: a fresh (non-reroute) plan opens a sheet over the map the moment planning starts — loading state ("Finding your best routes…", with the sections note), error state with the real message + Try again (re-runs the exact plan from `lastPlanRef`) + Close, then the 2–3 options as compact rows (miles · drive time · ETA clock · net · safety · toll/traffic flags; tap previews the line on the map; best pick marked PICKED, following the existing auto-pick) and one big START ROUTE button into guidance (which now also flips the map to Go). "Full comparison →" jumps to the Routes tab. The planner sheet's Show routes now stays on Drive under the chooser instead of jumping tabs. All plan entry points (Explore Route button, planner, saved places, recents, loads, multi-stop next leg) flow through planRoute, so all of them prompt. The chooser shows every returned option on every plan (the Routes tab keeps its existing plan-tier comparison gating — recorded tension: the owner's words ask for 2–3 prompted routes, and the prompt honors that; plan-page copy still describes Basic as 1-route comparison on that tab).
  - **Routes tab order**: option cards (+ the Basic locked note) now sit directly under the "Your route — ready to start" card and the auto-pick note; multi-stop planner and ChainLawPanel moved below them, above Fuel brands/Directions (nothing removed).
- **Verification**: Valhalla cap + sectioned stitch proven with live curl/Python against the real server (above). Client typecheck clean; 54/54 client tests pass. Live on-device proof (tiles painting true-color, a >932 mi route completing) remains the driver's next drive, as with every map fix in this sandbox.

## Addendum — Resume route + traffic lines on the route (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report**: "Resume route doesn't work. And traffic lines doesn't appear like slow or traffic jam heavy traffic like my apple map show" (with an Apple Maps screenshot: blue route with orange slow + red heavy stretches; and the app's own Go screenshot: solid blue line to Biloxi, no colors).
- **Resume root cause**: the trip SHEET had an on-device snapshot (tgps-open-trip) but the ROUTE had none — destination/options/guidance were React state only, wiped by any app close/reload; the Today "Trip paused" card also only switched tabs instead of resuming. **Fix** (`client/src/routePersistence.ts`, unit-tested): the live route is snapshotted to localStorage `tgps-active-route-v1` on every route/option/guidance change (geometry rounded to 5 dp; stale traffic overlays stripped — congestion is never resurrected from storage; quota fallback keeps just the picked route). Resume restores destination, all options' geometry + maneuvers, picked option, toll choice, and guidance instantly from stored geometry (offline-safe, no re-plan), then weather/traffic/fuel refresh in the background. Surfaces: Go empty-state card, Drive-panel section, Today card; resuming the trip restores the route with it; the Today trip card now actually calls resumeTrip; End route clears the snapshot.
- **Traffic lines**: TomTom was already the live traffic source (see Live traffic section) but only ever produced TEXT notes — the line was never drawn. Now `getRouteTraffic` also returns per-flow-point speed ratios and on-route incident GEOMETRY (server `tomtom.ts` incident coords, ≤48 pts); `client/src/trafficSegments.ts` (unit-tested) builds orange (slow: flow ratio 0.55–0.85, or magnitude 2 / delay ≥5 min) and red (heavy: ratio <0.55, magnitude ≥3, delay ≥15 min, or road closure) stretches from the route's OWN coords, painted over the blue line in the layer effect; heavy wins overlaps, off-route incidents paint nothing, no data → plain blue, nothing invented. A legend pill on the map states the live state (flowing/slow/heavy + TomTom as-of time, or unavailable / Gold-gated / switched-off). Traffic re-fetches every 5 min while a route runs (server cache is also 5 min). The Layers panel switch is now labelled "Traffic" and gates the colors AND the notes (fetching + scan-ahead warnings never gated). Verified live through the app's own action on the driver's own corridor (Theodore → Biloxi): available, flow ratios returned, 9 on-route incidents incl. road closures and a magnitude-3 jam at +14.5 min.

## Addendum — route chooser see-the-route fix (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report**: "when you choose route, you can't see which route you're actually choosing because the route pick is blocking it" (with screenshots: the chooser sheet covered ~3/4 of the map and the map's symmetric fit centered the picked line behind it).
- **Fix (client-only; no schema, no new source)**: (1) The chooser is now a compact bottom sheet (max ~330px / 54% of the map, tighter rows) instead of max 460px / 74%. (2) The route-lines effect frames the picked route with asymmetric fit padding that reserves the sheet's height at the bottom and the turn strip at the top, so the blue line draws in the clear map above the sheet; chooser shape (open / peek / closed) is part of the fit key so each transition re-frames once. The MapLibre adapter's `fitBounds` (mapEngine.ts + globals.d.ts) now accepts Leaflet-shaped `paddingTopLeft`/`paddingBottomRight` alongside the symmetric `padding`. (3) A "👁 See route" button drops the sheet to a slim peek bar (picked route summary + Start) for a full-map look; "▴ Routes" pops it back. (4) While the chooser is up, each unpicked option gets a numbered pin on the map at the first point its line leaves the picked route (>0.35 mi), matching its chooser row number; tapping the pin previews that route as the blue line. Pins clear with the chooser.

## Addendum — "behind the End route" + tap-a-route-on-the-map (edit, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report** (verbatim, with map screenshots + a zoomed crop of the bottom bar): "What's this behind the end route? When you tap see route on the map, route selection disappears & you can't select the route on the map itself." The 7:00 PM screenshot shows the traffic legend pill running UNDER the red End route button and the Normal size button ("flowing / slow / he…" — "heavy" and part of the TomTom stamp cut off), and a full map with the blue pick + a grey alternate but no visible way to choose the alternate on the map.
- **Diagnosis (from the screenshots + source)**: (1) The traffic legend pill and the view-controls row (End route · Full/Normal size) were BOTH anchored `absolute bottom-2 right-2` at the same z — the buttons, later in the DOM, painted over the pill's right half. Two elements, one corner, guaranteed overlap whenever a route was live. (2) The "See route" peek bar carried only a summary of the PICKED route — the other options left the screen entirely — and the only on-map way to pick was the 26px numbered split pin: the route LINES themselves were dead, because the MapLibre adapter's polylines are GeoJSON layers with no click surface at all (`MLPolylineImpl` had no `on()`; the map's `on()` served dragstart only).
- **Fixes (client-only; no schema, no new source)**: (1) The legend pill now rides at `bottom-[46px] right-2`, directly above the End route / map-size row — one bottom-right stack, nothing behind anything; its content, live states, and Gold/off/unavailable variants are unchanged. (2) Tappable lines in the engine (`mapEngine.ts` + `globals.d.ts`): `MLPolylineImpl.on("click")` registers the line with the map; a native MapLibre click (clickTolerance already swallows pans/drags) is hit-tested in screen pixels against every registered line — nearest line within a 26px thumb reach fires, long-haul geometry strided to ≤600 segments per test. DOM marker taps now stopPropagation so a pin tap never double-fires through to the line beneath it; registrations die with the layer's `remove()`. While the chooser is up (open OR peeked), each unpicked route's grey line calls the same `selectRoute` as its row and pin; once guidance starts, lines return to display-only. (3) The peek bar keeps the selection on screen: a second row with "▴ Routes" plus a numbered chip per option (number + miles, picked chip filled blue — same numbers as the pins and rows), and the peek fit padding grows to match the taller bar (bottom reserve 92→138px). Split pins grow 26→30px. Sheet helper copy now says "tap a pin or a grey line to preview that route".
- **Verification**: client typecheck clean; 64/64 client unit tests pass. On-device proof (thumb-tapping a grey line at arm's length in the cab, legend read at a glance) remains the driver's next run, as with every map fix in this sandbox — the hit-test math is plain geometry over the same projected coords the engine already draws from.

## Addendum — route disappeared on base-style switch (fix, 2026-10-27)
- **Driver report**: "When selecting streets, satellite or hybrid the route disappears" (screenshot: guidance banner live, 46.1 mi left, truck + destination markers present, NO blue route line on the map).
- **Root cause (verified against the bundled maplibre-gl 4.7.1 source, not guessed)**: `MLMapImpl.setBaseStyle` called `map.setStyle(...)` with MapLibre's default `diff: true`. The style differ serializes the CURRENT style — which includes every runtime-added overlay source/layer (route casing, route line, traffic segments, flow dashes) — and diffs it against the target base style, emitting `removeLayer`/`removeSource` for all of them. The diff path (`Style.setState` → `Map._updateDiff`) never fires `style.load`, which is the only event the adapter's restore hook listens to, so the overlays were never re-added. DOM markers are not style layers, so pins/truck survived — exactly the screenshot. The route only reappeared if the React layer effect happened to re-run and recreate the polylines.
- **Fix (client/src/mapEngine.ts only)**: both `setStyle` calls in `setBaseStyle` now pass `{ diff: false }` — a full style rebuild, which fires `style.load`, whose handler re-adds every registered overlay in registration order on top of the new base. `addOverlayToStyle` now restores partial states (source without layer, layer without source) instead of early-returning when a source exists. `MLPolylineImpl.setLatLngs` now also updates the registered overlay def, so a geometry change survives a style swap instead of resurrecting the old line. No routing, data, or server changes; no new source.
