## Addendum — four field fixes (edit, 2026-10-28)

Driver report (with screenshots, 2:31–2:43 PM CT): (1) "The speed limit still isn't working" — LIMIT "––" on I-10, on CR 28/Halls Mill Rd, and parked; an Explore Fuel tap in the same session answered "Couldn't load places from the map data — check your connection" with a good 5G signal. (2) "The pin place address in wrong spot but close" — destination pin for 4568 Halls Mill Road, Mobile, AL landed down the street from the building. (3) "When you end trip on the drive it still renders in today tab — you'll have to end on gps (drive) and on today." (4) "Prompt stay until you press ok. They should show then disappear within 5-10 sec. Therefore it's safe and handsfree."

- **Speed limit — root cause**: the 2026-10-27 rebuild fixed WHICH road's limit is picked (nearest way by geometry, `client/src/speedLimit.ts`, still unit-tested and unchanged) but every lookup still traveled from the phone's webview to the single public Overpass instance `overpass-api.de`. Verified from this host 2026-10-28: that instance answers EVERY request — POST or GET, any User-Agent, even `/api/status` — with HTTP 406 in ~1 s (mod_security IP rules); kumi.systems hung / returned a dispatcher 504; private.coffee and nchc.org.tw timed out; osm.ch answers fast but is a Switzerland-only extract (0 US ways). One sick/blocked host therefore killed the limit, the hazards, and the Explore places at once — exactly the joint failure in the screenshots.
- **Speed limit — fix (three independent paths, any one can answer)**: (a) NEW server action `getSpeedLimit` (`server/src/speedLimit.ts`): server-side Overpass across four full-planet instances with last-good-first ordering, a descriptive User-Agent, cheap maxspeed-only queries (200 m → 500 m), the same nearest-way math mirrored server-side, and a 3-min cache per ~110 m grid cell. NOT plan-gated (safety readout; Basic made the same lookup directly before). (b) When every Overpass instance fails server-side, the action map-matches the truck's recent fixes through the app's own Valhalla router (`trace_attributes`, shape sent by the client from a new ≤8-fix breadcrumb ref; edges scanned from the end = the current road; `speed_limit` is km/h → mph). Verified live 2026-10-28 through the built action on the driver's own ground: I-10 shape → `{available: true, limitMph: 70, highway: "motorway"}` (Valhalla returned speed_limit 113 km/h); Halls Mill Rd shape → `{available: true, limitMph: null, estMph: null}` — the road genuinely carries no tag and NO number is invented. (c) The client's direct `overpass()` helper now fails over across the same four instances (last-good-first, per-attempt timeout capped at 20 s) and `querySpeedLimit` runs server-first, direct-second, with cheaper maxspeed-only queries; the display ladder (posted → LAST KNOWN <3 min/<½ mi → ~70 EST motorway-only, never feeding overspeed → honest no-data) is unchanged and now shared via `applyPostedLimit`/`applyNoPostedLimit`/`applyLookupFailed`. The failover also repairs the other Overpass features (hazards, corridor fuel, Explore places) on the direct path.
- **Pin — root cause**: the geocode chain stopped at the first engine with any hit. For "4568 Halls Mill Road, Mobile, AL" (Covenant Academy's address — camobile.us / NCES), Nominatim free-text returns an INTERPOLATED point ~180 m southeast of the building (an OSM `place` way), while ArcGIS `findAddressCandidates` returns a `PointAddress` at score 100 ~40 m from the school's published location (30.61686, −88.149052). ArcGIS was in the chain but never reached. **Fix**: `GeoHit.exact` (set only for ArcGIS `Addr_type = "PointAddress"` with score ≥ 85); in `geocodeSearch`, house-number-shaped queries (`/^\d+\s+\S/`) now also ask ArcGIS and promote exact hits ahead of all others. Place searches keep the original chain order.
- **End — fix**: `endRoute` with a manual (non-silent) tap while a trip sheet is running now delegates to `stopTrip()` — one tap ends the route AND saves/closes the sheet, so nothing keeps rendering as "rolling" on Today (trip state is one shared state; it is now also ENDED once). Silent/programmatic route ends (auto-stop, reroute flows, stopTrip's own cleanup) are unchanged, and a manual End trip still ends the route as before. The old banner copy ("use End trip to close the sheet") is gone from the route-ended message.
- **Prompts — fix**: the global banner (the OK prompt in the screenshot) now auto-dismisses after **8 s** (driver's 5–10 s window) via an effect keyed on the banner value; OK still dismisses instantly and a new banner restarts the clock. The proximity-alert banner's self-clear drops from 20 s to **10 s** (its voice announcement is the hands-free channel). State-driven safety alerts (overspeed, GPS weak/error) are not timers and are untouched.
- **Verification**: build green; all 355 unit tests pass (`bun test client/src server/src`); `getSpeedLimit` invoked live against the built artifact for the I-10 and Halls Mill Rd shapes above. Client-side failover behavior on the phone's carrier network cannot be exercised from this host (the host itself is one of the blocked networks — which is the point of the layered paths).

## Addendum — Saved Pins 📍 (edit, 2026-10-28)
- **Owner ask** (verbatim): "Could you add a way you can save pin 📍" — confirmed design: save a pin anywhere a pin drops (searched address, long-press on the map, current location), name it (default = the address/place name), store it on the driver's own account, list it on the Drive/map screen, and from the list show it on the map / route to it / rename / delete. Ask Meca knows "Saved Pins".
- **Storage**: no new table or source — a pin IS a `saved_places` row (migration 0040), written through the existing `saveSavedPlace` / `listSavedPlaces` / `deleteSavedPlace` actions (name, addressLabel, lat/lon, created/updated; optional truck gate preserved for places saved through the address form). Server-side per-account rows: private, never shared with other users or companies, follow the account across sessions, wiped by the same delete-all as every driver record. Pins saved from search / long-press / current location use category "custom" and no gate.
- **Save entry points (client)**: (1) the Explore selected-place card gains "Save pin 📍" (or "📍 Saved" when a pin already sits on that spot); (2) Drive-panel search results gain a per-result "Save pin 📍"; (3) a long-press on the map (engine `contextmenu` — right-click on desktop, long-press on touch — plus a 650 ms pointer-hold fallback in App.tsx using the adapter's new `screenToLatLng`) drops a red pin whose card offers Save pin 📍 / Route / Clear; (4) "📍 Save pin — my location" in both the map Saved Pins panel and the Drive panel's Saved Pins section (GPS position required). Every save opens the same name sheet (input pre-filled with the place/address, editable) — nothing saves without the driver's name confirm.
- **List + actions**: the map right rail's places button is now a labeled "📌 Pins" pill opening the **Saved Pins 📍** panel on the map itself: each pin shows name, address, and saved date; tapping it flies the map to it and arms the focused-pin card; every pin offers Route (the normal `routeToPlace` flow, gate-aware), Rename (inline, saved through `saveSavedPlace` with the row's other fields preserved), and Delete. All saved pins also draw as 📍 markers on the map (focused = gold ring, tap = focus). The Drive panel section is renamed "Saved Pins 📍" with the same Show on map / Route / Rename-Edit / Delete actions plus the by-address form and gate editor unchanged underneath.
- **Ask Meca**: new destination `saved_pins` ("Saved Pins 📍") in the client resolver + destination info, gated like Drive (no lock), jump opens Drive with the Saved Pins panel open; the server `askMeca` navigate vocabulary and the client app-destinations snapshot line name it too.
- **Unchanged**: routing behavior, trips, trip sheets, and every other feature — pins only ever feed the existing route flow as a destination.

## Addendum — Giveaway verification + $STONER token liability in all disclaimers (edit, 2026-10-28)
- **Owner ask** (verbatim): "Also app to disclaimer that the team will have giveaways and all giveaway token will have verified CA and can be checked via announcements board on site or app. Word it properly please. We don't ask for seed phrase. Ask Meca for help if needed — Add to all disclaimers" plus follow-up: "We also need something to give use zero liability for token" with the polished token-liability text supplied by the parent agent.
- **Copy added (plain words, no new data source, no invented CA — no contract address is published in the app because none was supplied; the clause says where the verified CA WILL be checked)**: (1) Giveaways clause — team runs giveaways; every official giveaway token has a verified contract address (CA) published on the Announcements Board on the site and in the app; if not listed there it isn't ours; never ask for seed phrase/private key/password to enter or claim; if unsure, don't send/sign, ask Meca for help. (2) $STONER liability clause verbatim as supplied — price can rise/fall without warning and lose all value; nothing Stonervision says is financial advice or a promise of profit; participation is own decision/risk; to the fullest extent the law allows Stonervision is not liable for any loss connected with the token (price movement, acting on good-faith publication, fake imitating tokens); if you cannot afford to lose it, do not buy it.
- **Surfaces ("all disclaimers")**: DisclaimerSheet (two new full sections), Settings → Security & privacy plain-language list (Giveaways rule + $STONER risk bullets beside the Keys rule), WalletSection (new box beside Honest custody label), GiveawaySection (two new rule bullets), SwapSection footer box, DonateSheet (standing safety line extended + new Giveaways & $STONER box). Condensed versions on secondary surfaces point to / carry the same operative phrases as the full Disclaimer text.

## Addendum — Speed limit STILL blank on I-10: starvation fix (edit, 2026-10-29)

Driver report (screenshot, 2:23 PM CT): doing 79 mph on I-10 at Carol Plantation Rd, Mobile AL with a live route — LIMIT badge still "––". Telling detail: NO "NO LIMIT DATA" caption under the bubble, i.e. the readout never left the "loading" state; it was not reaching any answer, good or bad.

- **Root cause — lookup starvation, in two stacked parts**: (1) Server-side, `fetchSpeedLimit` ran its fast rung LAST: two sequential Overpass passes (each up to 4 instances × 8 s) had to fail before the Valhalla map-match ran. Re-measured from this host 2026-10-29: overpass-api.de 406/502 in ~1–2 s, kumi.systems took **20.5 s** for ONE small maxspeed query (past the 8 s per-attempt cap → aborted), private.coffee > 30 s, nchc closed the connection — a server answer took ~40 s when it came at all. (2) Client-side, `onPosition` re-fired a lookup whenever the truck moved > 0.08 mi OR 30 s passed — at highway speed 0.08 mi is ~4 s — and every new lookup bumped the sequence guard, so the previous lookup's answer was discarded as "stale" when it finally landed. Parked was starved too (30 s re-fire vs > 30 s latency). No answer could EVER land, while direct action invocations (no racing client) verified fine — which is why the 2026-10-28 verification passed and the cab still showed "––".
- **The data was never missing**: a Valhalla `trace_attributes` call for a shape along the exact screenshot stretch (I-10 at Carol Plantation Rd) answered in **1.7 s** with edges 89 → 113 → 113 km/h; the current edge is 113 km/h = **70 mph posted**.
- **Fix — server** (`server/src/speedLimit.ts`): Valhalla map-match now runs FIRST whenever the client sends a breadcrumb shape (≥ 2 fixes) and its answer (posted limit, or road class for the labelled EST rung) is final for that query — the edge's `speed_limit` is the same OSM maxspeed data, matched to the road actually driven, so the slow Overpass pass could only repeat it or tempt a neighbour road's number. The Overpass chain (4 instances, 200 m → 500 m maxspeed ways, class-only query for the EST rung, 3-min grid cache) remains as the fallback for no-shape / Valhalla-down queries. Cache trimming factored into `trimCache()`. Honesty rules unchanged: only a real OSM limit is ever "posted"; the ~70 EST stays motorway-only and display-only.
- **Fix — client** (`client/src/App.tsx` + `client/src/speedLimit.ts`): new pure throttle `speedLimitQueryDue` (unit-tested) — first fix due; then due at 30 s, or > 0.08 mi once 15 s have passed, or > 0.6 mi at once; movement alone inside 15 s NEVER re-fires. Plus an in-flight guard: at most one lookup runs at a time; a due fix during a lookup only arms `speedQueryPendingRef`, and exactly one follow-up for the newest position runs when the in-flight answer lands. The sequence guard remains as a backstop.
- **Verification**: all 361 unit tests pass (incl. 6 new `speedLimitQueryDue` regression tests: the old > 0.08 mi-in-5 s case is now NOT due). Built artifact invoked live: the exact screenshot shape (I-10 at Carol Plantation Rd) → `{available: true, limitMph: 70, estMph: null, highway: "motorway"}`; a Halls Mill Rd shape → `{available: true, limitMph: null, estMph: null, highway: "residential"}` — no number invented for the untagged road, same as before. Phone-in-cab confirmation is still the driver's retest, as always.

## Addendum — Shipper Portal v1 BUILT + Auto-Dispatch spec (paper only) (edit, 2026-10-29)
- **Owner asks**: "Spec shipper portal first" / "Spec auto-dispatch as an optional thing for owners and fleet owners. Spec shipper portal. We going to work on the portal today" — the portal locked spec lives at `~/workspace/goals/trucker-gps-app/files/shipper-portal-spec-2026-10-29.md`; this build implements it. Auto-dispatch stays SPEC-ONLY (below); no auto-dispatch code shipped.
- **New tables (migration 0069)**: `shipper_accounts` (name, email login identity, contact, logoInitials monogram — no image/logo blob in v1, linkedUsername, companyId = the RUGGS desk company, ownerUsername, status active|suspended, isDemo, shipper defaults incl. pickup/commodity/freight class/contact) and `shipper_loads` (full post-form fields, equipment flatbed|step_deck|rgn — step_deck maps to board open_deck on release, tarps, edgeProtectors, rate, rate-con blob key + SHA-256 + mime, signoff/offload booleans, status draft|sent|returned|released, boardLoadId, trk_ tracking token, review fields). **No fee, percentage, or payment column exists in either table by design** — the carrier-side dispatch fee can never leak shipper-side because the server's shipper shapes don't carry it.
- **Identity & wall**: staging is single-device with the local profile username standing in for the email-login account. The server resolves the caller's shipper account from `linkedUsername == myUsername(ctx)` — a client can never name another shipper; `getShipperPortal` returns only the caller's own loads; another shipper's load id answers exactly like a missing one. Desk side = Owner/Dispatch membership in the company the account belongs to, mirroring Pay Book roster scoping.
- **Draft rule (server-enforced)**: `saveShipperLoad` with send=true re-checks completeness server-side — rate, rate-con attachment, sign-off, offload confirmation. Incomplete loads stay draft and the action returns the plain `missing[]` list; the UI shows it verbatim. Draft/returned loads are editable/deletable by the shipper only; sent/released loads can't be edited shipper-side.
- **Review queue & release**: desk actions `returnShipperLoad` (note required in practice, shown to the shipper), `updateShipperLoadDesk` (desk edits a sent load), `releaseShipperLoad` — release re-verifies sent + complete, inserts a normal `boardLoads` row through the same fields `postBoardLoad` uses (the board row REFERENCES the portal's rate-con blob by key with the same SHA — a byte copy is impossible because blob URLs are document-relative and server-side fetch can't read them back, the vault-key lesson; sharing is safe because a released load can never be edited/deleted portal-side again, and `withdrawBoardLoad`'s blob-delete guard was extended to count portal loads as key-sharers; board notes assembled from ref #, freight class, pieces/dims, equipment, tarps, EDGE PROTECTORS REQUIRED, load contact, confirmations, shipper notes), mints the `trk_` token, and seals chain events `shipper_load_released` + `board_load_posted`. From there the existing claim → sealed POD → auto-invoice machinery runs unchanged. Chain events also seal account create/update (`shipper_account_created/updated`, refKind driver). If dispatch withdraws a released load from the board, the shipper's plain status honestly falls back to "With dispatch".
- **Plain status (derived live, never stored)**: draft → Draft; returned → Returned — needs fixes; sent → With dispatch; released + board open → On the board; board taken + driver load booked → Carrier assigned; picked_up without pickup seal → Picked up; picked_up + pickupSealedAt → In transit; POD sealed → Delivered.
- **Carrier phone — honest gap**: STONEMILES holds no carrier phone number anywhere in its data, so the server returns `carrierPhone: null` and the shipper card says plainly the phone isn't on file and dispatch can connect them. No number is invented.
- **Tracking**: `getShipperTracking` is token-only (no login) and returns lane, plain status, commodity, pickup/delivery text, carrier name, POD seal time — state-level summary only, no coordinates, no rate, no fee data. In this build the token opens the portal's tracking view (copy-token button says exactly that); a public tap-to-open web link is future work with the public portal.
- **Documents**: `getShipperLoadDoc` serves rate-con to the owning shipper or the desk; the sealed BOL is served only after the POD seals, to the same two audiences. Rate-con accepts PDF or photo — always accepted (image rate cons also run the existing extractLoad prefill, best-effort).
- **Findability**: Loads tab pill "🚚 Shipper Portal" (visible when the caller's portal mode ≠ none, with review-queue count for the desk), More list entry for every role (opens Loads → Shipper Portal; the panel's none-mode is a plain explainer), Owner Console sidebar section "Shipper Portal" (review queue + accounts at desktop width), and an Ask Meca destination named exactly "Shipper Portal" (resolver regex on shipper phrasing, role-aware note, jump lands on the portal). Meca's context carries `shipperPortalMode` + the portal rules.
- **Demo data**: NO seeded shipper rows. The desk can create accounts flagged `isDemo`; every surface badges them DEMO ("staging demo account").
- **Auto-dispatch — SPEC ONLY, not built (owner ask 2026-10-29)**: an OPTIONAL owner/fleet-owner toggle (off by default; manual board claiming stays the default forever). When on, a newly posted board load is first OFFERED to best-fit carriers instead of opening instantly: fit scored on equipment match, distance to pickup, Sealed Driver Score, and on-time record; the best-fit carrier gets a short claim window (minutes, owner-set) to accept; unaccepted offers cascade to the next-best carrier; when the window ladder empties the load opens to the whole board as today. Drivers never have to use it — an offered load is an offer, not an assignment; declining costs nothing and doesn't touch score. Shipper Portal loads join the same ladder only AFTER desk release (the review queue is never bypassed). No auto-dispatch UI, tables, or actions exist in this build.

## Addendum — Scan & auto-fill: BOL / rate con + payroll (edit, 2026-10-30)
- **Owner ask**: "add that BOL n rate con can be scanned and photographed or uploaded and everything auto do what they say AI extraction does since Meca is the ai agent for app" + "Yes, build the scan and auto-fill" + "What about payroll".
- **Loads (BOL / rate con)**: `extractLoad` (Meca = `ctx.inference` vision read, server-side only) now returns the full document: doc kind (rate_con | bol | load_sheet | other), shipper, broker company + contact + MC # (kept apart from the shipper), pickup/delivery city/state AND street addresses, pickup/delivery windows, rate, itemized charges (fuel surcharge / detention / lumper / other) + charges total + printed grand total, weight, commodity, pieces, seal number, and load / PO / PRO numbers. The Add-a-load form gained broker, load #, pieces, and street-address fields; itemized charges roll into the load's additional-charges with the breakdown as the note. A scan-review card lists exactly what the document showed, what it didn't (fields stay blank — never guessed), and flags: (a) printed total ≠ rate + charges (≥1¢), (b) document reads as a BOL rather than a rate con. Nothing saves from the read — the driver confirms; saving seals the document (SHA-256, existing `doc_sealed` path) and the confirmed fields land in the rate-con snapshot (`saveLoad` gained optional `docLoadNumber/docPoNumber/docProNumber/docPickupAddress/docDeliveryAddress/docPieces`) that the rate-con vs BOL cross-check reads. Fleet Board posting and the Shipper Portal fill their broker/address/charges fields from the same read.
- **Cross-check goes automatic at pickup**: the pickup BOL read now also returns addresses/pieces/reference numbers; `recordBolExtraction` stores them on the BOL snapshot, and confirming the BOL details auto-runs `runLoadDocVerification` — the verdict is bannered and differences land as open exceptions on the load's existing Document verification card (the sealed-POD/invoice packet path is unchanged).
- **Payroll**: new action `extractPayStub` — the same Meca vision read for a pay stub, weekly settlement statement, or year-end W-2/1099 form photo. Returns doc kind, pile (w2 | 1099 | null when the document doesn't say), payer, pay date (ISO), period text, tax year, gross, federal/state withheld (W-2 only), W-2 other deductions, per diem (its own field — never inside gross), printed net, and itemized 1099 deduction lines. Pay Book → Log pay gained "Scan stub / settlement — Meca fills the form": the read fills the existing form (kind, form flag, payer, date, figures, deduction-line editor, per diem), the photo seals behind the entry on log, and a review card shows found/missing plus flags (pile unclear; printed net ≠ gross − amounts taken off, per-diem docs excluded from that check). Filing still happens only through `logPayEntry` — W-2/1099 piles separate, per diem non-taxable and never in a filing gross, 1099 lines never tax. No plan gate (Pay Book logging was never gated).
- **PDF honesty**: the vision reader takes JPEG/PNG only (the `ctx.inference` image contract). PDFs keep the existing behavior — attached, fingerprinted, and sealed with the load/entry, with the driver's confirmed fields recorded as the cross-check's rate-con side — and both scan surfaces say plainly that a photo/screenshot of the pages is what Meca reads automatically. No PDF text is ever invented or silently half-read.
- No schema migration: loads keep broker columns (0049); references/addresses/pieces live in the existing `load_doc_extractions` snapshots (0053); pay entries unchanged (0064–0068).

## Addendum — Confirmed-document copies: emailed to driver / dispatch / owner + saved in the user's Files (edit, 2026-10-30)
- **Owner ask**: "add a copy of any confirmed uploads get emailed to driver, dispatch and owner/ fleet owner after upload and saved in users file" — with the same-morning scope clarification recorded in MEMORY: covers confirmed LOAD documents (BOL, rate con, POD); pay-stub delivery keeps its locked in-app-only rule (email only via the driver's own share sheet) and is NOT routed through this system.
- **Confirmation moments (the only triggers)**: (1) `saveLoad` with a NEW document attached — saving after reviewing Meca's scan read (or typing the fields) is the confirmation; a plain field edit with no new document files nothing. The scan's doc kind rides in on the new optional `docKind` arg and labels the copy (unread hand-attached files file as a plain load document, never upgraded). (2) `recordBolExtraction` — the driver's explicit confirm of the pickup BOL read; the filed copy references the sealed pickup BOL photo blob (the load owns that blob). (3) `sealDelivery` — the POD: the filed copy references the sealed pickup BOL photo when one exists, else the booking document, else no file (the POD is then the sealed chain record itself, and the email says so). Deduped per document fingerprint (per seal hash for PODs): re-confirming the same bytes never files or emails twice; a re-seal after a POD void is a new POD and copies again.
- **Data (migration 0070)**: `doc_copy_settings` (single row: enabled default ON, driver/dispatch/owner emails — the app holds no login email for the driver in this staging model, so the settings' driver email is the address of record, all editable in the UI), `filed_doc_copies` (load, kind rate_con|bol|pod|load_sheet|load_document, label, blob key + owns_blob flag — booking docs get their OWN byte copy at save time so the filed copy is independent of the load's doc blob; BOL/POD copies reference the sealed photo — mime, SHA-256, source load_doc|pickup_bol|pod_seal, filed_by, unique dedupe key), `doc_email_deliveries` (one row per copy × seat: role, resolved email + username, status, plain-language detail, provider id, sent time, unique dedupe key). Every filing seals a `doc_copies_filed` chain event; settings saves and re-sends seal their own events. Load deletion and the full data wipe take the copies, their owned blobs, deliveries, and settings with them.
- **Recipients (pure rules in `server/src/docCopies.ts`, unit-tested)**: driver ← settings driver email; dispatch ← settings dispatch email (username from the company roster's first dispatch seat); owner / fleet owner ← settings owner email, else the company setup contact email. Solo accounts: the owner seat is the driver. Missing/invalid addresses are never invented — that seat's row reads `no_address` with where to add it.
- **Email transport — honest staging, same pattern as Stripe**: the app previously had NO server email rails (the pay-stub posture). This build adds a real privileged transport (`docEmailConfig` / `sendDocEmail` in `server/src/privileged.ts`) against the Resend API, key + sender read from the server environment at call time only (`RESEND_API_KEY` / `DOC_EMAIL_FROM`, never stored or returned). NOT configured in this staging environment: deliveries with an address therefore record **queued** with the plain detail that nothing has been sent and queued copies send when email connects at go-live; a row reads **sent** only with a provider id returned by the transport. Booking-document emails attach the actual file bytes when sent; BOL/POD emails carry the load summary + SHA-256 fingerprint and point at the sealed file in the app (blob bytes cannot be re-read server-side — the shipper-portal lesson). Re-send lives on each delivery row and refuses honestly while the provider is unconnected.
- **UI**: Loads → All loads gains the **Filed document copies** card directly under Add a load: the rule in plain words, the staging honesty banner while the provider is unconnected, Document copies settings (on/off + the three addresses + where each copy currently resolves), and the files list — each copy with kind chip, lane, fingerprint, view/download (PDF via PdfViewer, images inline), and its three delivery rows with status chips and re-send. Save/confirm/POD-seal banners report the copy outcome (filed / sent n / queued n / no-address n / copies off). Ask Meca's loads destination explains the card. Pay-stub surfaces are untouched and the panel states the pay-stub exclusion plainly.
- **Hardening (audit security correction, same edit)**: settings are PER-USER (migration 0071 adds `doc_copy_settings.username`, unique; the 0070 single row is backfilled to the profile username and pre-split rows are claimed by their owner on first read) — a confirmation always resolves recipients from the FILING user's own row, so one account's addresses can never govern another's documents. In a company, the dispatch/owner addresses are writable only by Owner/Dispatch seats (server gate in `saveDocCopySettings` + the panel disables those fields for drivers); a driver edits only their own driver email and the switch. Reads are scoped: `listFiledDocCopies`, `getFiledDocCopyFile`, and `resendDocEmailDelivery` serve only copies the caller filed or was the addressed recipient of (the delivery's named username) — anyone else gets the empty list / no-file answer / a plain refusal, so rate cons, BOLs, PODs, delivery email addresses, lanes, and fingerprints never leak across drivers.

## Addendum — Both email paths optional: pay-stub email toggle (edit, 2026-10-30)
- **Owner ask**: "Keep it like that but have both optional also" — clarified in MEMORY the same morning: keep the confirmed-upload email rule, but BOTH email paths are optional settings: (1) confirmed load docs (BOL, rate con, POD) to driver/dispatch/owner — default ON; (2) pay-stub email — default OFF, driver opts in. Saving a copy in the user's files is NOT gated by the toggles.
- **Data (migration 0072)**: `doc_copy_settings.pay_stub_email_enabled` (boolean, default 0/OFF) — a SEPARATE column beside the load-document `enabled` (default ON); neither toggle reads the other. New table `pay_stub_email_deliveries` (pay_entry_id, delivery_id for company-sent stubs, kind w2|1099, is_form, payer label, recipient role/email/username, status, detail, provider_id, unique dedupe key `payentry:<id>:<role>`, created/sent times). No stub file and no pay figures beyond the payer label the email carried are stored there — the stub stays sealed in its Pay Book entry. The full data wipe deletes these rows with the settings.
- **Triggers (confirmations only, mirroring the load rule)**: `logPayEntry` when the logged entry carries a stub file (a scanned/uploaded stub the driver checked and logged — a hand-typed entry with no file is not a confirmed upload and never copies), and `acceptPayStub` when the accepted company delivery carried a stub file. `sendPayStub` NEVER emails — sending stays in-app only; the copy fires on the DRIVER's acceptance. Both callers wrap the helper (`maybeEmailPayStubCopy` in actions.ts) so a copy failure never fails the Pay Book filing, which always lands first.
- **Rules (pure, unit-tested)**: `shouldAutoEmailPayStub` in payStub.ts (toggle ON + stub file present), `planPayStubDeliveryStatus` + `payStubEmailDedupeKey` + `payStubKindLabel` in docCopies.ts (same honest ladder as load docs: off / no_address / queued while no provider is connected / send only with a provider; sent only with a provider id). Recipients resolve from the DRIVER's own Document copies settings via the existing `resolveDocCopyRecipients` (driver / dispatch / owner seats, company-contact fallback for owner). Each email carries ONE stub only — W-2 and 1099 are never merged into a filing figure, and per diem prints as its own non-taxable line. Confirmations seal a `pay_stub_email_copies` chain event; re-sends seal `pay_stub_email_resent`; settings saves now record `payStubEmailEnabled` in `doc_copy_settings_updated`.
- **Settings + UI**: `getDocCopySettings` returns `payStubEmailEnabled`; `saveDocCopySettings` accepts it as an optional field (omitted = keep saved choice; it is the driver's own opt-in and is NOT part of the fleet-address gate). Document copies settings (Loads → All loads → Filed document copies) now shows two labelled switches — "Load documents — email copies on (rate con, BOL, POD)" default on, and "Pay stubs — email copies off (your opt-in)" default off — plus a "Pay-stub email copies" list with per-seat status chips and re-send (`listPayStubEmailDeliveries` / `resendPayStubEmailDelivery`, scoped to the entry's owner or the addressed seat, same as filed copies). The Pay Book shows a status card at the top ("Pay-stub email copies: ON — your opt-in / OFF (default)") pointing at the settings card, and its log/accept banners report the copy outcome from the new `stubEmail` summary on `logPayEntry` / `acceptPayStub` responses. Owner Console send-desk copy now states the send is never emailed and the copy is the driver's opt-in on acceptance. Ask Meca's loads and pay_book destinations both name the two toggles, their defaults, and where they live.
- **What did NOT change**: the driver's own share-sheet email (StubShareButton) works either way and still stores no recipient; load-document copies, recipients, transport (Resend privileged path, queued honestly in staging), per-user settings scoping, and fleet-address gates are untouched; filing in the user's Files / Pay Book is never gated by either toggle.

## Addendum — Pay Book Year-to-Date lane pies + editable itemized deductions (edit, 2026-10-30)
- **Owner asks**: (1) "Can we have paybook/settlement with a year to date pie chart" — approved shape: YTD pie(s) with W-2 gross, 1099 gross, per diem on its own slice (marked non-taxable), and deductions, year picker defaulting to the current year, tracking view only. (2) Owner's added rule, verbatim: **"Keep w-2 & 1099 separate lanes. Don't mix"** — applied strictly to the YTD view: TWO separate pies (one per lane), each with its own gross / per-diem / deductions slices, and NO combined pie and NO combined grand total across the lanes anywhere in the YTD card or its action response. (3) Same pass: users can EDIT a Pay Book entry's deductions (add/edit/remove itemized lines, existing suggestion list + free text), and scanning a stub/settlement files the printed deductions as itemized lines on the entry automatically. Both paths feed the same lines and that lane's YTD deductions slice; lanes never cross; 1099 rule held — deductions are never tax withholding.
- **YTD action**: `getPayBookYtd({year})` — filters non-form `pay_entries` to the pay date's UTC calendar year (entries are stamped at local noon, so the UTC year is the driver's calendar year), reuses `summarizePayBook` on that year's checks, and shapes slices via the pure `payBookYtdSlices` in `server/src/payBook.ts`. Per lane it returns gross, perDiem, deductions (W-2 lane: federal + state withheld + other deductions; 1099 lane: the stub deductions), that lane's OWN pie total (its three slices — the pie's denominator, labelled a size-comparison total, never a filing number), paycheck count, the withheld/other breakdown, `availableYears` (current year + every year present in the book, desc), and the tracking-only note. There is deliberately no combined field in the response.
- **YTD UI**: Pay Book card "📊 Year-to-Date pies — one per lane" (anchor `#pay-book-ytd-pie`, above Logged pay), one `<select>` year picker (current year default) driving both pies, Recharts pies (`YtdLanePie` in NextLevel.tsx): gold W-2 gross / green 1099 gross, per diem sky in both lanes, deductions coral in both lanes; legend rows with money + % of that lane's own total are built from the same slice array the renderer draws; each lane carries its own empty state ("No pay logged in this lane for {year} yet") and its own deductions composition note. The card renders independent of the period tabs. Log / accept / delete / deduction-edit all invalidate `["pay-book-ytd"]`.
- **Deduction lines generalized (no migration — the 0068 JSON column already exists)**: `resolveDeductionFigures` (actions.ts) now lets itemized lines live on ANY non-form entry in either lane. When lines are present their sum IS the entry's `otherDeductions` (one deductions number — a bogus lump sent beside lines is replaced, verified live); with no lines a W-2 entry keeps its lump figure exactly as before; 1099 federal/state stay forced to 0; W-2 federal/state pass through untouched in their own fields — lines are never tax in either lane. Forms never carry lines. Company stub deliveries are unchanged (`deliveryToPayEntry` still maps 1099 deliveries only — the send desk sends no W-2 lines).
- **Edit action**: `updatePayEntryDeductions({id, deductionLines})` — normalizes the lines, replaces the entry's stored lines + `otherDeductions` with their sum, seals a `pay_entry_deductions_edited` chain event, and refuses year-end forms with the plain reason ("a form's figures tie to the printed form exactly"). UI: every non-form Logged-pay entry gets an "✏️ Add/Edit deductions" button opening the existing `DeductionLinesEditor` inline (prefilled from the entry's lines; a lump-only W-2 entry prefills one "Other deductions (as logged)" line so its existing figure is preserved visibly, never silently dropped); the editor's live total is what saving stores. Entry rows now show `DeductionLinesList` for both lanes (packet named per lane) and the W-2 packet itemizes each check's lines like the 1099 packet already did.
- **Scan path**: `extractPayStub` now itemizes NAMED non-tax deductions on W-2 stubs too (401(k), health insurance, child support, union dues…) into `deductionLines` — the federal/state tax lines go only to the withheld fields, never into lines; the W-2 lump `otherDeductions` is read only when the stub prints an unnamed total. Client scan review lists the found lines for either lane and the printed-net cross-check counts the lines when present; the Log pay form shows the lines editor on the W-2 side as well (the lump input steps aside while lines exist, so there's never two deductions figures), and submit sends the lines for both lanes.
- **Findability**: new Ask Meca destination `pay_book_ytd` (MecaDestId + MECA_DEST_INFO + resolver rule keyed on pie/YTD wording + pay context, placed ahead of the settlements/pay_book rules; jump opens the Money tab's Pay Book and scrolls to `#pay-book-ytd-pie`); the Meca context snapshot's appDestinations line and the server askMeca navigate list name it; add/edit-deduction questions route to `pay_book`, whose blurb now describes entry-level deduction editing; Money tab Pay Book button + More list row mention the Year-to-Date lane pies.
- **Verification**: `server/src/payBook.test.ts` gains the YTD lane-pies describe (per-lane slices from own pile only, per diem never pooled, the shape carries NO combined cross-lane total, empty lane = honest zeros); full suite 318/318 (21 files), client + server typecheck clean. Live probes (sandbox data workspace, all probe rows deleted after): a W-2 entry logged with lines [401(k) $60, Health insurance $40] beside a bogus $999 lump stored otherDeductions $100 with fed/state untouched; `getPayBookYtd(2026)` returned w2 {gross 1000, perDiem 80, deductions 250, total 1330} and ten99 {gross 2000, perDiem 20, deductions 300, total 2320} with no combined field, and a $40,000 W-2 FORM entry correctly excluded (count 1 per lane); editing the W-2 entry's lines to [401(k) $75, Union dues $10] moved only the W-2 lane (deductions 235, total 1315) while the 1099 lane stayed 300/2320; editing the form entry was refused with the plain reason. KNOWN LIMIT (unchanged from the scan build): extraction quality on real stub photos is still not exercised end-to-end — the scan→lines path is wired and reviewed in the form, but no live photo read was run in this pass.
- **What did NOT change**: the pre-existing period totals card (including its labelled combined TRACKING total from the 2026-10-27 rule — the no-mix rule was applied strictly to the new YTD view, per the owner's wording), both tax packets' filing figures, per-diem semantics, stub deliveries, email copies, pricing/rewards, and every non-Pay-Book surface.

## Addendum 2026-10-30 — Broker poster accounts + fleet overflow to the Open Board (migration 0073)

Requested by the app owner 2026-10-30, with the fee decision confirmed the same day: "Build both — dispatch fee applies to overflow claims."

- **Open Board = a scope on the existing board, not a second board.** `board_loads` gains `board_scope` ('fleet' default | 'open'), `origin_kind` ('fleet_board' | 'fleet_overflow' | 'broker_poster'), `origin_label` (the fleet's or broker's name as shown on the board), `origin_company_id`, `vetted_only`, and provenance columns (`overflow_reason`, `overflow_from_board_load_id`, `broker_poster_load_id`). `getLoadBoard` now returns fleet-scope rows only; the Open Board has its own read/claim actions.
- **Vetted owner operator** (`vettedStatusFor`, server): the credential standard that already gates broker uploads — Diamond-tier access (`hasDiamondAccess`: Diamond Driver / Owner plan, fleet members riding the fleet's included Diamond) AND running your own operation — a solo account (no company membership) or a fleet **owner** seat. Employee-driver and dispatch seats are NOT vetted. Non-vetted callers to `getOpenBoard` receive zero load rows (the panel renders the plain vetting reasons); claim and doc actions re-check server-side.
- **Fleet overflow** (`moveBoardLoadToOpen`, Owner/Dispatch, reason `fleet_full` | `short_on_drivers`): creates a full open-scope copy of an open fleet-board load (rate, additional charges, rate-con blob reference shared — a blob is only deleted when no board row or poster load references its key), flips the fleet row to the new status `"overflowed"`, and seals `board_load_overflowed_to_open`. The load lives in exactly one claimable place. `recallBoardLoadFromOpen` (while the copy is still open) deletes the copy, reopens the fleet row, seals `board_load_recalled_from_open`. A claimed overflow load can never be recalled.
- **Dispatch fee on overflow claims — charged once, never twice.** No fee-logic change was needed: `ensureDispatchFeeInvoice` keys off any TAKEN `board_loads` row and `dispatch_fee_invoices.load_id` is UNIQUE, so a load can carry exactly one fee invoice across lanes. Open rows carry `companyId` = the origin fleet company (overflow) or the reviewing desk's company (broker), so invoice creation works unchanged; the settle-to-claim gate for open claims uses the new `feeStandingForCarrierGlobal` (standing across all companies — a solo OO claimer has no fleet company). Same rules as fleet claims: 10% of the final freight amount, invoiced at sealed delivery, day-7 warning, day-10 board revocation.
- **Direct pay.** Freight pay on every Open Board load runs direct from the origin (fleet owner or broker) to the claiming carrier; RUGGS never holds it. This is stated on the board, on every load card, and in the claim flow.
- **Broker poster accounts** (`broker_accounts`, `broker_poster_loads` — parallel to the Shipper Portal's 0069 tables, plus `broker_mc` on the account): desk-invited and desk-approved exactly like shipper accounts (invite username claims the account; no self-signup). Brokers post loads to the OPEN board under their own name/logo; every load lands in the Owner Console review queue (`status='sent'`) and nothing hits the board until the desk releases it (`releaseBrokerLoad` creates the open-scope board row with `originKind='broker_poster'`, `originLabel` = broker name, `vettedOnly=true`, and mints the tracking token). Same gates as the Shipper Portal: rate + rate-con required (otherwise draft only), customer sign-off and offload-equipment checkboxes. **No fee numbers on any broker-facing surface** — the broker response shapes are built field-by-field with no fee field, mirroring the shipper rule. Broker tracking (`getBrokerTracking`) mirrors shipper tracking: state-level status for the load's own carrier company; other carriers get the plain status only.
- **Findability**: Loads tab gains an always-visible 🌐 Open Board pill and a 🏢 Broker Portal pill (visible when the account is a broker or a desk seat, with the review-queue count); More list gains Broker Portal + Open Board rows; Owner Console gains Open Board and Broker Portal sidebar sections; Ask Meca gains `open_board` and `broker_portal` destinations (resolver rules ahead of the generic broker rule), context-snapshot notes (`openBoardNote`, `brokerPortalNote`), and plain gate notes.

## Addendum — Team Portal, developer / support seats (edit, 2026-10-30)
- **Owner ask**: "a dev portal in case users need something changed or help for the 'team' — a portal only us the developers can use and have access to, to edit all accounts/users but no access to sensitive information." Approved shape: platform-level, named team seats only (separate from company owner seats; portal screens and actions do not render or resolve at all for non-team accounts); the team CAN look up any account by name or email, see the account shell only (display name, account type, tier, company, seats, account status, signup date), change tier or seats, unlock a stuck account, grant/revoke beta-trial access, manage shipper and broker portal accounts (approve, suspend), and resend invites; the team can NEVER access Pay Book entries/totals, pay stubs, documents/photos or filed copies, load paperwork contents, chat/messages, passwords (hashed, invisible to everyone), wallet keys, seed phrases, or payment credentials — enforced server-side (team actions never SELECT those fields); every team action writes a visible admin audit log (who, which account, what changed, when) viewable in the portal; NO impersonation / "log in as user"; Ask Meca destination "Team Portal" resolving only for team seats.
- **Data (migration 0074)**: `team_seats` (username PK, role developer|support, active, granted_by — starts EMPTY; seats are named by the app owner from Beta Panel → "Admin — Team Portal seats", thereafter by team members from inside the portal; revoke is an active=false flip so history survives, and self-revoke is refused), `team_audit_log` (actor, action, target, detail, created_at — append-only, also mirrored into the sealed hash chain as `team_action` events), `account_admin` (per-username team overlay: tier / trucks / dispatch_seats / beta_access / status, all nullable = "never team-set, derive from the account's own records").
- **The wall is by construction**: pure module `server/src/teamPortal.ts` — `buildAccountShell` copies a fixed 11-key whitelist (username, displayName, email, accountType, tier, companyName, trucks, dispatchSeats, status, betaAccess, signupAt) and `teamPortal.test.ts` proves a shell carries exactly those keys and that sensitive fields stuffed into the input never reach it. The actions layer's `accountShellFor` selects ONLY shell-safe columns (social_users display/company, company membership + company name, doc-copy driver email / linked portal-account business email, subscription_events latest plan, profile plan/seats for this install's account, account_locks, beta redemption/unlock stamps). Team response schemas in actions.ts are the full contract — there is no field through which a sensitive value could travel.
- **Fixes write through for this install's own account** (the live account in staging): tier → `profile.plan`, seats → `profile.owner_trucks/owner_seats`, beta revoke/grant → `beta_access`, unlock → `account_locks` cleared + `company_members.login_disabled` cleared + PIN guard reset. For directory accounts the overlay is the record the shell reads (overlay wins over derived values).
- **Actions**: `getTeamAccess` (isTeam/role/canNameSeats/username — the only team action a non-seat can call, and it returns nothing but the caller's own standing), `teamSearchAccounts`, `teamGetAccount` (shell + linked shipper/broker status + lock reason; the view itself is audit-logged), `teamSetTier`, `teamSetSeats`, `teamUnlockAccount`, `teamSetAccountStatus`, `teamSetBetaAccess`, `teamResendInvite` (mints a FRESH single-use driver beta code via the existing minter; the code returns to the team member to send and is never written into the audit detail), `teamListPortalAccounts`, `teamSetPortalAccountStatus` (shipper/broker approve = active / suspend), `teamListSeats` / `teamGrantSeat` / `teamRevokeSeat` (gated on team seat OR beta owner — seat naming rights), `teamGetAuditLog`. All non-seat calls return the plain refusal "Team Portal access is limited to named team seats." with empty payloads.
- **Findability**: App Menu (☰) gains a "Team Portal" row ONLY when `getTeamAccess` says the account holds a seat; Ask Meca gains the `team_portal` destination (resolver rule ahead of the console/generic rules; `buildMecaNav` hard-locks it for non-team askers with the plain reason + Today alternative; snapshot gains `teamPortalIsTeam` / `teamPortalRole` / `teamPortalNote` and the destinations string names it "team seats only"); the Beta Panel's owner branch gains the seat-naming desk so the first seats can be granted. The portal UI (`client/src/TeamPortal.tsx`) states the wall in plain words and carries no impersonation control anywhere.

## Addendum — Team Portal expansion: boards, giveaways, sends, transparency, consent (edit, 2026-10-30)
- **Owner expansion (verbatim)**: "Dev portal should have access to load board both open and private. (keep double booking from happening) host giveaway on giveaway board. Have access to wallet address to send point and $stoner. Everything but paybook entries, pay stubs, paperwork, messages, passwords or seed any personal sensitive information. Upon owner portal and driver can see when changes been made and by who via app & email. Dev portal can help change passwords or personal information ONLY WITH USERS APPROVAL! (NO APPROVAL NO CHANGE/HELP)." **Owner clarification (same day)**: "Approval only for — nothing sensitive": the consent gate applies ONLY to password-reset help and personal-information edits; the sensitive categories (Pay Book entries, pay stubs, paperwork/documents, messages, seed phrases, payment credentials) are hard-walled with NO consent override — approval never grants the team access to them, ever.
- **Data (migration 0075)**: `team_board_flags` (per board row), `team_giveaways` + `team_giveaway_recipients` (platform campaigns mirroring owner_giveaways shapes minus company scope, plus funding_source), `team_change_notices` (per-account transparency notices with honest email_status: sent only with a provider id / queued while no provider / no_address / failed), `team_consent_requests` (kind is a CLOSED enum `password_reset | personal_info` — the structural guarantee that approval can unlock nothing else; fields_json carries the exact proposed personal-info values; status pending → approved/denied → consumed, one application per approval).
- **Boards**: pure detection in teamPortal.ts (`boardLineageKey` / `boardConflicts`, unit-tested incl. the healthy-overflow non-conflict): lineage = fleet row + its overflow copies, or one broker portal load's copies; a lineage with >1 live (open/taken) row, or one driver load claimed from two rows, is a double booking. `teamGetBoards` returns BOTH boards' rows (work fields only — doc blob/sha never selected) with conflict reasons, per-row claim-return eligibility from the desk's own `boardLinkedState`/`toBoardLoad` probe, and flags. Resolutions mirror the desk mechanics: `teamReturnBoardClaim` (the unassign hand-back verbatim, team seat as actor, chain events carry `via: "team_portal"`), `teamWithdrawBoardRow` (open rows; blob deleted only when NO board row / driver load / portal load shares it; withdrawing an overflow copy whose fleet row sits "overflowed" reopens the fleet copy), `teamFlagBoardLoad` / `teamClearBoardFlag`. All logged; claim returns and withdrawals notify the affected driver/poster.
- **Giveaways**: `teamCreateGiveaway` (selected shares via the owner portal's `equalSplit`/`validatePerDriverAmounts`; random mode seals `commitmentCanonicalString` with companyId 0 = platform; payout addresses 0x-validated; entrants must be known accounts), `teamFundGiveaway` ($STONER only, against `rewardWalletBalance` — verified source; USD refuses with the designed `USD_GATE_NOTE` rails gate and stays draft), `teamDrawGiveaway` (owner draw discipline verbatim: seed = SHA-256(commitment ‖ chain head), `drawWinners`), `teamDistributeGiveaway` (per-winner `reward_wallet_ledger` rows kind `team_giveaway_payout` with UNIQUE refKey `team_giveaway_payout:<recipientId>` — never paid twice; balance re-checked per payout; a short wallet pauses distribution with paid winners final; winners notified), `teamCancelGiveaway` (owner `cancelGate`; funding is reservation-only so no money ever moved pre-distribution). Users see their team giveaways in `getMyTeamActivity`.
- **Sends**: `teamSendReward` (points via `awardPoints` source `team_reward`; $STONER via reward wallet ledger kind `team_reward`, balance-checked, chain-sealed like `payTeamReward`). BOTH honour the rewards go-live gate `pointsConversionLive` (admin_settings.points_conversion_live — OFF until Nov 20 + owner's go-live word) and refuse plainly while off (verified live in the sandbox). The account detail shows the PUBLIC wallet address on record (latest giveaway payout address — owner or team — else "None on record"; no address is ever invented) and the send record names amount, asset, destination, and actor.
- **Transparency**: `notifyTeamChange` helper — every account-changing team action (tier, seats, unlock, status, beta, portal-account status, board resolutions, sends, giveaway payouts, consent requests/applications) writes a `team_change_notices` row AND attempts email through the doc-copy transport (`sendDocEmail` privileged contract; actions declare it). Users read notices + consent requests + their team giveaways via `getMyTeamActivity` on the Driver tab card "Team changes to your account" (`MyTeamActivityCard`, renders only when the team has touched the account), mark read via `markMyTeamNoticesRead`, and answer approvals via `decideTeamConsent` (target-only; the decision itself is written to the team audit log as `consent_approved`/`consent_denied`).
- **Consent gate**: `teamRequestConsent` (password_reset carries no values; personal_info carries the exact field values the user approves), `teamListConsentRequests`, `teamApplyConsent` — refuses plainly on pending ("no approval, no change"), denied, and consumed (one application per approval). Password application = trigger only: clears `profile.pin_hash` + revokes the account's `login_sessions` for THIS install's account (other accounts' sign-ins live on their own devices — the action says so plainly); the user sets their own new PIN. Personal-info application writes exactly the approved fields (social_users display name / home city / home state; doc-copy driver email). The portal UI, the user card, the Ask Meca blurb, and the snapshot note all state the hard line in plain words.

## Team Portal — consent-gated sensitive help (x, 2026-10-30, migration 0076)

Owner-approved exception to the wall, narrowly built: `team_item_consent_windows` + `team_item_access_log`. A window names ONE item (`item_kind` closed set in teamPortal.ts: `pay_entry | pay_stub | document` — structurally NO password/seed/payment-credential kind, unit-tested), ONE named team seat, 30 minutes (`ITEM_CONSENT_WINDOW_MS`; `itemWindowState` derives expiry, unit-tested). Flow: `teamRequestItemConsent` (names the item in WORDS only — opens nothing; user notified in-app + email) → `decideItemConsent` (user-side only, from their own account; the SERVER verifies the picked item belongs to them — pay entry stamped owner or linked delivery recipient, stub delivery recipient, filed copy filedBy — and derives `item_label` server-side; canEdit is the user's separate toggle) → `teamViewConsentedItem` (named seat only, active window only; returns ONLY that item — pay entry fields + parsed deduction lines + its own stub file URL, stub delivery fields + file URL, filed copy fields + file URL; blobs via `ctx.blobs.getUrl`, rendered client-side by MIME with PdfViewer/img, never iframe) / `teamEditConsentedPayEntry` (canEdit + pay_entry + non-form only; replaces deduction lines with the same normalize/sum discipline as `updatePayEntryDeductions`; stubs/documents refuse as sealed copies). Every view/edit writes `team_item_access_log` + an audit-log entry. `revokeItemConsent` closes instantly; expiry closes itself via `sweepItemWindow` on any read; closeout sends the FULL session record through `notifyTeamChange` exactly once (`closeout_sent_at` guard). Team side lists windows via `teamListItemWindows`; user side reads/decides/revokes inside `getMyTeamActivity`/`MyTeamActivityCard` ("Sensitive-item access" — item pickers fed by the user's own `listPayEntries`/`listReceivedStubs`/`listFiledDocCopies`). No phone/email approval path exists. The earlier password/personal-info consent (`team_consent_requests`) is unchanged and separate.

## Team seat access — owner-issued invite codes only (x, 2026-10-30, migration 0077)

- **Owner's lock (verbatim intent)**: "I'm the only granted access to team portal. I can give access to the dev team via code and email different from reference code or other code. No access to anyone but me n you with approval for add on team." Locked rules: the owner is the ONLY team seat holder until he approves an addition; the ONLY paths to a seat are (a) the owner's own seat and (b) redeeming an owner-issued team invite code; direct seat grants by username are closed; revoking is instant and owner-only.
- **Data (migration 0077)**: `team_invite_codes` (id, code UNIQUE, email normalized lowercase, role developer|support, issued_by = owner's username, status pending|redeemed|revoked|expired, created_at, expires_at = +7 days, redeemed_by/redeemed_at). Starts EMPTY — no invite is seeded; the owner's existing seat row (granted 2026-10-30) is untouched and remains the only seat.
- **Distinct namespace**: format `TEAM-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX` (prefix TEAM-, 3×4 chars from the same unambiguous alphabet), vs referral `MILE-REF-XXXXXX`, beta/driver `MILE-XXXX-XXXX`, owner `MILE-X-OWNER1`. Pure rules in teamPortal.ts (`isTeamInviteCode`, `otherCodeFamily`, `inviteEmailMatches`, `maskInviteEmail`, `maskTeamInviteCode`, `teamInviteState`, `TEAM_INVITE_TTL_MS` = 7 days) are unit-tested, including that MILE- codes are never team invites and the alphabet excludes I/L/O. Redemption reads ONLY `team_invite_codes`; no other redemption reads it — a team invite does nothing else (no beta unlock, no referral link).
- **Actions**: `teamCreateInvite` (OWNER-ONLY — `isBetaOwner`; generating the code IS the owner's approval action; one live invite per email; refuses when an account under that email already holds a seat; the full code returns ONLY in this response, is never written to the audit detail, and lists show it masked `TEAM-••••-••••-XXXX`), `teamListInvites` (owner-only, masked), `teamRevokeInvite` (owner-only, pending only), `teamRedeemInvite` (any account; refuses other code families BY NAME, unknown/used/revoked/expired codes, the owner's own account (already seated — invite not burned), accounts already holding a seat (not burned), accounts with no email on file, and email mismatches — the binding is `accountEmailFor` (doc-copy driver email, else linked shipper/broker email) vs the invite email, exact after trim/case; success burns the code via a conditional pending→redeemed update (single-use even under a race) and inserts/reactivates the seat with granted_by = the issuing owner), `teamGrantSeat` (KEPT ONLY as a refusal stub — every caller gets "Direct seat grants are closed…"; no code path inserts a seat except redemption and the pre-existing owner row), `teamRevokeSeat` (now OWNER-ONLY; the owner's own seat can never be revoked; the revoked holder is notified), `teamListSeats` (view: owner or active seat, read-only), `getTeamAccess` (adds `isOwner`, `canManageSeats` = owner-only, and the caller's own `email` on file; `canNameSeats` now means owner-only management).
- **Audit + owner notification**: issuance (`invite_issued`), redemption (`invite_redeemed`), invite revocation (`invite_revoked`), failed redemption attempts (`invite_redeem_failed`, no code recorded), and seat revocation (`revoke_seat`) all write `team_audit_log` rows via `teamLog` (mirrored into the sealed chain). Issuance notifies the owner via `notifyTeamChange` (in-app notice + email through the doc-copy transport, honest queued/no_address staging); redemption notifies the OWNER (new seat exists, who redeemed, revoke path named) and confirms to the new seat holder the same way.
- **Findability**: Team Portal → Team seats carries the owner's invite desk (generate + one-time code reveal + masked invite list + seat list) inside the portal, and Beta Panel → "Admin — Team Portal seats (owner only)" mirrors it. A non-seat account's App Menu → Team Portal entry now opens the sheet's redemption door ONLY (email-on-file shown, TEAM- code form; the portal content still never renders for non-seats, and every team action still refuses them server-side). Ask Meca: `team_portal` resolver also matches team-invite/redeem phrasing (ahead of the referral/wallet rule), the nav for non-seats explains invite-only access and opens the redemption door instead of hard-locking, and the blurb + snapshot note state the lock verbatim in plain words.

## Team contact email — stonervisionteam@gmail.com (x, 2026-10-30, migration 0078)

- **Owner directive**: the owner created the Stonervision team email stonervisionteam@gmail.com and ordered it wired in as THE team email — the Team Portal's team contact address, the address recorded as the team's own wherever the app records a team email. The owner's own account keeps its own email; this is the team's mailbox, separate from ruggsllc7@gmail.com, which stays RUGGS-only. Email transport remains in its current staged state (queued honestly where no provider is connected) — this change records and uses the address; it does not change transport.
- **Data (migration 0078)**: `admin_settings.team_email` (single-row platform settings, NOT NULL DEFAULT 'stonervisionteam@gmail.com') is the recorded team mailbox, and `team_change_notices.team_email` (NOT NULL DEFAULT 'stonervisionteam@gmail.com') stamps every transparency notice with the team address it was addressed from/for the team under. No row is seeded beyond the column defaults; existing rows pick the address up from the default.
- **Rules (teamPortal.ts)**: `TEAM_CONTACT_EMAIL = "stonervisionteam@gmail.com"` and `TEAM_CONTACT_NAME = "Stonervision Team"` are the locked constants; `teamContactFooter()` and `isTeamContactEmail()` are unit-tested in teamPortal.test.ts (including that ruggsllc7@gmail.com is NOT the team address). `getTeamContactEmail` in actions.ts reads admin_settings and falls back to the constant — the address is never guessed.
- **Where it is used**: `getTeamAccess` returns `teamEmail` + `teamEmailName` alongside (never instead of) the caller's own `email`; `notifyTeamChange` — the single funnel for every team notice, seat-invite issuance notice, and consent-session closeout — addresses its email body `From: Stonervision Team <stonervisionteam@gmail.com>`, names the team email in the body and footer ("contact the team at …"), records queued/sent/failed detail mentioning the team mailbox, and stamps the notice row's `team_email`. `teamCreateInvite` records issuance "from the team mailbox …" in the audit log and the owner's notice; `teamRedeemInvite`'s audit entry names the team mailbox; `closeoutItemWindow` names the team mailbox in the closeout detail and its audit entry. `getMyTeamActivity` returns the top-level `teamEmail` and each notice's stamped `teamEmail`.

The driver's "Team changes to your account" card (`MyTeamActivityCard`) shows the team contact and each notice's "From team: stonervisionteam@gmail.com". Ask Meca's `team_portal` blurb and the snapshot `teamPortalNote` state the team email, the RUGGS separation, and the staged transport.

## EIA weekly diesel averages — auto-refresh (x, 2026-10-30, migration 0079)

- **Owner directive**: "Yes" to wiring the EIA weekly diesel averages to auto-refresh weekly, replacing the static Oct 22, 2026 research snapshot (week of Oct 19, 2026: national $6.276 / Gulf Coast $5.903 / West Coast $7.394) previously hardcoded in `getFuelPriceData` and `getLoadFuelTips`.
- **Source (tested live at build time)**: the U.S. Energy Information Administration's official weekly retail on-highway diesel pages — plain public HTML, no API key (the EIA API v2 requires a registered key this app does not hold). Declared host: `www.eia.gov`. Pages/series: U.S. `https://www.eia.gov/dnav/pet/pet_pri_gnd_dcus_nus_w.htm` (EMD_EPD2D_PTE_NUS_DPG) · Gulf Coast (PADD 3) `https://www.eia.gov/dnav/pet/pet_pri_gnd_dcus_r30_w.htm` (EMD_EPD2D_PTE_R30_DPG) · West Coast (PADD 5) `https://www.eia.gov/dnav/pet/pet_pri_gnd_dcus_r50_w.htm` (EMD_EPD2D_PTE_R50_DPG). Verified 2026-10-30: all three pages report week of Oct 26, 2026 — national $6.352, Gulf Coast $5.951, West Coast $7.329 — matching EIA's Oct 27, 2026 release. Parsing (`server/src/eiaDiesel.ts`, unit-tested in `eiaDiesel.test.ts`) anchors on the series id and the Series5 week headers; the three pages must agree on the week and prices must be plausible, or the refresh stores nothing.
- **Storage**: `eia_diesel_snapshots` (migration 0079) — one row per week (week_of = the Monday, ISO, UNIQUE), national/gulf_coast/west_coast, `source` + `source_url` stored WITH the data, `fetched_at` = when the values were obtained. The table bootstraps in code from the labelled seed (the old Oct 19, 2026 snapshot, source labelled "via the Oct 22, 2026 research snapshot (seed — awaiting the first live weekly refresh)") so a last good week always exists; no seed row is written by the migration.
- **Refresh cadence**: (1) the scheduled weekly action `refreshEiaDiesel` (cron `trucker-gps-eia-weekly-refresh`, Tuesdays 12:00 America/Chicago — after EIA's weekly release); (2) a throttled on-read fallback in `loadEiaView` — when the last successful fetch is over 7 days old, a read may spend one attempt (≤1/hour/process); (3) the manual "↻ Refresh EIA week" button in the Diamond fuel planner. One retrieval path only: server-side fetch of the three EIA pages in `fetchEiaDieselSnapshot`.
- **Failure honesty**: a failed, unparsable, or self-disagreeing refresh writes NOTHING — readers keep serving the last good week. `getFuelPriceData`'s `eia` object now carries weekOf (label) + weekDate (ISO) + source + sourceUrl + fetchedAt + stale + ageDays; stale (>10 days old) flips the note to name the last good week and its age. The Fuel tab paragraph shows the source, last-fetched date, and a stale warning; `getLoadFuelTips` builds its EIA line from the same stored snapshot (with the same stale wording) instead of the hardcoded line. The honest labelling is unchanged everywhere: **regional context only, never a station price** — no live per-station price feed is added by this change.
- **Untouched**: the IFTA tax matrix snapshot, driver-reported prices, and own-fuel-log prices keep their existing sources and labelling.
