## Addendum — Road test #3: wrong limit + parallel-road match (edit, 2026-10-30)

- **Driver report (verbatim)**: "Road test- first speed limit shows not but not accurate. 2. Truck goes off on the side not in the road. Sometime you'll be on the service road but it shows you on the interstate." Screenshots the same drive: (1) near I-10 ("Turn left to take the I 10 ramp", 0.5 mi), speed 69, LIMIT 55, red "OVER THE LIMIT — 69 in a 55" banner; (2) Theodore Dawes Rd, 45 in a 45 — correct. MEMORY.md records the same report (~14:28 CT screenshots, reported 19:05 CT).
- **Diagnosis (all in the matching layer, not the data)**: (a) Client + server limit selection scored DISTANCE ONLY — inside the fix filter's ±65 m accepted accuracy, parallel carriageways/frontage roads 15–50 m apart flip fix-by-fix; a single point carried no heading and no history. (b) The server's Valhalla scan walked backwards WITHOUT LIMIT across road changes — the previous road's tagged limit (e.g. 55) became the current road's answer. (c) The server cache was keyed by ~110 m grid cell alone — two roads crossing one cell shared one cached answer for up to 3 min. (d) The widened Overpass pass (500 m) accepted ways up to 200 m away — at an interchange that is a different road. (e) The visible overspeed banner derived from the DISPLAYED limit, including EST / LAST KNOWN rungs and any single unconfirmed match — one wrong match fired the banner, beep, voice, sealed safety event, and over-limit minutes. (f) The truck marker drew the RAW accepted fix — honest GPS scatter put it beside the road it was on; nothing smoothed or snapped the display.
- **Fix — matching (`client/src/speedLimit.ts` matchRoad, mirrored server-side)**: candidates are scored on distance + heading alignment (one-way ways judged directionally — the opposing carriageway is the wrong road however close; crossing streets ≈ +85 m-equivalent) + continuity with the previously matched road (ref −34 / name −30 / class −12, +14 switch cost) + speed plausibility (≥50 mph on a service/residential parallel +40). Distance cap stays a hard 120 m on the TRUE distance at both query radii, client and server. Server: `selectValhallaLimit` walks back at most 3 edges and never across a road-class change; `cacheKeyFor` keys cell + 8-wind course bucket from the breadcrumb; Overpass picks take the shape-derived heading. The breadcrumb grew 8 → 20 fixes (~300 m) client-side and in the `getSpeedLimit` request schema (response gained `roadRef`/`roadName` for continuity; no migration — no stored data changed).
- **Fix — alert honesty (`PostedLimitStabilizer`)**: the LIMIT display still follows every posted answer immediately, but the overspeed alert input (banner, red bubble, beep, voice, sealed safety event, trip over-limit minutes — all now keyed off the reactive `alertLimit` state / `speedLimitRef`, which only the stabilizer writes) requires TWO consecutive agreeing posted lookups. A changed limit drops the alert to null until the new one confirms; a held LAST KNOWN limit alerts only if it was the confirmed one; EST never alerts (this last rule now actually holds for the banner, which previously read the display value directly).
- **Fix — marker (`gpsFilter.ts` displayPosition + `routeGeometry.ts` snapDisplayToRoute)**: the filter now also returns a DISPLAY-ONLY smoothed position (parked → the anchor; rolling → 0.7 blend, >60 m jump = straight to the raw fix; rejected fixes hold it). Records (miles, speed, seals, geofences) keep using the raw `position`/`segmentMiles` chain untouched. The truck marker rides the display position, and when a route is on screen it snaps onto the route line only within 25 m AND with course within 40° of the segment — deliberately too tight to drag a frontage-road driver onto a parallel interstate; off-route detection (128 m) still owns that case.
- **Proof standard (unchanged, stated in the App.tsx lookup comment)**: NOT phone-proven until x's next drive confirms on his phone — the sandbox has no GPS and no live road test is possible here. Unit proof: full suite 454/454 (31 files), including new describes pinning each cause above (crossing-street steal, opposing carriageway, frontage continuity, speed plausibility, Valhalla previous-road borrow + 3-edge stop, cache course buckets, stabilizer confirmation gate, display jitter/rolling/rejection holds, snap radius + heading gate).

## Addendum — Road test #4: covered bottom buttons, false slow traffic, service-road dead zone (edit, 2026-10-30)

- **Driver report (verbatim)**: "Road test done. 1 the bottom buttons covered, Ik it's in app view so it won't be the in the App Store. 2. its show slow traffic and it was clear. 3. It's still put the route on the interstate and not on the service road as I was." Screenshots: I-65 Mobile — route line + marker on the interstate mainline while driving West I-65 Service Rd N; a red slow stretch painted on a clear road; LIMIT "-- / NO LIMIT DATA" in two of three shots (the LIMIT item was diagnosed in repair #3 and is not re-litigated here; this pass takes the three items the owner named). Owner approval: "Yes — run repair #4 on all three."
- **Diagnosis (from the pre-dispatch inspection)**: (1) Every bottom-anchored map control (speed bubble, End route / map-size row, traffic legend, pick + chooser sheets) anchored flush at the map's bottom edge (`bottom-2` / `bottom-[46px]`) with no reserve for the Muse shell's floating bottom bar, which covers the bottom ~84 px in the in-app view. (2) Traffic painting smeared: `buildTrafficSegments` colored the whole span between the route's 4 flow samples (plus head/tail) with the slower end's ratio — one marginal reading painted miles; incidents were admitted from up to 0.75 mi off the line client-side (painting) and server-side (`getRouteTraffic` delay + geometry), so trouble on a parallel road painted/annotated this one. (3) The service-road dead zone: display snap is 25 m and off-route is 128 m; an interstate-vs-service-road offset (~30–60 m) sits between them, so the app neither snapped (correct) nor rerouted (wrong) — and routes/reroutes started from a bare GPS point, which Valhalla could snap onto the mainline through the accepted 65 m accuracy fuzz.
- **Fix — bottom reserve**: `main.tsx` sets `data-app-view="embedded"` on `<html>` before first paint only when the app runs inside an iframe (the in-app view; a cross-origin parent that refuses the comparison counts as embedded). `theme.css` defines `--map-bottom-reserve: 0px`, raised to `84px` under that attribute; every bottom-anchored map control and the route-fit bottom padding offset by it. Standalone/store builds run top-level, never set the attribute, and keep the original layout byte-for-byte in effect.
- **Fix — traffic paints only where measured (`client/src/trafficSegments.ts`, server `getRouteTraffic`)**: a flow sample now paints a bounded window of ±`FLOW_PAINT_RADIUS_MI` (1.25 mi) around its own point; two neighbouring congested samples bridge the short gap between their windows (two agreeing measurements), a free-flow neighbour blocks the bridge; no head/tail extrapolation. Incident painting requires the incident geometry to lie within `TRAFFIC_INCIDENT_MAX_OFF_MI` (0.035 mi ≈ 56 m — a geometry-to-centreline distance, so the tight cap is sound where a GPS fix's fuzz would not be) of the route line, down from 0.75 mi. Server `getRouteTraffic` on-route admission tightened 0.75 → 0.15 mi (delay sums + geometry), and the scan-ahead's TomTom incidents likewise 0.75 → 0.15 mi in App.tsx (driver reports keep 0.75 mi — a human pin is approximate in a way a TomTom point is not).
- **Fix — parallel-road awareness (`client/src/routeGeometry.ts` + App.tsx)**: new `ParallelTrackTracker` owns the 28–128 m band: course aligned within 30° of the route segment, sustained ≥8 s AND ≥0.12 mi of along-track travel in the band, fires once per episode (parked beside the route and brief weaves can never fire). Firing joins the ordinary off-route signal in the reroute logic (notice + heartbeat + cooldown shared), with the sustain backdated so there is no second wait. The router now receives the truck's course: `currentCourseDeg()` (breadcrumb span via `headingFromTrack`, else platform heading while rolling) is passed as the Valhalla origin location's `heading`/`heading_tolerance: 60` in `planRoute`, sectioned routing's origin hop, the alternates backfill, and the scan-ahead's `fetchFreshOptions` — so the (re)route snaps its origin onto the carriageway actually being travelled instead of the nearest parallel line.
- **Proof standard (unchanged)**: NOT phone-proven until x's next I-65 drive confirms on his phone — the sandbox has no GPS and no live TomTom/Valhalla road test of the service-road corridor is possible here. Unit proof: `routeGeometry.test.ts` (dead-zone candidate, sustained-fire-once + re-arm, brief-pass and parked negatives, breadcrumb heading) and `trafficSegments.test.ts` (single-sample window cap, two-sample bridge, parallel-road incident rejection) run with the production modules unchanged; all 74 tests across routeGeometry/trafficSegments/gpsFilter/speedLimit pass.
- **Platform v26 (managed upgrade, same edit)**: every `defineAction` in `server/src/actions.ts` (387) now carries a one-sentence `description` derived from its action name; no names, schemas, handlers, or guards changed.

## Addendum — Automatic rate per mile on loads + board ranking by $/mi (edit, 2026-10-31)

- **Owner request (verbatim)**: "Yes — add automatic $/mi to loads", plus the steer: "Also have load board ranking rate per mile + total price of the load."
- **The honest gap this closes**: before this edit, $/mi existed only where miles were typed by hand (the Worth-It Advisor's own inputs, Money Truth from sealed trips, CPM trip pay). Saved loads and board posts carried a flat rate with no miles attached, so no $/mi could be shown — and none was invented.
- **Data (migration 0080)**: `loads`, `board_loads`, and `broker_submissions` gain `loaded_miles` (real, nullable) and `miles_source` (text: `document` | `entered` | `estimate`, null when no miles). No backfill — existing rows keep null miles and show no $/mi until miles arrive. $/mi itself is NEVER stored: every load/board/submission response derives `ratePerMile` (linehaul rate ÷ miles) and `totalPerMile` ((rate + additional charges) ÷ miles, the headline all-in figure) at read time via the server helper `perMileFor`, so a corrected rate or corrected miles can never leave a stale $/mi behind.
- **Where miles come from (provenance, in priority order)**: (1) `document` — the rate con/BOL as printed: the client vision extraction (`extractLoad`, `extractBrokerDoc`) now returns the lane's printed loaded miles, and the fill paths + Broker Inbox approval pre-fill use them with source `document`; (2) `entered` — typed by the owner/driver on the load form, the Fleet Board post/edit form, or the Broker Inbox review form (each form shows the live all-in $/mi as you type); (3) `estimate` — an explicit "Estimate from cities" action on the load form (Nominatim city geocode + haversine × 1.25 road-circuity, rounded), saved only on the owner's tap and always labelled an estimate. Stored miles always display as fact; the label names their source wherever miles are quoted (card details, paperwork summary, share text, Ask Meca context).
- **Display-only estimate fallback**: cards whose load has a rate but no stored miles (and both cities) resolve a city-to-city estimate client-side (`client/src/useLaneEstimate.ts` — Nominatim city search, session-cached, ≤8 lanes per screen, failures leave the figure blank) and show the $/mi TILDE-prefixed with "est." (`client/src/RatePerMile.tsx`, math in `client/src/loadRate.ts`). Estimates are never written to the database from a card render. No miles and no estimable lane → no $/mi shown at all; it is never guessed.
- **Board ranking (Fleet Board + Open Board)**: a sort control defaults to **Best $/mi** — loads rank by effective all-in $/mi (stored figure, else the labelled lane estimate), biggest total breaks ties, unrankable loads (no rate, or no miles and no estimable lane) sink to the bottom newest-first, and ranked cards carry their `#N $/mi` position chip. "Highest total" and "Newest" sorts remain available. The total price (`totalWithExtras`) stays the big number on every card beside the $/mi, exactly as before.
- **Surfaces covered**: Loads tab cards + active-load hero + Load Tracker + load form + paperwork summary + share text + Ask Meca context (loads list and most-recent-load answer); Fleet Board (post/edit form with live preview, ranked open list, claim/taken cards); Open Board (same ranking); Broker Inbox (upload-form extraction fill, review form, submission cards); Shipper/Broker portal "your loads" cards (labelled estimate $/mi beside the rate). Portal-posted loads still enter the board with null miles (the portal forms don't collect miles) — the estimate display covers them, and the Fleet Board edit form can add real miles.
- **Untouched by design**: the stored rate itself, driver CPM/percent pay, RUGGS LLC's 10% dispatch-fee math, additional-charges handling, and trip/IFTA accounting. $/mi is a read-only figure everywhere.
- **Proof**: `client/src/loadRate.test.ts` (12 tests — per-mile math, null rules, estimate circuity, ranking order/tie-break/sink rule) passes; the rest is verified by the platform build + audit.

## Addendum — GPS day / night mode with an Off option (edit, 2026-10-31)

- **Driver request (verbatim)**: "Oh can we add a day n night mode to gps with options to turn off"
- **What shipped**: a four-way GPS map day/night setting — **Auto / Day / Night / Off** — persisted per account like every other map preference. It restyles the **Streets** vector base only: Day (and Off) = the existing true-color OpenFreeMap Liberty style (https://tiles.openfreemap.org/styles/liberty, unchanged); Night = CARTO dark-matter GL basemap at https://basemaps.cartocdn.com/gl/dark-matter-gl-style/style.json (verified live at build time: HTTP 200, valid GL style JSON, OSM data via CARTO, 28 symbol label layers so the existing Labels switch keeps working; attribution line on the map now credits © CARTO). OpenFreeMap's own "dark" style (https://tiles.openfreemap.org/styles/dark) was checked and REJECTED: its motorway fill paints near-black (#000) on a black background — roads would vanish. Satellite/Hybrid are imagery and ignore day/night (stated in the panel copy).
- **Semantics**: Auto = day map in daylight, night map after dark, resolved by the pure `resolveMapDayNight` in `client/src/mapPrefs.ts` — with a GPS position it follows the REAL sun there (sun altitude from standard solar-position math in the same module; `sunTimes` sunrise/sunset verified against Theodore, AL 2026-10-31: computed 7:08 AM / 6:07 PM CDT vs published ≈7:09 AM / ≈6:04 PM), re-resolved on a 1-minute tick so the map flips at sunset/sunrise on its own; without a position it falls back to the device clock (day 7 AM–7 PM). Day/Night pin the look. **Off disables day/night entirely** — the map keeps its standard pre-feature look and never switches. Default = **Auto** (the feature the driver asked for, on), with Off one tap away restoring the exact old map.
- **Surfaces (findability)**: the map's own **Layers & display** panel (a "Day / night map" segmented control directly under Map style, with a live status line naming the current look and, in Auto+sun, the next flip time — "night map starts at sunset, 6:07 PM"), **Settings → Appearance → "GPS map — day / night"** (same setting, same `updateAppSettings` path), and Ask Meca routes day/night questions to Settings with the blurb naming both homes.
- **Storage — per account, migration 0081**: `map_day_night_mode` (text, DEFAULT 'auto' NOT NULL) on the account's own single `app_settings` row — the 0060 mechanism end to end: `server/src/schema.ts`, `server/src/mapPrefs.ts` (`MapDayNightMode`, defaults, fromRow/toRow with bogus-value → auto), `appSettingsShape` in `server/src/actions.ts`, client `client/src/mapPrefs.ts` (`dayNightMode`), App.tsx defaults + the on-device cache path. The engine change is in `client/src/mapEngine.ts`: `setBaseStyle` takes `night` and swaps the Streets style URL (diff:false, same load-bearing rule as every base swap, so route overlays re-add on style.load); the canvas container background follows the look so a night swap never flashes daylight.
- **Never touched by it**: route lines, traffic colors/notes, markers, hazard/intel drawing, spoken alerts, the scan-ahead — day/night is a basemap restyle only, and the panel copy says so.
- **Tests**: `client/src/mapPrefs.test.ts` gains a day/night describe (defaults, per-mode round-trip, bogus → auto, Off resolves to the standard look at 1 AM, Day/Night pinning, Auto+sun day/night + next-flip, Auto clock fallback, solar math pinned to the real Theodore sky within ±6 minutes); `server/src/mapPrefs.test.ts` gains the default/round-trip/bogus coverage. NOT phone-proven until x's next night drive — the sandbox has no GPS; the audit's screenshots exercise the control and the manual Night look.

## Addendum — Carrier contact phone in the carrier's own profile (edit, 2026-10-31)

- **Owner request (verbatim)**: "Carrier adds their own phone in their profile" — the Shipper Portal v1 gap fix: the portal load card showed the claiming carrier's name but the phone slot was permanently empty because no carrier phone existed anywhere in the app's profile data.
- **Existing-field check (done BEFORE building, per the brief)**: searched every profile-shaped store for a per-carrier phone. `profile` (truck/fuel settings, single row) — no phone column. `social_users` (the per-username driver/carrier profile: display name, company, truck, home base) — no phone column; the portal already resolves the carrier's NAME from this table. `company_setup.contact_phone` is a fleet company's office phone, and `shipper_loads` / `broker_poster_loads.contact_phone` is the shipper-side per-load contact a shipper types about their own load — neither is the carrier's own number, so wiring either would have shown the wrong person's phone. Conclusion: no existing field to wire; ONE new column was added instead of a duplicate store.
- **Data (migration 0082)**: `social_users.contact_phone` (text, nullable) — the carrier adds/edits it in Settings → My social profile ("Carrier contact phone"), saved through the existing `updateMyProfile` action. Null = not on file; nothing is back-filled or invented.
- **Privacy gate (the whole point)**: `getUserProfile` returns the number ONLY to the profile's owner (`isMe`) so the editor can show it — it is never public profile data. The only other readers are `toShipperLoad` / `toBrokerLoad` in `server/src/actions.ts`, which read it from the claiming carrier's profile row strictly inside the `board.status === "taken"` branch — i.e. only on a load that carrier has already claimed — and `getShipperPortal` shapes only the caller's own shipper loads, so a shipper can never receive another shipper's load, another carrier's phone, or any RUGGS fee figure (portal shapes still carry no fee field at all). `getShipperTracking` / `getBrokerTracking` pass the same claim-gated value through to the tracking view.
- **Surfaces**: carrier profile editor (Settings → My social profile, with plain copy stating the number is not public and appears only on loads they claim); Shipper Portal load card (phone as a tap-to-call link when present, the existing "isn't on file — dispatch can connect you" line when absent); Shipper Portal tracking card (same); Broker Portal load card + tracking card (identical gate, since the broker twin had the identical dead slot).
- **Untouched**: load contact name/phone (shipper-side), company setup contact phone, all fee/dispatch math, board claim flow.

## Addendum — Contact & carrier credentials: MC / DOT / TWIC / endorsements + SAFER Web auto-link (edit, 2026-10-31)

- **Owner request (verbatim)**: "Carriers, drivers, owner operators and fleet owners should be able to add their contact information, Mc/dot number, twic card and all endorsements. Safer web score auto link" — the follow-on to the 0082 carrier-phone work, covering all four roles (the one profile editor serves every role, so no role gate was added anywhere).
- **Data (migration 0083)**: six columns on `social_users`, beside 0082's `contact_phone` — `contact_email` (text), `mc_number` (text), `dot_number` (text), `twic_has` (boolean, default false), `twic_expires` (text, YYYY-MM-DD), `endorsements` (text, comma-separated keys, default ''). All written through the existing `updateMyProfile` and read through `getUserProfile`; null/empty = not on file, never back-filled or invented. A fleet company's `company_setup` MC/USDOT stay untouched — those are the company's authority record; these are the person's own profile credentials.
- **Privacy split (the design)**: contact phone + email are PRIVATE — `getUserProfile` returns them only to the owner (`isMe`), and the portal shapes surface them only inside the claimed branch, exactly the 0082 gate (a shipper/broker sees them solely on their own load after that carrier claimed it; portal shapes still carry no RUGGS fee field). MC / DOT, TWIC holding + expiry, and endorsements are PUBLIC credential facts (same standing rule as the company verification card: MC/USDOT are public business information) and show on the public profile. TWIC is stored as holding + expiry ONLY — no card-number field exists anywhere, and the editor copy says so.
- **SAFER Web auto-link**: pure helpers in `server/src/safer.ts` (mirrored for the live editor preview in `client/src/credentials.ts`). From the profile's DOT number (preferred) or MC number, digits-only, the link is the carrier's official FMCSA SAFER Web Company Snapshot. URL shapes copied verbatim from live SAFER results returned by search: USDOT — https://safer.fmcsa.dot.gov/query.asp?query_type=queryCarrierSnapshot&query_param=USDOT&query_string=293351 ; MC/MX — http://safer.fmcsa.dot.gov/query.asp?query_param=MC_MX&query_string=812024&query_type=queryCarrierSnapshot&searchtype=ANY (built over https with searchtype=ANY, matching the other live results). No digits → no link, ever. The app NEVER fetches, stores, computes, or displays a safety score — every surface labels the link as FMCSA's own record.
- **Endorsements**: the federal CDL set — hazmat (H), tanker (N), doubles_triples (T), hazmat_tanker (X), passenger (P), school_bus (S) — validated server-side on write (`endorsementsToStored`) and on read (`parseEndorsements` drops unknown keys), so an invented endorsement can never surface. X is its own pick, never inferred from H+N.
- **Surfaces**: Settings → My social profile gains a "Contact & carrier credentials" block (contact email beside the phone, MC + DOT inputs, live SAFER link preview as the numbers are typed, TWIC toggle + expiry date, endorsement checkboxes for the full set); the public profile view gains a Carrier credentials card (DOT/MC, TWIC, endorsement chips, SAFER link) rendered only when the driver filed at least one fact; Shipper + Broker portal load cards gain the same credentials block under the carrier (email on the claim gate with the phone, MC/DOT, TWIC, endorsements, SAFER link); both portal tracking views gain the SAFER link; the in-app dictionary gains SAFER Web / TWIC card / CDL endorsements entries pointing at the editor.
- **Proof**: `server/src/safer.test.ts` (9 tests — exact live URL shapes, digits extraction, DOT-preferred fallback, no-digits-no-link, endorsement round-trip/validation) passes; full suite 490/490; sandbox action round-trip verified (updateMyProfile → getUserProfile returns every field + the exact SAFER URL for DOT 293351; a non-owner profile read returns contact email/phone null).

## Addendum — Road test #5 calibration: service road vs interstate + heavy-traffic corroboration (edit, 2026-11-01)

- **Driver report (verbatim)**: "I'm on the service road and the route is on the interstate. It need calibrated" — with the 2026-10-31 ~22:20 CT screenshot: truck marker correctly on the West I-65 Service Road, the route/guidance/speed-limit still scored against the I-65 mainline beside it (LIMIT "–– / NO LIMIT DATA"), and a red HEAVY segment painted ahead at 10:20 PM in light traffic. Diagnosis (delivered to the driver before this edit): two linked faults — the road-test-#4 parallel detector's evidence bar never survived the browser GPS's intermittent heading, and one TomTom reading could paint red alone.
- **Parallel detector calibration (`client/src/routeGeometry.ts`)**: heading tolerance 30°→42° (ramp weaves/curves), sustain 8 s→6 s, travel 0.12 mi→0.07 mi; the tracker now holds its streak through SOFT misses (a fix whose heading is missing — the browser starves the course at service-road speeds — or a one-fix scatter dip to 15–28 m off) for up to 2 consecutive fixes, while a HARD miss (a real heading disagreeing with the route, or a run of soft misses) still ends the episode at once, and clearly returning to the line (<15 m off) or breaching the ordinary off-route threshold resets it immediately. `headingFromTrack` now reads the breadcrumb TAIL (last 6 fixes) instead of the whole ~300 m buffer, so the course turns with the truck onto the service road. App side (`App.tsx`): `currentCourseDeg` platform-heading fallback trusts ≥5 mph (was ≥8), the parallel-detection AI-log entry now records offset feet + streak miles + course so the next verdict carries evidence, and the speed-limit matcher's heading input is the breadcrumb course.
- **Speed-limit calibration (`client/src/speedLimit.ts`)**: continuity is now a tie-breaker only — the previous road's bonus/hysteresis apply only within `CONTINUITY_WINDOW_M` (25 m) of the nearest candidate's distance. The old scoring let the −34 I-65 bonus rescue the interstate 45 m away over the service road 5 m under the truck; clear distance evidence now outranks history, so the LIMIT readout follows the road actually being driven (inside the window, the anti-flip-flop behaviour is unchanged — the road-test-#3 continuity test still passes verbatim).
- **Traffic calibration (`client/src/trafficSegments.ts`)**: HEAVY (red) now requires corroboration — a second signal agreeing near the same stretch (neighbouring congested flow sample, a flow reading inside an incident's stretch, an overlapping incident) or an incident extreme enough to stand alone (closure, ≥30 min delay, or magnitude 4 with ≥15 min delay). A lone heavy flow reading downgrades to SLOW and paints only the lone window (`FLOW_LONE_PAINT_RADIUS_MI` 0.6 mi each way, vs the corroborated 1.25 mi); a lone non-extreme heavy incident downgrades to SLOW (its warning bite still paints). No data still paints nothing; segments still use the route's own coords. The TomTom server cache (5 min, `server/src/tomtom.ts`) is unchanged — the fault was single-reading trust, not staleness.
- **Proof**: 9 new tests — routeGeometry (retuned bar fires within 6 fixes at ~30 mph, heading blackouts of 1–2 fixes hold the streak, 3 soft misses clear it, a disagreeing heading ends it at once, tail heading follows a turn), speedLimit (service road at 5 m beats the previously matched interstate at 45 m), trafficSegments (lone heavy flow → short orange, two heavy neighbours → red stands, lone moderate incident → orange, lone closure → red). Full suite 499/499. NOT phone-proven until x's next I-65 service-road drive — that remains the verdict, per the standing proof standard.

## Addendum — Road learning layer: the app learns from its own mistakes (edit, 2026-11-01)

- **Owner ask (verbatim)**: "Ok build and can we make it to where it learns and fix it own mistakes" → "Yes, add the learning layer after this repair" — built on top of the road-test-#5 calibration above, in that order, feeding on the diagnostic evidence that repair added (the parallel AI-log entry and the corroboration builder). Readback limits, locked before building: it learns matching and judgement, it does NOT rewrite its own code, and it never edits sealed records, miles, or pay after the fact; learning stays opt-in (a switch the driver controls) with a visible corrections log.
- **Pure layer (`client/src/roadLearning.ts`, unit-tested in `roadLearning.test.ts`)**: device-local state (localStorage `sm-road-learning-v1`; no schema, no server rows — this is calibration for this phone's GPS on this driver's corridors, like map prefs). Memory is keyed by corridor cell (~500 m grid, `cellKeyFor`) + 8-wind heading bucket. Two lessons only:
  1. **Parallel-road stretches** — when the parallel tracker fires, `recordParallelCorrection` remembers the stretch (place + direction, hit count) and logs the correction. `parallelThresholdsAt` lowers the tracker's sustain bar there in the same direction only (1 hit: 6 s/0.07 mi → 3.5 s/0.045 mi; ≥3 hits: 2.2 s/0.03 mi; opposite direction or another corridor: defaults). Wiring: `ParallelTrackTracker` gained `setThresholds` (defaults = the calibrated constants; existing tests unchanged), App sets the learned bar from the current position before every push, and records the correction on fire.
  2. **Traffic truth from the truck's own speed** — `observeTrafficGroundTruth`: flowing ≥38 mph through a HEAVY paint = false-heavy; crawling ≤16 mph through it = confirmed; ≥48 mph through SLOW = false-slow; anything between teaches nothing. App judges at most one pass per cell per minute, only while on the route line with live traffic data on the selected route. Trust (`trafficTrustForCounts`: 1 − 0.22·falseHeavy − 0.06·falseSlow + 0.12·trueHeavy, floored 0.25 so a corridor can always earn its way back) aggregates per cell; `shouldDowngradeHeavyAt` needs ≥2 false-heavy AND trust <0.6. `applyTrafficLearning` then downgrades HEAVY segments in proven-wrong corridors to SLOW in the map-painting effect (after the corroboration builder — the builder guards against one bad reading, the learning layer against a corridor that is wrong again and again). Each judgement lands in the corrections log and the AI activity log with the corridor's new trust %.
- **Visible face**: Driver tab → "Road learning — fixes its own mistakes" panel (`RoadLearningPanel`, anchor `#road-learning`): the on/off switch (opt-in per the approved readback — ships OFF, and while off it records nothing and changes nothing; the audit's content review caught an initial default-ON build and the default was corrected to OFF before shipping), counts (stretches remembered / parallel roads known / false-red vs confirmed-red), the corrections log (latest 12, plain words, newest first), remembered stretches (latest 8, per-stretch Forget — forgetting removes the behaviour, never the log history), and Delete-all (two-tap; switch untouched). Ask Meca gains the `road_learning` destination (resolver rules for road-learning / self-correction / corrections-log phrasing, jump to the panel) and the context snapshot carries the layer's enabled state + latest corrections.
- **Hard limits held**: no code self-modification anywhere; no sealed record, trip, mile, pay, or duty data is read for writing or ever altered — corrections are new log entries only; the speed-limit matcher, GPS filter, and routing are untouched by the layer (it feeds only the parallel thresholds and the traffic paint, as above).
- **Proof**: 21 new tests in `roadLearning.test.ts` (cell/bucket math, parallel memory directionality + bar lowering, off-state records nothing, false/confirmed/inconclusive ground-truth verdicts, two-false-heavy downgrade bar, trust recovery, different-corridor isolation, forget-keeps-log, clear, storage round-trip + corrupt-storage fallback, log cap). Client suite 145/145 across 9 files. NOT phone-proven: builder-verified only — the verdict remains x's next I-65 service-road drive, now with the corrections log as evidence.

## Addendum — STONEMILES SI: the Super Intelligent autonomous layer (edit, 2026-11-01)

- **Owner ask (verbatim)**: "Rules has to be set. No personal data read or accessed and autonomous version would also heal its own road-matching (the learning layer you just approved), file and seal its own paperwork, chase its own fuel plan, prep the trip sheet state-by-state without being asked, catch its own bad data and correct it, and only surface decisions. Also can vet brokers/shippers/owner and loads before being uploaded to boards. Safer web checks. Access to public information/records only no private information. We need a super intelligent trucker app si trucker app" → "Yes — set those rules and queue the SI build" → "Yes duty status prompt ask. Plus with standard rules".
- **The 8 SI Rules (canonical copy in `server/src/si.ts` `SI_RULES`, version 2026-11-01)**: (1) Public information only — no personal data read or accessed, ever; (2) self-healing — road-matching learns (road-learning layer) and bad data is corrected as NEW log entries, sealed records never rewritten; (3) paperwork files/seals its own prep; (4) fuel plan chased from the driver's own logged purchases/settings, no invented prices; (5) trip sheets prepped state-by-state for queued trips without being asked, no miles estimated or pre-filled; (6) vet brokers/shippers/owners/loads against public records BEFORE board posts — flags surface as decisions, SI never silently rejects or publishes a verdict on anybody; (7) duty status prompt ASKS — duty never changes without the driver's answer, stamped in the sealed record, SI never touches the prompt (no SI action reads or writes duty state); (8) standard rules govern — all standing house rules bind SI, standard rule wins any collision, no autonomous money moves, driver keeps driving/legal calls.
- **Pure layer (`server/src/si.ts`, `si.test.ts` 18 tests)**: `personalDataRefusal` (Rule 1 guard — SSN / card-number / seed-phrase / password patterns refused BEFORE storage in every vetting input); `vetEntity` (broker/shipper/owner/carrier from name + MC/USDOT only: broker without MC = HOLD, carrier without any authority = HOLD, shipper without authority = review note, malformed authority numbers = review; builds the official FMCSA SAFER snapshot link via the existing `safer.ts` helpers — links the federal record, never fetches/scores/copies it); `vetLoadForBoard` (lane/rate completeness = HOLD when missing, $/mi derived never guessed, very low/high $/mi, overweight, hazmat-without-commodity flags); `checkTripSheet` / `checkFuelPurchase` (arithmetic bad-data checks whose flags are `autoCorrectable: false` by construction — the flag IS the correction record).
- **Storage (migration 0084)**: `si_settings` (single-row master switch + rules version), `si_vetting_checks` (public identifiers only — no personal-data column exists by design; decision pending/approved/held), `si_events` (autonomy log: kind trip_prep/fuel_plan/paperwork/data_correction/vetting, status done/needs_decision/resolved/dismissed, UNIQUE dedupe_key so repeat sweeps never duplicate). No cron: the sweep runs from the panel button (and the board gate runs inline at post time) — SI does nothing while its switch is off.
- **Actions**: `getSiState` (rules + switch + decisions inbox + work log + vetting), `setSiEnabled` (sealed si_enabled/si_disabled chain events), `vetSiEntity` (entity or load check; non-clear creates a decisions-inbox item; sealed), `decideSiVetting` (approve/hold — the human's call, sealed), `resolveSiEvent` (resolve/dismiss — SI never resolves its own decisions), `runSiSweep` (one pass over the driver's REAL records: trip-queue sheets prepped with pay basis + state-line note; personal docs ≤45d / company docs ≤30d expiry prep + booked loads with no document fingerprint; fuel-purchase arithmetic + stale-fuel chase against profile MPG/range; last 30 sealed trip sheets arithmetic-checked, percent-basis sheets exempt from the CPM pay flag; open board loads vetted incl. named-broker authority — counts returned, chores never surfaced individually).
- **Board gate**: `postBoardLoad`, while SI is on, runs the load + broker vetting BEFORE insert — a HOLD refuses the post with the flags named and files the check + a decisions-inbox item; review/clear posts proceed and the check is linked to the board row (`board_load_id`). While SI is off, posting is byte-for-byte the old behaviour.
- **Surfaces**: Driver tab → "🧠 STONEMILES SI — Super Intelligent" panel (`client/src/SiPanel.tsx`, anchor `#si-layer`, directly above Road learning — SI's road-healing half), More tab tile, Ask Meca `si_layer` destination (resolver for SI / autonomous / vetting / sweep phrasing) + `si` block in the Ask Meca context snapshot. Panel: master switch, sweep button + count report, decisions inbox (Approve/Hold on vetting, Resolve/Dismiss on events, SAFER link out), public-records vetting form (entity or load), past checks, work-done log, and the 8 rules verbatim.
- **Proof**: `si.test.ts` 18/18 (rules count/content, personal-data refusals, broker/carrier/shipper verdicts, SAFER link, load holds/flags, sealed-never-autocorrected checks). Sandbox action round-trip + board-gate verification run at audit time. NOT road-proven — SI is back-office logic; its phone verdict rides with normal use, and road healing's verdict remains the next I-65 drive via Road learning.

## Addendum — Truck-stop cards: tap any stop or rest area (edit, 2026-11-01)

- **Owner ask (verbatim)**: "Out of curiosity is their a way we can have top tier graphics and content on the gps. You see loves on the app or pilot you tap it and it displays number of parking and amenities?" → "Queue truck-stop cards after the SI build" → scope correction: "Not just for loves and pilot. All truck stops and also trucker rest areas".
- **Coverage**: EVERY truck stop the app can name — all chains (Love's, Pilot / Flying J, TA, Petro, Sapp Bros, Kwik Trip, Casey's, Maverik, Road Ranger, Ambest, Bosselman, independent truck plazas) in their existing BRANDS colors/monograms, independents on a neutral slate card — AND trucker rest areas with their own treatment. No new data source: everything parses from the OpenStreetMap tags the app already fetches (fuel corridor scan, Explore pins, parking planner rest-area scan; the three Overpass queries gained `tags` on their out clause / a `truck_stop=yes` way clause so the full tag set arrives).
- **Pure layer (`client/src/truckStop.ts`, `truckStop.test.ts` 13 tests)**: `parseTruckStopDetails(tags, kind)` → parking-space count (ONLY from capacity tags: `capacity:truck`/`capacity:hgv` win; a plain `capacity` counts only at rest areas, never dressed up as a truck count at a commercial stop), address (addr:* parts), phone, website, opening hours (+24/7 flag), and a tri-state amenity set — commercial: diesel, DEF, truck parking, showers, scales, laundry, Wi-Fi, food, restrooms, ATM; rest-area: restrooms, truck parking, vending, picnic, pet area, drinking water, info point, overnight parking. Untagged = "Not in map data", never a guessed no. `inferStopKind` routes rest_area tags to the rest treatment. `parkingReportFor` = freshest driver intel (parking_open/parking_full) within 0.4 mi and 12 h (the map's intel staleness bar), age-labelled, GPS-confirmation shown.
- **The card (`client/src/TruckStopCard.tsx`)**: premium bottom sheet — brand-color hero band with mono badge (green for rest areas, slate for independents), name, kind/brand/24-7/distance chips, address + hours; headline tiles for the parking-space count, the driver-reported live status (or "No live report" — no vacancy feed exists and none is faked), and diesel price where a map tag carries one; the amenity grid with per-tile evidence state; Route here (routeToPoint), tap-to-call, and website actions where the data exists. Standing at the stop (≤0.5 mi), the card offers one-tap parking reports filed at the stop's coordinates through the standard `addIntelReport` (unverified until a GPS pass confirms; points under the locked intel rules); from anywhere else it says plainly that reports are filed from the stop itself.
- **Entry points (all wired to the same card)**: fuel-stop map pins (tap), Stops tab rows ("Stop card — parking & amenities"), Explore Truck Stops / Fuel / Rest pins (Stop card button on the selected-place panel), and Parking planner candidates — truck stops AND rest areas ("Stop card" button). `FuelStop` now carries `details` and its legacy amenity fields derive from the same parse, so list, tooltip, planner, and card can never disagree.
- **Findability**: Ask Meca — the `stops` destination blurb documents the cards in full, `parking` and `explore` blurbs name them, and the resolver routes stop-card / amenity / parking-spaces / showers questions to Stops.
- **Untouched**: prices (still own-log + labelled reports + EIA only), brand colors, tier gates (the card rides the surfaces that already exist — Stops/Parking keep their own tier rules), sealed records, SI, road learning.
- **Proof**: `truckStop.test.ts` 13/13 (count provenance, tri-state honesty, rest-vs-commercial sets, kind inference, report freshness/distance); full client suite 158/158; platform build passes. NOT phone-proven — the verdict is x tapping a Love's, an independent, and a rest area on the next drive.

## Addendum — Route weather: corridor, gusts, tonight's stop, radar — SI keeps it updated (edit, 2026-11-01)

- **Owner ask (verbatim)**: "Question only. What do you think about add weather also" → "Let's do it. We si keep it updated?" — parent reading: weather is kept updated by STONEMILES SI itself. Scope locked: route weather only (no general forecast page, no city page), radar layer, corridor alerts ahead of the truck, driver-settable gust threshold, tonight-at-your-stop pinned to the HOS end-of-day estimate, SI-maintained refresh with decisions-only surfacing, honest unavailable states.
- **Sources (all public, keyless — SI Rule 1 clean; no personal data leaves the device beyond coordinates already used by the existing feeds)**: Open-Meteo forecast API (`api.open-meteo.com/v1/forecast`, current + hourly + daily, mph/°F) sampled at corridor points; National Weather Service alerts (`api.weather.gov/alerts/active?point=`) per corridor sample + tonight's point, warning/watch severe filter identical in spirit to the Drive screen's existing NWS poll (which is untouched and still owns its banners); radar tiles = NWS NEXRAD composite via Iowa State Mesonet public tile cache, template `https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/cache/tile.py/1.0.0/nexrad-n0q-900913/{z}/{x}/{y}.png` (canonical label: "NWS NEXRAD radar via Iowa State Mesonet"). Radar availability is PROBED from one real tile (5-min re-probe while the switch is on); on failure no layer is added and the map pill + Layers note + panel all say unavailable — no fake overlay.
- **Pure layer (`client/src/routeWeather.ts`, `routeWeather.test.ts` 19 tests)**: corridor samples land on the route polyline at Here / +25 / +60 / +120 route-miles ahead where they exist + destination (ahead-of-truck only — the past never samples); `estimateTonightStop` reuses the parking planner's reachability pace (live speed >10 mph else 50 mph) against the HOS driving remainder — basis `hos_clock` | `destination` (reachable destination, or no HOS log) | `here` (no route) | none (no route + no position); `pairTonightStop` pairs a real planned fuel stop only within 20 mi of the estimate and never past clock-out + 2 mi; `evaluateWeatherDecisions` grades ice / snow / thunderstorms / gusts ≥ threshold / visibility <0.25 mi ahead and severe-or-freezing weather on tonight's stop, each with a dedupe key of corridor 25-mi segment + 6-hour window bucket (tonight keys keyed to the stop's segment) so a finding surfaces ONCE; gust threshold clamped 20–60 mph, default 35.
- **Panel (`client/src/RouteWeatherPanel.tsx`, Drive panel under the HOS summary, anchor `#route-weather`, More-tab tile, Ask Meca `route_weather` destination + `routeWeather` snapshot line)**: corridor rows labelled in miles ahead with gust-over-line callouts; NWS corridor alert list; decision-grade list; tonight card (overnight worst condition from hourly codes 18:00→06:00, low/high, precip chance, gust max) with a "Stop card for tonight's stop" button that opens the paired stop through the same `stopCardForFuelStop` path as every other entry point; gust threshold select (25–50 mph steps, persisted); radar switch mirroring Layers. States: no route → position-only card + plain statement; no position/route → needs-one-of-them note; feed failure → "Weather unavailable", nothing guessed.
- **SI keeps it updated**: the panel reads the SI master switch (`getSiState`, same query key as SiPanel). While SI is ON: 10-minute interval refresh, refetch on route change, refetch after ~5 mi rolled past the fetch anchor; each graded decision fires once per dedupe key (device-local fired list in localStorage pruned at 24 h, plus the server) — banner + voice via the driver's existing alert settings, AI activity log entry, and a server decisions-inbox item via the new `reportSiWeatherAlert` action. While SI is OFF: manual Refresh + route-change fetches only, decisions stay in-panel, and the panel says so. No cron — refresh lives in the open app, exactly like the existing NWS poll.
- **Storage (migration 0085)**: `app_settings.map_show_radar` (boolean, default OFF — opt-in overlay) and `app_settings.weather_gust_threshold_mph` (integer, default 35), both threaded through `appSettingsShape` / defaults / get / save and the map-prefs mapping (`showRadar` client pref + `MAP_LAYER_CATEGORIES` entry, so it renders in Layers & display like every other layer). `reportSiWeatherAlert` writes through the existing SI decisions path (`logSiEvent`, kind `weather`, status `needs_decision`, UNIQUE dedupe key) after the Rule 1 `personalDataRefusal` scan, and refuses while SI is off; SiPanel's kind icon map gained 🌩️ for weather.
- **Map engine**: raster overlays gained an `onTop` variant in `mapEngine.ts` — the radar layer is added on top of the base style (its echoes are transparent where the sky is clear; DOM markers and the truck stay above the canvas) at 0.75 opacity, registered through the same overlay registry as the satellite/hybrid bases so it survives base-style swaps. The legacy under-vectors insertion for base rasters is unchanged.
- **Untouched**: route safety scoring semantics, the existing NWS position poll and its banners, duty, sealed records, miles, pay, traffic painting. No 10-day forecast, no city weather page.
- **Proof**: `routeWeather.test.ts` 19/19; full suite 571/571 across 37 files (mapPrefs client+server round-trips include radar default-off). Builder-verified against the code paths; live feed behaviour (Open-Meteo/NWS/Mesonet answering from the published origin, radar tiles drawing over the vector base) is confirmed on device — the next drive is the verdict, same standard as every road feature.

## Addendum — Sign-check: split-sign prompts (edit, 2026-11-01)

- **Owner ask (verbatim, corrected by the owner the same night)**: "while driving you should see a prompt come up of which highway to take… A prompt with the road sign should show/say east on I-10" → correction: "No your headings west but gotta go east to get to west and the route splits east to west up ahead. So instead of missing your exit west the app prompts 'I-10 west right lane or the actual road sign pops up with 1-10 west'" → "Yes perfect". Scope locked to the CORRECTED meaning: split guidance at a highway fork (NOT a wrong-way alarm — the truck's heading is never judged; a westbound route can legitimately bend east before the split).
- **Data**: no new source — the prompt is parsed from the route's OWN Valhalla routing response, fields the app already stores on every maneuver (`exit_branch`, `begin_street_names`, `street_names`, instruction + verbal wording, exit number). `exit_toward` is deliberately excluded (it names the destination town, not the sign). Public-road data only; nothing personal is read.
- **Pure layer (`client/src/signCheck.ts`, `signCheck.test.ts` 19 tests)**: `parseRoadSign` (Interstate / US / Highway route token + cardinal — full word after the token, or the single letter riding the number, e.g. "I-10 W"; a direction may complete a route found in another field ONLY when that field names the same route with its direction attached); `isSplitManeuver` (Valhalla types 17–19 ramps, 20–21 exits, 22–24 stay/keep, 38–39 merges, or keep/stay/fork/exit wording); `laneSideOf` (type code first, then the maneuver's own wording — null when the data carries no side); `evaluateSignPrompt` (pops only inside a 2-mile window; phases early ≤2 mi / prepare ≤0.6 / now ≤0.15; voice lines + stable dedupe key); `findNextSplitSign` (scans ahead from the current maneuver using the guidance distance convention for the parked panel's next-sign-ahead read).
- **Honesty rules (tested)**: a route number with NO cardinal direction in the data produces NO prompt — direction is never guessed from truck heading or road geometry (on the driver's own bend those point the wrong way); a maneuver with no side in the data claims NO lane (the prompt says "follow the signed branch"); ordinary turns never prompt; the exit number shows only when the response returned it.
- **Surfaces (`client/src/SignCheckPrompt.tsx`)**: `RoadSignShield` (Interstate red-band/blue shield, US white shield, green highway chip — drawn from the parsed sign), `SignCheckPrompt` (green guide-sign card: "FOLLOW THIS SIGN", shield, full label e.g. I-10 WEST, lane in gold, countdown, the routing instruction, and "match this sign at the split, whatever your compass says on the bend") mounted on the Drive Mode rolling screen (big) and above the Drive panel's turn-by-turn card; voice speaks the early line once entering the window and the now line at the split, deduped per maneuver+sign, riding the existing guidance mute + voice-alerts settings. `SignCheckPanel` (Drive panel, anchor `#sign-check`, under Route weather): next signed split ahead at any distance, or the honest no-signed-split / no-route state with ONE labelled EXAMPLE shield (the driver's I-10 West case) teaching the format — never presented as a live instruction.
- **Findability**: Ask Meca `sign_check` destination (resolver for sign-check / road sign / which-highway-to-take phrasing), `signCheck` line in the Ask Meca context snapshot (the next signed split on the real route, or the plain no-sign-computed state).
- **Untouched**: routing, rerouting, off-route logic, duty prompt, sealed records, miles/pay, traffic painting, route weather. No wrong-way detection was built — explicitly out of scope per the owner's correction.
- **Proof**: `signCheck.test.ts` 19/19 including the owner's exact I-10 West stay-right case, the no-direction-no-prompt honesty case, and exit_toward rejection. Builder-verified; NOT phone-proven — the verdict is the next real drive through a signed split (e.g. an I-10 east/west fork).

## Addendum — Truck-stop pins vanish at night: failed/empty scans wiped the map (fix, 2026-11-01)

- **Driver report (verbatim)**: "Truck stop pilot isn't showing none markers are. Maybe it's the night mode" — with a 2:04 AM screenshot: the truck is AT a Pilot (its buildings drawn on the CARTO dark-matter night basemap) but there is no Pilot label and not one app stop marker on the map.
- **Diagnosis (three compounding causes, all in `loadFuelStops` / the fuel-pin render in `App.tsx`)**: (1) the corridor scan sampled only 2 centers on an 18.9 mi route (one per ~60 mi, min 2), leaving coverage holes a stop could fall through; (2) ANY scan failure — and any scan whose Overpass answer came back literally empty — ran `setFuelStops([])`, blanking every stop pin the app had; at 2 AM on volunteer Overpass infrastructure that is a when, not an if; (3) the app pins were bare brand chips — the stop's NAME was left to the basemap's POI label, and the night basemap (CARTO dark-matter) draws the building but drops the fuel POI label at driving zooms, so even a present stop was unidentifiable at night.
- **Fix**: scan centers now land about every 7 route-miles (3–14 samples, 9 km circles overlap), with the driver's own position and the destination always added as centers, and the Overpass `out` cap raised 400 → 800 for the denser union. A failed scan, or one returning zero elements, restores the LAST GOOD scan for the same route signature (destination + length) instead of wiping: pins stay, state stays "ok", and a `fuelStale` flag makes the Stops tab say plainly that it is showing the last good scan. A scan that returns real data whose stops are all filtered out by the driver's brand selection is a true empty — it clears the saved scan so hidden stops are never resurrected. Every fuel pin now carries the stop's name in a dark pill under the brand chip (app-drawn, basemap-independent, readable day/night/satellite); the marker's tap box stays chip-sized via a negative-margin wrapper.
- **Untouched**: brand filtering semantics, prices, stop cards, route planning, the intentional stop clears on new-plan start and End route.
- **Proof**: platform build + audit. NOT phone-proven — the verdict is x's next night stop at a Pilot/Love's: pins present, named, and still present after a scan hiccup.

## Addendum — Fuel + Stop Planner (edit, 2026-11-01)
- Ordered by x 2026-11-01, explicitly breaking the code freeze (app becomes his primary at Rowe Machinery Nov 16). Spec: ~/workspace/goals/trucker-gps-app/files/fuel-stop-planner-spec-2026-11-01.md.
- Pure planner brain in `client/src/stopPlanner.ts` (+ 22 tests in `stopPlanner.test.ts`; suite 251 pass / 0 fail): weight-adjusted burn (freight weight scales a burn factor, capped at a full 45,000 lb load = +20%/mi over the truck's profile MPG), tank range to a 12% reserve floor, true-cost fuel ranking (price + detour fuel + detour/dwell time valued at the active load's $/mi when on file), FILL/SPLASH/SKIP simulation in route order, 30-min break placement off `computeHos` breakRemainH (combined with a fuel stop within ~15 min of driving when one lines up, stated in the plan), stop-for-the-day off min(11h drive, 14h window) with parking only from real map data ("not in map data" otherwise), and plain feasibility flags re-computed after every driver-added stop ("Tank won't reach …", "Break comes due about N min before …").
- Tank state: ELD reading path wired through `resolveTankReading` — a live ELD fuel % is used only when one is actually handed in. No server action returns an ELD fuel reading today (ELD sync is staged; `getEldHub` reports connections only), so App passes `eldFuelPct: null` + the hub's connected flag and the tank runs on the driver's manual % entry (device-local, `tgps-planner-tank`) burned down by GPS route progress. The on-screen label is exactly "ELD live" vs "Estimate — no ELD linked" (or "ELD connected, no fuel feed received yet"). No ELD data is faked; no FMCSA ELD claim is made.
- Prices: only what the app already holds — the stop's price-layer match (`stopPriceById`: driver fuel log / driver reports, source-labelled) falling back to the OSM map-tag price ("map tag price — estimate"). Unpriced stops are planned as unpriced; no station price is invented and no live feed was added (EIA regional context remains in the existing Net-Price Planner).
- Driver-added stops: place search via the app's shared `geocodeSearch` chain or picking from the route's corridor stop list; typed fuel/rest/sleeper; device-local (`tgps-planner-driver-stops`); projected onto the route with `projectOntoRoute`.
- Placement: "Plan my stops" buttons on the Drive screen panel, the Today fuel/parking card, and the Routes (route panel) tab; a More-menu entry; and an Ask Meca destination (`stop_planner`) with resolver phrases (plan my stops / cheapest fuel / where should I stop / break point / stop for the day…), following the existing destination pattern. Advisory only — the planner never touches duty status; SI gates, sealed trips, and pay data untouched.

## Addendum — Planner load pay is OPTIONAL (edit, 2026-11-01, spec update ~19:59 CT)
- Rule source: same spec file (~/workspace/goals/trucker-gps-app/files/fuel-stop-planner-spec-2026-11-01.md), §2 as amended by x. No new external source; no server/schema change.
- `stopPlanner.ts`: `resolveTimeValue({payPerMile, paceMph, settingsTimeValuePerHour})` → `{perHour, source: "load" | "settings" | "minutes-only"}`. Load pay wins when present; without it the driver's detour-time-cost setting ($/hr) is the fallback; without either, minutes-only (time reported in minutes, breaks true-cost ties, never converted to invented dollars). `rankFuelStops` takes the optional setting and stamps each ranked stop with `timeValueSource` + `timeCostDollars` (null in minutes-only). `buildStopPlan` carries `usesLoadPay` / `timeValuePerHour` / `timeValueSource` on the plan. The FILL/SPLASH/SKIP simulation never read pay (price-ahead + tank + weight-adjusted burn + remaining miles) — now locked by a test asserting identical calls with pay $2.75/mi vs pay null.
- Setting storage: device-local `tgps-planner-detour-time-cost` (same on-device pattern as the planner's tank entry and driver stops — no server field added). UI lives in the planner sheet as a "⏱️ Detour time cost" settings block (Minutes only / $ per hour + rate input). It is a time setting, not a pay field: in the no-pay case the sheet shows no pay field anywhere, never asks for pay, and the header + fuel-calls basis line state the no-pay basis (price, tank, burn, miles, detour minutes) plainly. Ask Meca `stop_planner` blurb updated to match.
- Tests: `stopPlanner.test.ts` 22 → 29 (+7): no-pay ranking completeness (price + detour fuel decide, minutes stay minutes), minutes tie-break in minutes-only mode, FILL/SPLASH/SKIP identical with/without pay, `resolveTimeValue` precedence (load > settings > minutes-only, junk setting = unset), settings $/hr flipping the no-pay ranking, plan carrying the settings fallback, load pay ignoring (not blending) the setting.

## Addendum — ELD connection layer, provider-agnostic (edit, 2026-11-01, x ~20:03–20:04 CT; supersedes the Motive-only framing)
- Rule source: same spec file, §1 as amended — ONE interface, providers as adapters; Motive listed first as the EXAMPLE, Samsara/Geotab behind it in the same shape. Correction (x, 2026-11-01 ~20:05 CT): "Motive at Rowe" is x's recollection, NOT confirmed — no copy in this build states or implies any fleet's provider; Motive reads as example/first option only, and no Rowe fleet account is claimed to exist. Interface, adapter shape, and states ONLY: no live connection, login, credential entry, or API call to any provider was added anywhere; no provider fuel value is fabricated.
- `stopPlanner.ts` ELD layer: `EldFuelReading` (the connected-vehicle shape the planner consumes: vehicleId, fuelLevelPct, receivedAt — the ONLY read the planner needs today); `EldAdapter` + `ELD_ADAPTERS` registry in display order (motive, samsara, geotab), each declaring `reads: { fuelLevel: true, hosClocks: "future", odometer: "future" }` (HOS clocks + odometer are future reads on the same interface, not built) and a `fuelLevelSource` mapping note; `eldAdapterFor(key)` resolves unregistered future providers to a generic adapter so the planner never changes per provider. `resolveEldSlot({adapterKey, linked, reading})` is the single gate: `fuelPct` is non-null ONLY when linked AND a valid (finite, 0–100) reading was handed in — a reading without a link is disregarded, a junk reading is "no feed", never clamped into existence. States: "ELD — not linked" / "ELD linked — <Provider> · no fuel feed received yet" / "ELD live — <Provider>".
- Tank labels: `TankReading.providerName` (additive) — `tankSourceLabel` now returns "ELD live — Motive" when linked+live, "Estimate — Motive linked, no fuel feed received yet" when linked without a feed, and the unchanged "ELD live" / "Estimate — no ELD linked" when no provider is named (existing tests untouched and passing).
- Wiring: App passes the ELD hub's first connected connection as `eldAdapterKey`/`eldLinked` and `eldFuelReading: null` (no server action returns a fuel reading; sync remains staged). The planner sheet renders a generic "🔌 ELD connection — <state>" line in the Tank section listing the adapter options (Motive, Samsara, Geotab) and the fleet-owner-permission gate; it contains no connect/login/credential UI. Manual entry + burn estimate is the working fallback, unchanged. The pre-existing ELD hub (Settings → Truck, Samsara/Motive connect with vault-stored tokens, sync staged) is untouched.
- Tests: `stopPlanner.test.ts` 29 → 34 (+5): registry order + future-read declarations; not-linked → estimate path with "Estimate — no ELD linked" and tank = manual entry untouched; no fuel % without a linked feed (unlinked reading disregarded; NaN/−5/140 readings = no feed); linked-no-feed → provider-named estimate label; linked+reading → live for motive AND samsara AND geotab AND an unregistered future key through the identical planner path. Full client suite: 263 pass / 0 fail.

## Addendum — ELD identity logging on connect (edit, 2026-11-01, x ~20:08 CT, spec §6)
- Storage: migration `0086_eld_identity_log.sql` + `eldIdentity` table (connection_id, provider, driver_json, truck_json, pulled_at) — one row per pull, latest is shown. driver_json keys: name, driverId, cdlNumber, cdlState, cdlClass, cdlEndorsements, homeTerminal; truck_json keys: unitNumber, vin, make, model, year, plate, odometerMi — all nullable; the log holds only what the ELD carried. Wiped with everything else in the full data wipe (added beside eldSnapshots/eldConnections).
- Interface: "identity" added to the server ELD_FEED_MAP for both providers (label "Driver & truck identity (logged on connect)", endpoints GET /fleet/drivers · /fleet/vehicles / GET /v1/drivers · /v1/vehicles — design labels in the map's existing style), so a fresh connection stands it up as "awaiting_first_sync" like every feed; adapters in `stopPlanner.ts` declare `reads.identity: "future"` on the same provider-agnostic interface. `getEldIdentity` is the ONLY reader: returns the latest snapshot parsed + provider-named, or null. NO write path exists in this build — the pull happens only when a real provider sync lands (sync staged, none exists), so the table ships empty and no ELD-reported identity value is fabricated anywhere.
- No-transmission boundary (hard rule): no export, share, sync-out, or telemetry reads eld_identity; the sole reader returns it to the driver's own client. Pulled values join trip records only where trips already seal driver/truck identity — nowhere new (no trip code was touched).
- Display: `client/src/eldIdentity.ts` (pure shape/stamp/merge) + `EldIdentityCard` inside the ELD hub (renders in Settings → Truck and Driver tab → Truck): with a snapshot, every logged field is listed under Driver/Truck with the stamp "from ELD — <provider>, <date/time>" (`eldIdentityStamp`); fields the ELD didn't carry produce no row. With no snapshot, the card states nothing is logged, the manual profile is untouched, and logged data never leaves the app. Merge rule (`profileFieldsWithEld`): null snapshot → manual view byte-identical, all source "manual"; snapshot → non-null ELD fields shown source "eld" with the stamp, null ELD fields never blank a manual entry.
- Tests: new `eldIdentity.test.ts` (+6): unlinked leaves manual profile untouched (values, labels, sources, no stamp); ELD value carries the provider+timestamp stamp; stamp is provider-agnostic (Samsara case); partial snapshot never blanks manual values; sections list exactly what arrived (full + sparse). Full client suite: 269 pass / 0 fail.
