## Head unit build + roadmap removal (2026-11-07, x's orders — "Let build head unit" / "Don't put roadmap stuff on the app")

- **Head unit (built):** `HeadUnitDisplay` in `CarModePanel.tsx` — the full three-screen head-unit surface, full-screen in-app: **Guidance** (the existing carReadout), **Stops ahead** (`headUnitStops` in carMode.ts — the corridor's real `fuelStops` from App state: route-miles remaining on a route via `alongMi - progressMi`, straight-line `haversineMi` off-route; facts only from OSM-sourced fields — diesel price, truckParking/showers/scales only when `true`, off-route miles; behind/unmeasured stops dropped; cap 4 rolling / 6 parked per the distraction rule), **Road alerts** (`headUnitAlerts` + `hosAlert` — the real `hazards` list by straight-line distance, driver-hidden weigh stations excluded via `placeSuppressed`, plus the drive-clock row only when HOS < 30 min and the HOS switch is on; cap 3, restrictions + dying clock urgent). Opens from a new 🖥️ Head unit button on the rolling Drive screen and from the Settings panel; tapping a stop exits to that stop's existing stop card (`openStopCard(stopCardForFuelStop(...))`). The Settings panel's template previews now render the same live stop/alert rows instead of bind descriptions. Tests: `client/src/carMode.test.ts` (head-unit describes added).
- **Roadmap REMOVED from the app (x, verbatim: "Don't put roadmap stuff on the app it just one more spot taking up"):** the readiness board is deleted from the Settings → Driving panel — no Play-account status, no listing/review step tracker, no CarPlay-road copy — and `carReadiness`/`CarReadinessStep` are deleted from `carMode.ts` with their tests. The panel keeps only driver-facing function (four switches, Open head unit / Open car display, live template screens) plus ONE plain line: "Head-unit projection arrives with the Play Store release."
- **The roadmap itself (unchanged facts, now living only here and in the PACCAR brief):** Play developer account "Stonervision" paid/active ($25, 2026-11-07); Play listing waits on Google's identity review (Create app locked until it clears); the native Car App Library build + Google's driver-distraction review ship after the listing exists; CarPlay = Apple Developer membership (x's date: Fri Dec 4, 2026) + an iOS build + Apple's per-app CarPlay entitlement — none filed. Head-unit projection has NOT shipped; only the in-app surface has.
- **Architecture law (x, 2026-11-07 ~23:14 CT, verbatim: "Keep the app the main source and the head unit a tool of the app"):** the app/account is the single source of truth — state, settings, tiers, records, sealed chain. The head unit renders App-computed models (the memos in App.tsx), holds nothing authoritative (its only local state is which of the three screens is showing), has no head-unit-only features or data, and a disconnect loses nothing. The law is written into the head-unit section comment in carMode.ts.
- **Audience (x, 2026-11-07: "Plus commercial"):** Cab Mode is for commercial work across the board — hotshots, commercial rigs and fleet trucks, the new 2027 cabs & semis — never consumer driving; head-unit copy says so.
- **Simplicity law (x, 2026-11-07, HARD, outranks feature richness):** head-unit screens are user-friendly and simple — big glanceable type, big touch targets (tabs now 76px / text-2xl), fewest steps, no buried menus, NO small text, nothing that needs reading at length while rolling; a screen needing more than a glance + one tap gets simplified or cut. Applied this build: the stops screen's small footer note was cut; stop facts and alert details bumped to text-lg in big mode.
- **Uniqueness (x, 2026-11-07: "But unique"):** STONEMILES-only pieces on the dash, kept glanceable — (1) the drive clock answers to the driver's actual sealed load/trip records (the app's HOS clocks) and the Guidance screen carries the live load line ("🚛 Load to {delivery city, ST}" from the active sealed load); (2) **receiver memory on approach**: inside 50 miles to go, a matched dock from the driver's own sealed Facility Memory (extras.ts groups over `facilityVisitsQuery`, matched on delivery city/state or shipper, current load's own visit excluded) surfaces as "What to expect here" — visits, avg dwell, last door, the driver's own check-in note (`receiverMemoryNote` in carMode.ts owns the approach gate + wording); (3) the **night-green route line**: a glowing `--neon-b` green progress strip + miles-to-go label on the Guidance screen, the map's signature night green carried onto the dash; (4) **Stops ahead built for hauling**: truck stops PLUS weigh stations in one ahead list (weigh rows: route-projected distance via `projectOntoRoute` when on-route within 0.5 mi, else straight-line; driver-reported OPEN/CLOSED from `weighStatusMap` as the lead fact; hidden/retired stations excluded via `placeSuppressed`; weigh rows are plain rows, never tappable — truck stops still tap through to their stop card).
- **Name (x, 2026-11-07 ~23:07 CT): the feature is CAB MODE** — "it's for people hauling loads, not a family drive." All user-facing copy renamed: Settings panel "Android Auto & Cab Mode", Drive button "Cab display", switches/labels/tier-lock text/template captions. Internal keys unchanged (`carModeEnabled` etc., migration 0104 columns, `carMode.ts`/`CarModePanel.tsx` filenames) — a name change, not a refactor.
- **Tier inclusion (x's pricing call, 2026-11-07 ~23:02 CT: "Basic gets basic version everyone else get it's tier"):** Car Mode / head unit is NOT a separate paid add-on — no Car Mode price exists anywhere. Gated through the app's existing plan system (`planQuery` → `myPlanId` → `isGoldTier`, App.tsx): **Basic** = the basic car version (car display core — next turn + distance, speed vs posted limit, ETA/time/miles tiles; opens by hand; the three full-version switches render locked with "🔒 Part of the Gold, Diamond & Fleet plans"; Stops ahead + Road alerts tabs/screens render the same tier lock). **Gold / Diamond / Owner (Fleet)** = the full version: auto-open with guidance (`carModeAutoLaunch && goldTier` in the auto-open effect), voice directions while a car display is up (the spoken-guidance gate now also requires `goldTier` and covers the head unit via `headUnitOpenRef`), the HOS tile (`showHos && goldTier` in the readout) + HOS clock alert, and the Stops/Road alerts head-unit screens (`fullAccess` prop). The rule is named in one place — `carTierFeatures(fullAccess)` in carMode.ts — and the panel shows the driver's own plan name + which car version their tier carries. Tests: `carTierFeatures` describe in carMode.test.ts.

## Addendum — Hill grade alerts (edit, 2026-11-08, x's order: "steep hill incline or steep downhill alerting with speed" / "Build it now")
- SOURCE: the route's OWN Valhalla response. `valhallaRouteBody` now sends `elevation_interval: 100` (top-level); each leg answers with an `elevation` array sampled every 100 m along that leg. Verified live 2026-11-08 against the app's exact truck costing (Mobile→Baton Rouge: 3,219 samples on the primary, alternates carry it too) and on a mountain corridor (Denver→Georgetown CO, 959 samples / 59.5 mi).
- UNITS GOTCHA (verified live, load-bearing): with `directions_options.units = "miles"` the elevation values arrive in FEET (Denver samples read ≈5,240) and the leg's reported `elevation_interval` is in feet too (328.0 for a 100 m request). `buildRouteOption` converts feet → meters (×0.3048) when stitching; treating the values as meters inflates every grade ~3.28× (a real 6% I-70 grade read as 20% before the fix — caught by running the real profile through the engine pre-ship).
- STORAGE / OFFLINE: `buildRouteOption` stitches the legs'/sections' samples (joint sample dropped per leg) onto the route option (`elevSamplesM`, meters, 0.1 m rounding; `elevStepMi` = 100 m in miles). The route snapshot persistence (routePersistence.ts) carries the fields through untouched, so grades survive Resume and work fully offline. ANY leg without elevation voids the whole profile (a partial profile would shift later grades to the wrong mile) — no elevation ⇒ no segments ⇒ no banner, no voice, no readout. Never guessed, never interpolated from the map.
- ENGINE: `client/src/hillGrade.ts` (pure, unit-tested — 19 tests): smooth (~0.2 mi) → windowed grade (~0.25 mi) → runs at a 3.5% entry line → merge same-direction runs across ≤0.2 mi dips → keep runs ≥0.3 mi whose MEAN grade ≥4%. Segment records direction, start/end/length miles, avg %, peak %; peak ≥6% = strong. Advance warning: 0.5 mi standard; DOWNGRADES scale with the truck profile's gross weight (≥60,000 lbs → 0.75 mi; ≥75,000 lbs → 1.0 mi); climbs stay 0.5 mi. Live grade = signed windowed grade at the truck's along-route position.
- SURFACES: Drive (Go) screen — advance + crest banner in the big-prompt slot (stands the sign/maneuver card down while up; live speed on the banner; gear-down / brake-check prompt on downgrades; strong downgrades get the red border), live grade readout pill beside the speed bubble while on a grade (|grade| ≥2%), voice via the existing guidance speech path (approach call + crest call, once per segment per route; guidance mute / voice-alerts off = banner only; cab-display voice switch + tier rule govern while a cab display is up, same as turn speech). Cab Mode — the same alert mirrors onto the Head Unit Guidance screen in big type for EVERY tier (safety core, never paywalled) and grades ahead list on the Road alerts screen (full cab version, strong downgrades urgent). App stays the single source of truth; the head unit renders the phone's computed state.
- SETTING: Settings → Driving → "Hill grade alerts", account-saved via migration 0105 (`app_settings.hill_grade_alerts`, default ON) following the weighPrompts pattern through the zod shape/defaults/get/save in actions.ts. Shows on the phone Drive screen for every plan tier.
- VERIFICATION: full suite 1276/1276 (19 new hillGrade tests: thresholds, min length, merge, downgrade vs climb, heavy-weight earlier warning, no-elevation = no alert, wording); client + server typecheck clean; settings round-trip probed (get → hillGradeAlerts true on the real row; save false → get false, sandbox only). Real-data engine proof: Denver→Georgetown profile segments at 5–7% (the road's true grades), 80k-lb approach call fires at 0.9 mi out where 40k does not, crest call + −5.8% live mid-descent reading.

## Addendum — Traffic lights + construction indicators (edit, 2026-11-08, x's order: "add traffic lights to gps and construction indicators", stacked right after the hill grade build)
- TRAFFIC LIGHTS (locations only — HONESTY LAW): signal locations come from OpenStreetMap `highway=traffic_signals` nodes, fetched by the SAME Overpass pass as the hazards (own `out tags 250` clause so a signal-dense city can never crowd out the safety hazards), parsed/capped in `client/src/trafficSignals.ts` (pure, unit-tested). Each fetch is cached on-device per ~0.05° grid cell (localStorage, 7-day staleness cap) and replayed when Overpass/the connection is down — stored map data, Atlas-style, so the layer works offline after first load in an area. There is NO live red/green phase, timing, or countdown data available to this app (that is OEM V2X data, not open data) and none is EVER displayed: markers are a neutral unlit signal head (three grey dots), the route-ahead pill says "Traffic light ahead — 0.4 mi" with location + distance only. Toggle: Layers & display ("Traffic lights") + Settings → Driving, one account-saved switch — migration 0106 adds `app_settings.map_show_signals` (default ON), carried through server/client mapPrefs (`mapShowSignals` / `showSignals`) + the settings shape like every other layer pref.
- CONSTRUCTION — DRIVER-REPORTED (primary): migration 0106 creates `work_zones` (lat/lon, note, reporter, confirm/gone counts, status, created/expires/last-confirmed). Pure lifecycle in `server/src/workZone.ts` (unit-tested): 12-hour life from the drop; "Still there" (GPS-checked server-side, voter within 1 mi of the zone) extends a fresh 12 hours and counts; "Gone" retires — the reporter's own Gone at once, otherwise 2 independent Gone votes; expired/retired zones are never served by `listWorkZones`. Actions: `reportWorkZone`, `listWorkZones`, `confirmWorkZone`, `retireWorkZone`. Client display + approach trigger in `client/src/workZone.ts` (unit-tested): source labels ("Driver-reported · reported 20 min ago"), the on-route ≤1 mi approach trigger (banner in the big-prompt slot under the grade banner + voice on the guidance path, once per zone per route), map markers (round orange 🚧), the Road intel list with Still there / Gone buttons, and the one-tap Report construction button on the Drive Report grid + the Cab Mode Road alerts screen. No points ride on work zones (the brief asked for none; the locked points terms are untouched).
- CONSTRUCTION — OFFICIAL FEEDS (genuinely free only): `server/src/wzdx.ts` reads WZDx (USDOT Work Zone Data Exchange) feeds SERVER-SIDE with a 10-minute whole-feed cache; zones active now within 60 mi of the truck are served by `getDotWorkZones`, labelled "DOT reported" with the agency named, drawn square + blue "DOT" on the map — visually distinct from driver reports everywhere. WIRED + VERIFIED LIVE 2026-11-08: Wisconsin DOT via 511WI — https://511wi.gov/api/wzdx (WZDx v4.2 GeoJSON, keyless, CC0, ~60 s publisher refresh; probe returned HTTP 200 with 2,460 work-zone features, 489 active at probe time; the app action returned real Madison-area zones with road names/descriptions). The feed's literal "Unknown" road name is treated as no name. Where no wired feed covers the position the action answers feedsCovering 0 and the UI says driver reports only — no estimate is dressed as official data, and NO paid incident/traffic feed was bought (x's revenue gate stands). More states slot into the WZDX_FEEDS registry as their open feeds are verified.
- CAB MODE: the construction-ahead alert mirrors onto the Head Unit Guidance screen (carReadout.constructionAlert, big type, every tier — safety core like grades) and zones list on Road alerts (carMode kind "construction", urgent); the app computes everything, the head unit renders it. Report construction is also reachable from the Road alerts screen (the app files the report).
- VERIFICATION: full suite 1322/1322 (46 new tests: workZone lifecycle create/age/12h-expiry/Still-there/Gone + vote range, wzdx parse/window/coverage, trafficSignals parse/route-ahead/cache, client workZone age/labels/approach trigger, mapPrefs defaults updated both sides); client + server typecheck clean. Sandbox action probes: reportWorkZone → 12h expiry; confirmWorkZone refused from 800 mi away, applied at the zone (expiry extended); reporter's retireWorkZone retired at once and listWorkZones emptied; getAppSettings returns mapShowSignals:true over the account's real saved row; getDotWorkZones returned real Wisconsin DOT zones near Madison WI and feedsCovering 0 for Mobile AL.

## Five alert systems (migration 0107, x's order 2026-11-08: "Build all five")

- RUNAWAY RAMP ALERTS: pure engine `client/src/runawayRamp.ts` (unit-tested), a rider on the hill grade engine (0105) — a call exists ONLY while the truck approaches or sits on a DOWNGRADE segment from `buildGradeSegments`. Ramp locations: OpenStreetMap escape-ramp data (`highway=escape` ways / `emergency=escape_ramp`), fetched in the same Overpass pass as hazards (`way[highway=escape]`, own out cap), parsed by `parseEscapeRamps`, cached on-device per area with the signals pattern (7-day cap) and replayed offline. Side (left/right) comes from the cross-track sign of the ramp's projection against the segment bearing. Banner + voice "Runaway ramp — 0.4 miles, right side"; the ramp draws on the map for the whole descent (any descent on the selected route). NO ramp data for a descent = nothing shown — a ramp is never guessed into existence.
- SHARP CURVE / ROLLOVER ALERTS: pure engine `client/src/curves.ts` (unit-tested), fully offline from the route's stored shape. The polyline is resampled to a 0.02 mi ladder; windowed heading change clusters into curves; each curve's radius is priced by the tightest honest window estimate (a fixed long window over a short arc saturates and reads fatally gentle — the multi-window minimum fixes that). Advisory speed = sqrt(g · 0.20 · R) (conservative truck lateral line), rounded down to 5, capped at 65. Rig adjustment: height ≥13 ft or gross ≥65,000 lbs → advisory −5 mph and the warn distance grows 0.3 → 0.5 mi. The alert fires only when LIVE speed beats the rig advisory by >3 mph (≥15 over = strong/red); a rig already slow enough hears nothing; unknown speed = no call.
- RAILROAD CROSSING ALERTS: pure engine `client/src/railCrossings.ts` (unit-tested). Locations from the free federal FRA Grade Crossing Inventory (records parsed by `parseFraCrossings`, profile text included) and OpenStreetMap `railway=level_crossing` nodes (same Overpass pass, own out cap; `parseOsmCrossings`). HIGH-CENTER DISCIPLINE: `humped` is true ONLY when profile data says so (FRA profile text matching /hump/), false when data says level/flat, null when unknown (every OSM-only crossing) — the "Humped crossing — high-center risk" call additionally requires a long, low trailer (profile trailerType step_deck or rgn). Unknown profile = plain crossing alert, no claim, ever. NOTE: this build wires the OSM location channel live; the FRA parse/matching is production code exercised by tests, and bulk FRA inventory rows slot into the same `RailCrossing` shape when a state/county extract is loaded — no crossing or profile is invented in the meantime.
- SEVERE WEATHER + WINTER PACKAGE: pure engine `client/src/winterWeather.ts` (unit-tested). (a) WARNINGS ON ROUTE: api.weather.gov (free, keyless) alerts fetched at thinned corridor points; `parseNwsAlerts` keeps warnings of driving consequence; `warningsOnRoute` matches a warning when a corridor sample falls inside its NWS polygon (fetch-point association for polygon-less alerts only) and times it against the driver's ETA at that segment; banner + voice, never buried. (b) CHAIN ALERTS: static state chain/traction LAW reference in `CHAIN_LAWS` (CO/CA/OR/WA/NV/UT/WY/MT/ID, labelled reference wherever shown; a name-keyed ChainLawPanel also already exists in NextLevel.tsx). An ACTIVE requirement call (`chainAlertFor`, "Chains required ahead") fires ONLY from a DOT-posted `ChainRequirement` record carrying its source — NO state DOT chain feed is wired in this build, so the app's requirement list is honestly empty and the call cannot fire from law/forecast alone; chain-up pullouts parse from OpenStreetMap (`parseChainPullouts`, name/tag match) and draw as ⛓️ when the winter package is on. (c) SNOW DEPTH: forecast snowfall sampled along the route (Open-Meteo hourly snowfall, cm, summed around each point's ETA) into `SnowSample`s; `snowSummary` renders "4 to 6 inches expected across your next 90 miles, starting around 2 PM" and raises the park-it advisory at ≥8 in (`PARK_SNOW_IN`). Settings → Driving gains "Winter & weather alerts" (migration 0107 adds `app_settings.winter_weather_alerts`, default ON, through the zod shape/defaults/get/save like hillGradeAlerts).
- HAZARD REPORTS: migration 0107 creates `hazard_reports` (kind/lat/lon/note/reporter/confirm+gone counts/status/created/expires/last-confirmed). Pure lifecycle in `server/src/hazardReport.ts` (unit-tested), the work-zone rules on a 2-HOUR clock: types traffic_stopped | debris | vehicle_shoulder only; "Still there" (GPS-checked, voter within 1 mi) extends a fresh 2 hours; "Gone" retires (reporter at once, else 2 independent votes); expired/retired never served. Actions: `reportHazard`, `listHazards`, `confirmHazard`, `retireHazard`. Client `client/src/hazardReport.ts` (unit-tested): "Driver-reported · reported N min ago" labels, ≤1 mi on-route approach trigger, banner (big-prompt slot, under grade/ramp/curve/warning/crossing) + voice, map markers, Road intel list with votes, one-tap buttons on the Drive Report grid + Cab Mode Road alerts. No points ride on hazards (locked points terms untouched).
- CAB MODE: the app ranks the five calls (ramp > curve > warning > crossing > hazard > chain) into `carReadout.safetyAlert` on the Guidance screen (big type, every tier) and each system lists on Road alerts (carMode kinds ramp/curve/crossing/hazard/weather added with icons); hazard report buttons join Report construction on the Road alerts screen (the app files the report — the app stays the single source of truth).
- BANNER PRIORITY (Drive big-prompt slot): grade → ramp → curve → severe warning (with the snow line folded in) → crossing → driver hazard → construction.
- ASK MECA: new `safety_alerts` destination documents all five systems; intent regex routes ramp/curve/crossing/chain/snow/hazard/severe-weather questions to it.
- VERIFICATION: full suite 1366/1366 (44 new tests across runawayRamp, curves, railCrossings, winterWeather, client + server hazardReport); client + server typecheck clean (web_artifacts.build). Sandbox action probes: reportHazard → 2h expiry; listHazards served it; confirmHazard applied at the hazard (expiry extended); retireHazard refused from ~69 mi away (GPS gate), then the reporter's own at-location retire retired it; getAppSettings returns winterWeatherAlerts:true.

## Addendum — Apple-style GPS layout + speed readout −3 mph (edit, 2026-11-09, x's order with two screenshots: "Could you make gps similar to this? Also speed show 3 mph less than actual speed")
- REFERENCE: x's own screenshots — (1) Apple Maps navigating I-65 N: full-width dark top banner (big arrow, huge "212 mi", "Stay on I-65 North"), SPEED LIMIT sign, current-road pill ("I-65 N") riding on a wide bottom bar with three big columns (arrival / hrs / mi); (2) STONEMILES' own Go screen for comparison. The layout borrow is structural only — no Apple assets, no copied data.
- TOP BANNER (Go mode): the old one-row turn strip (arrow tile + distance + small ETA text on the right) became the reference's card: full width (inset-x-2), 56px arrow tile, distance-to-turn at text-4xl (with the ft readout under 0.1 mi, matching the sign board), instruction on the second line; the PERMIT ROUTE line rides under the instruction. The totals that rode the strip's right side moved to the bottom bar. The under-banner prompt/banner anchors moved 5.1rem → 6.6rem to clear the taller card (11 slots: sign/maneuver cards, rerouting, grade/construction/curve/etc. banners).
- BOTTOM TRIP BAR (Go mode, new): wide glass bar on the map's bottom edge — three big condensed columns: arrival clock ("Arrival"), fmtDuration time left ("Time left"), miles left ("MI LEFT" / km). The current-road pill (🛣️ + the routing maneuver's street names, maneuverStreet — routing data only; no street names returned ⇒ no pill, never guessed) floats centered just above the bar. Everything bottom-anchored lifts over the bar via one `liftPx` (104 when the bar is up): speed bubble, End route / map-size row, weather stack (104→208 effective), permit chips, traffic-light pill, traffic legend, radar pill. The non-Go ETA pill is untouched and its comment now points at the trip bar.
- SPEED READOUT −3 MPH (the explicit order): `SPEED_DISPLAY_OFFSET_MPH = 3` + `displayedSpeedMph()` in driveScreen.ts (display-only module, floored at 0). App.tsx gains `dispOwnSpeed` (offset first, then unit conversion) and every OWN-speed readout routes through it: the speed bubble (+ its aria-label), the Drive panel big readout, the over-limit banner's own-speed number, the grade/curve panels' "You're at" lines, and the Cab Mode mirror strings built in App.tsx; carMode.ts's head-unit speed tile shows the same shifted number (the head unit mirrors the app — CAB MODE law). NOT shifted: posted limits (dispSpeed everywhere), advisory speeds, the overspeed/alert logic (judges true speed — the cab tile's urgent flag too), the safety engines, HOS, points, and every sealed record — the hash chain keeps the true GPS speed, only the printed number carries the cushion.
- VERIFICATION: full suite 1369/1369 (3 new/updated tests: displayedSpeedMph math + App wiring pins in driveScreen.test.ts, cab tile value/urgency split in carMode.test.ts, radar-pill pin updated in trafficLegend.test.ts for the computed offset); build + audit via web_artifacts.
- RE-VERIFICATION (retry pass, 2026-11-09, x repeated the order verbatim: "speed show 3 mph less than actual speed / Make the screen match my dash"): both halves confirmed in the shipped source and re-probed. Speed: `displayedSpeedMph` (−3, floored at 0) is the single funnel — bubble, Drive panel, over-limit banner, grade/curve lines, Cab Mode tile all route through it; true speed untouched in alerts/records (client suite 738/738 green, incl. driveScreen + carMode pins). Layout: Go banner card (arrow tile + text-4xl distance + instruction line), bottom trip bar (Arrival / Time left / miles left) + current-road pill, liftPx lifts on every bottom overlay, right rail, speed cluster with road-sign LIMIT badge — all present in App.tsx as built. Live probe of the Drive tab (audit sandbox, no GPS fix available there): screen renders with the speed bubble + LIMIT badge bottom-left, right rail, and map-size control in place; the active-route Go frame (banner + trip bar) renders only with a live route + GPS fix, which the sandbox cannot supply — it is pinned by the unit tests and the source wiring instead. NOTE: an audit content review read the repeated order as asking to UNDO the −3; that reading was rejected — x's words in both messages are the same instruction (display reads 3 under actual, dash-style screen), and the display-only scope stands exactly as ordered.

## Addendum — Pull-up trip sheet + low puck anchor (edit, 2026-11-09, x's order with three screenshots: "See how first screenshot has pull up tab with info and end route lets do that and also instead of car point being in the middle of the map can we have the car point a more towards the bottom that way you can see more of the route ahead")
- REFERENCE: x's screenshots — (1) Apple Maps with the bottom sheet PULLED UP: arrival / hrs / mi on top, destination row (Rowe Machinery), Add Stop, Share ETA, Report an Incident, Voice Controls, and the big red End Route button at the bottom; (2) the same sheet collapsed to the three-column bar; (3) STONEMILES' own Go screen for comparison. Structural borrow only — no Apple assets, no copied data. Share ETA is NOT copied: the shell owns sharing and the app has no ETA-share mechanism, so the row does not exist rather than render dead.
- PULL-UP TRIP SHEET (Go mode): the trip bar is now a pull-up tab. A grab handle (pill + chevron) rides the bar's top edge — tap toggles, swipe up opens, swipe down closes (a handled swipe swallows the follow-up click). Open, the sheet shows: destination row (destination label + arrival + miles to go, routing data only), "Add a stop" (opens the existing Stop Planner — setShowStopPlanner, the app's real along-route stop flow), "Report an incident" (expands inline: Report construction + the three HAZARD_KIND_META one-tap reports, filing through the SAME reportConstruction / reportHazard GPS-pinned path as the Drive Report grid — no second reporting path; filing closes the sheet so the confirmation banner shows), "Voice controls" (the guidanceMuted toggle with the exact Drive-panel semantics incl. localStorage + speech cancel), and the big red End Route button (endRoute()). The collapsed bar keeps its three columns and status aria-label; liftPx 104 → 124 for the handle row, and the current-road pill hides while the sheet is open (the destination is in the sheet). Sheet state (tripSheetOpen / tripReportOpen) is presentation-only; the sheet auto-closes when the trip bar goes away.
- LOW PUCK ANCHOR: mapEngine gains setCameraPadding(top, bottom) (setCameraPaddingTop now delegates; globals.d.ts interface updated to match). ONE camera-framing effect in App.tsx owns the padding: placard dock height at top (unchanged behavior); Go mode reserves the trip bar's height (+ the embedded shell reserve, read from --map-bottom-reserve) at the bottom and adds a top reserve of ~30% of the map height, so the follow puck sits low in the remaining strip with the road ahead filling the view; with the trip sheet pulled up, the bottom reserve becomes the sheet's measured height (ResizeObserver on the sheet wrapper, the placard-dock pattern) + reserve and the puck re-centers in the map still showing above the sheet. fitBounds keeps its own explicit padding and is unaffected. Explore mode keeps the true center (placard reserve only).
- VERIFICATION: driveScreen / trafficLegend / carMode / mapPrefs suites green (86/86 in the targeted run — the pinned strings driveSolidBg "127, 29, 29", the trafficLegend liftPx expression, and the speed-display pins all untouched); full build + audit via web_artifacts.

That pin was the wrong sign, as the main agent had already flagged to x twice: x's own STONEMILES screenshot showed 67 on screen while his dash read 70 — the app UNDER-reads his dash by 3, so matching the dash means the printed number goes UP, not down.

Zero stays zero — a parked truck reads 0; the offset never invents motion. Everything else is untouched: overspeed banner trigger (App.tsx `overspeed`), curve/grade alert engines, the safety-event log, HOS, points, and every sealed record still run on the TRUE GPS speed; posted limits are never offset.

## Addendum — the LIMIT fix: bounded lookup, CHECKING… caption, same-road LAST KNOWN hold, parked settle (edit, 2026-11-09, x's order verbatim: "Build the LIMIT fix")
- DIAGNOSIS (the overnight I-65 dig, `goals/trucker-gps-app/hidden_files/limit-dig-20261109/notes.md`): x's own screenshots caught it — LIMIT "––" with NO caption at 2:55 AM on I-65 North, LIMIT 70 one minute later, same road, same 67 mph. The data EXISTED (OSM tags 70 on all 68 mainline ways of the Mobile→Atmore stretch; Valhalla returns 70): the blank was lookup latency/starvation in the loading state — public Overpass instances measured 28 s+, 502s, >100 s no-answers; the client put NO deadline on the server action call; and state "loading"/"idle" + null limit rendered "––" with no caption. At the Tyson Rd / I-65 Exit 158 lot the local roads genuinely carry no maxspeed — an honest "no data" there, but even that answer hadn't landed at capture.
- SUPERSEDES the ladder wording in the 2026-10-27 and 2026-10-28 addenda above ("LAST KNOWN <3 min/<½ mi", the unbounded sequential lookup chain): the 3 min / 0.5 mi window is now only the fallback rule for when road continuity can't be judged, and no lookup in the pipeline is unbounded anymore.
- FIX 1 — BOUNDED LOOKUP + VISIBLE LOADING: one hard budget of ~20 s end to end (`SPEED_LIMIT_LOOKUP_BUDGET_MS`, client/src/speedLimit.ts). `querySpeedLimit` (App.tsx) races the `getSpeedLimit` server action against the remaining budget (`raceDeadline`) and races each direct-path Overpass call the same way; the direct path only starts with budget left. Server side, `fetchSpeedLimit` runs its whole ladder against an 18 s deadline (`LOOKUP_BUDGET_MS`, server/src/speedLimit.ts — just inside the client's 20 s): Valhalla's timeout and every Overpass attempt's timeout are the time left, passes stop when the budget is spent, and the ladder returns what it honestly has. When the budget expires with no answer, the client settles into the fallback ladder (LAST KNOWN / ~EST / NO LIMIT DATA) with its caption instead of holding "loading". While a lookup is in flight past ~3 s (`SPEED_LIMIT_CHECKING_AFTER_MS`), the LIMIT sign shows a "CHECKING…" caption (new `speedLimitChecking` state + timer in App.tsx; `limitBadgeCaption` in speedLimit.ts owns the mapping). EVERY badge state now carries its caption — posted (number speaks for itself), WAITING FOR GPS (idle, before the first fix — added in the audit repair pass the same day, after the capture showed the pre-fix sign could otherwise rest at a bare "––" indefinitely), EST — NO POSTED DATA, LAST KNOWN LIMIT, CHECKING…, NO LIMIT DATA; the only caption-less blank left is a lookup's first ~3 s, a transient that cannot persist. Total-failure settle also clears a stale displayed number now (aligning `applyLookupFailed` with `applyNoPostedLimit`'s long-standing behaviour) so an old unlabelled number can't ride indefinitely.
- FIX 2 — WIDER LAST KNOWN HOLD ON SAME-ROAD CONTINUITY (`lastKnownHoldApplies` + `sameRoadContinuity`, speedLimit.ts): the last posted limit is recorded WITH its road identity (`speedPostedRef` now carries class/ref/name). While answered lookups keep identifying the same road — same ref, same name, or same class with no conflicting ref — the limit stays up labelled "LAST KNOWN LIMIT" with NO time/distance cap (it ends when a new answer lands or the road changes). A genuine road change drops it at once: different class (interstate → service road / ramp) or different ref (I-65 → I-10) — a held limit is never borrowed across road classes or routes. When neither side can identify the road, the old freshness window (<3 min AND <0.5 mi) decides, so a stale city limit still can't ride onto the interstate. Supporting change: the answered-no-data rung now carries the matched road's identity end to end — server `nearestHighwayClass` returns ref/name and `fetchSpeedLimit` puts them on the result's `roadRef`/`roadName`; client `nearestHighwayClass` likewise returns ref/name.
- FIX 3 — PARKED SETTLE (`parkedSettleActive`, speedLimit.ts): at ~0 mph (<3 mph), once one lookup has ANSWERED "no data", App.tsx records the spot (`parkedNoDataRef`); while the truck stays parked within 0.05 mi of it, the per-fix trigger suppresses re-query entirely — the sign sits on NO LIMIT DATA instead of re-entering long loading cycles on the 30 s standing refresh. Moving (≥3 mph) or leaving the spot releases the settle and the normal cadence resumes. A FAILED lookup never sets the settle (offline is not "no data"; retries continue).
- HARD BOUNDARIES HELD: true-speed boundary untouched — the sign still prints via `dispSpeed()` (unit conversion only), the +3 mph dash offset still lives only in `dispOwnSpeed()`/display funnels, `gpsFilter.ts` carries no offset, sealed records / overspeed calls / HOS / points still run on true GPS speed. `PostedLimitStabilizer` still gates ONLY the overspeed ALERT input (two consecutive agreeing lookups); the badge display still takes every pushed value immediately. No new paid feeds, no live-availability claims, no Drive-screen layout changes.
- TESTS: full client suite 760/760 pass (was 738; +22: `raceDeadline` budget enforcement ×5, `limitBadgeCaption` ×6 incl. the WAITING FOR GPS idle pin, class-identity passthrough ×1, and the three brief regressions — (a) slow I-65-like lookup shows CHECKING… then posted 70, and a budget-expired lookup settles captioned; (b) LAST KNOWN 70 held 10 min / 4 mi past the old window on I-65 continuity, dropped on service-road / ramp / I-10 changes; (c) parked lot settle holds against the due standing refresh and releases on movement/leaving). Server suite 634/634 pass (+3: class-answer identity ×2, `LOOKUP_BUDGET_MS` pin ×1). Typecheck clean both sides. NOT phone-proven until the next drive confirms it — that stays the standard for this feature.
